- **Fix round** (2026-10-19 23:51 to 2026-10-20 01:05 (-04:00), one agent; the review found ten issues, one major and nine minor, all valid, all fixed; commits `8e48d00`, `7e58ee9`, `73d2775`, `56fa859`, `fc84cb5`, and this note).
  1. **(major) `gate_run.py server` left the API key store on the owner's machine.** The five state variables are not all of a server's state: `citar/keystore.py` keeps keys in `%APPDATA%\citar\keys.enc` (the XDG config base on Linux) and by default in the OS credential store, so a key saved on the Servers page through a gate (5-05's bundle QA and on) would have reached the owner's real file or Windows Credential Manager. The review reproduced it: the old harness gave a probe `C:\Users\jimmi\AppData\Roaming\citar\keys.enc` and `Windows Credential Manager`; rerun through the new one, the same probe reports the file under `--state` and no credential store (gate 1 below). Now the server runs with `CITAR_KEYS_FILE=<state>/config/keys.enc`, `PYTHON_KEYRING_BACKEND=keyring.backends.fail.Keyring` (so `keystore._keyring()` is None and the Servers page offers the file and the environment only), and the per-user bases (`APPDATA` and `LOCALAPPDATA` on Windows, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` on Linux; macOS's are not variables, and the key file covers it) under `<state>/user`. A `--env` may not name `PYTHON_KEYRING_BACKEND` or a per-user base; its `CITAR_KEYS_FILE` must be inside `--under`, and its `CITAR_DB_URL` a SQLite database inside `--under` or in memory (anything else, PostgreSQL on loopback included, is refused: a gate's server keeps its own database). `test_gate_run` runs `citar.keystore` under the environment the harness gives and asserts the file under `--state` and no credential store; the docstring and CONTRIBUTING name the key store.
  2. **The xtask the signing job will run with the key was linked from a cache.** Every cache in package.yml (the wheels', the helpers' and `helper-manifest`'s rust-cache, and the container cache of item 9) is now restored only in a run of its own (`needs.meta.outputs.mode == 'ci'`, a conjunct of each `if:`), so what a release or a dry run ships, and the xtask 5-09's signing job runs, is compiled from the locked sources alone (P5.4.2: the key never meets a build). The wheels' cache is 5-02b's, changed for the same reason (their binaries ship too). `test_no_cache_reaches_a_release_build` holds all four.
  3. **The container builds piped `sh.rustup.rs` into a shell as root.** They now fetch `rustup-init` 1.29.1 (`RUSTUP_INIT_VERSION`, released 2026-09-01) from `static.rust-lang.org/rustup/archive/<version>/<target>/rustup-init` and run it only once `sha256sum -c` has matched the SHA-256 its matrix leg names (`rustup_sha256`: `dda72343...` for x86_64, `15f6e4ce...` for aarch64, each the archive's own `.sha256`, and each checked against the downloaded file in WSL), with `--no-modify-path --profile minimal --default-toolchain none`, then `rustup toolchain install` as before. The version and the hashes are held equal across package.yml and helper.yml (`test_workflows`, `helper.test.mjs`), and no workflow names `sh.rustup.rs`.
  4. **helper.yml's checkouts kept the token** in `.git/config` of a workspace the artifact job mounts into a third-party image. Every helper.yml checkout now sets `persist-credentials: false` (nothing there needs the token); the test holds them.
  5. **A helper run could write outside `--under`.** `gate_run.py helper` now holds every file a run names inside `--under`: `--config`, `--hardware` and `--ca-file` (their abbreviations refused too), the FILE `scan` writes, and, for `update apply` and `update rollback`, the BINARY itself, which they replace (keeping the old one beside it). The review's `scan C:/Users/jimmi/x.json` wrote that file; now such a run is refused and nothing is written (gate 1 below).
  6. **The Unix process table read no start time** (`ps -o pid=,ppid=` has two columns), so the reused-id protection held on Windows only. Linux now reads `/proc/<pid>/stat`'s fields 4 and 22 (counted after the command's last parenthesis) and macOS `ps -o lstart=` in the C locale; `TheProcessTable` asserts this process's start is read on every OS, that the same id with another start is not counted as left, and that a child's start is its parent's or later.
  7. **The verifiers' limits were not held to citar-jobs'.** `TheLimitsAreCitarJobs` reads `crates/citar-jobs/src/update.rs` and `xtask/src/manifest.rs` as text and holds verify_manifest.py's `MANIFEST_DOMAIN`, `MANIFEST_MAX_BYTES`, `MANIFEST_VERSION` (new, used where `1` was written) and `MAX_KEYS`, and xtask's domain and size, to citar-jobs'. The test is in the Python suite, not in citar-jobs: the jobs crate's content code hashes its `src/`, so a test there would change the jobs build.
  8. **`--check-keys` accepted a test key.** It now refuses the public half of any seed of 32 equal bytes (every Rust and Python test signs with `[n; 32]`) and any key in a `.pub` file under `tests/data/` (the committed fixture's), naming where it comes from and never the line; `test_helper_release` plants the fixture's key, two seeds' and one beside a fresh key.
  9. **The container builds were cold on every pull request.** Their cargo home, rustup and target now live under `target/container*` (`CARGO_HOME`, `RUSTUP_HOME`, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` set in the container), kept by `actions/cache` (v6.1.0, pinned to `55cc8345...` by `action_pins.py pin`) under `helper-container-<image>-rustup-<version>-<hash of Cargo.lock and rust-toolchain.toml>` in both workflows, so package.yml's and helper.yml's Linux legs share one entry; mode ci only in package.yml (item 2); a restored cargo home skips the rustup download; the step gives `target` back to the runner's user with the builds.
  10. **Nothing checked the macOS deployment target.** `helper_release.py macos --min V BINARY...` reads each binary's oldest macOS from `otool -l` (`LC_BUILD_VERSION`'s `minos`, or an older linker's `LC_VERSION_MIN_MACOSX`) and fails unless it is V; package.yml's `check` passes `--macos-min "$MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET"` (required for a Mac platform, recorded in the report as `macos_min`) and helper.yml's Mac artifact legs run `macos`.

## P5.5 Third-party notices

- **`cargo xtask licenses [--check]`** (5-04) writes `packaging/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` from `cargo metadata --locked`: the normal-dependency closure of `citar-py` and `citar-helper` over every release target (Windows-only and Unix-only crates included), each crate's name, version and licence expression, and the text of its licence and NOTICE files from the registry's sources, in name order. A licence outside the allow-list (MIT, Apache-2.0 and its LLVM exception, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, ISC, Zlib, Unicode-3.0, Unicode-DFS-2016, MPL-2.0, CC0-1.0, BSL-1.0, 0BSD, CDLA-Permissive-2.0) is refused by name. `--check` regenerates and compares; rust.yml's lint job runs it after `cargo fetch --locked`, so a lock change without the file fails.
- **Where it travels:** the wheels (`license-files`, so pip installs it in the dist-info and the image carries it), the bundle, every packaged helper archive, and the release as an asset. The Windows bundle also gets `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` for what it ships of Python: `installer/python_licenses.py` (5-05) walks the installed dependency closure of `citar[all]` through `importlib.metadata` (so PyInstaller, pillow and the build tools, which are not shipped, are left out), writes each distribution's name, version, licence and licence files, and adds CPython's own `LICENSE.txt` (from `sys.base_prefix`) and PyInstaller's bootloader licence (P5.6). (5-05's fix round: also the run-time hooks' licences, PyInstaller's and its community hooks', which the executables carry; CITAR's verbatim copy, `installer/licenses/`, of the licence of a shipped package whose wheel has none; and a bundle that carries no file outside the closure, P5.6.)
- **`NOTICE.md`**'s "Third-party dependencies" says all this.

**As built in 5-04: third-party notices** (Appendix F, 5-04). One agent, from 2026-10-20 03:21 to 05:05 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-04` from `8ca6890`; its fix round from 05:25 (below, "Fix round").

- **What was built.**
  - **`cargo xtask licenses [--check] [--metadata FILE] [--out FILE]`** (`xtask/src/licenses.rs`): `cargo metadata --locked --format-version 1` with the release builds' features (`citar-helper/packaged`, and pyproject's maturin features for `citar-py`, none today), or the copy `--metadata` names. For each of the five release targets (xtask's `manifest::TARGETS`, which a test holds equal to package.yml's wheel and helper matrices), the target's configuration from `rustc -O --print cfg --target T` (the pinned toolchain's own definitions; rustc prints any built-in target's without its standard library) and the normal-dependency closure of `citar-py` and `citar-helper`: an edge counts when one of its kinds is normal and its condition (a `cfg(...)`, read by a small parser for `all`, `any`, `not`, names and `name = "value"`, or a target triple) holds there. CITAR's own crates are walked through, never listed. **180 crates** at this head: 169 on Windows, 160 and 162 on the Macs, 158 and 156 on Linux x64 and arm64 (151 on all five, ring, rustls, tokio, reqwest and PyO3 among them; 29 on some only: the windows crates on Windows, security-framework and the objc2 crates on the Macs, openssl-probe on Linux); `wasm-bindgen`, `js-sys`, `jni`, `r-efi` and the `*-pc-windows-gnu` crates are out.
  - **The file** (`packaging/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`, 530,854 bytes after the fix round): a header (what links what, how the list is made, that it errs on the side of more, `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` for the bundle's Python), an index of the 180 (`name version: expression`), then one section a crate in name order (semver within a name): `Licence:`, `Source:` (its crates.io page), `Repository:`, `Targets:` when not all five, `Files:`, then each licence or notice file of its package, at its top or below it (a file whose name starts `license`, `licence`, `copying`, `copyright`, `notice` or `unlicense`, and every file of a folder named `license`, `licenses`, `licence` or `licences`, wherever it is; never source code, C's included; nothing in a `tests`, `benches`, `examples` or `.github` folder at any depth; and the manifest's `license-file`), each text under `---- name version: PATH ----`, its path from the package's top (`ring 0.17.14: third_party/fiat/LICENSE`). A text byte for byte one printed earlier is named, not printed again (`---- anyhow ...: LICENSE-APACHE: the same text as ... ----`): 333 texts, 132 distinct, so the file is 0.53 MB where printing each would make it about 1.7 MB. Texts are read with LF line endings, no byte-order mark and no blank lines before their first; no path, time or CITAR version is written, so Windows and Linux write the same bytes and a release's version bump leaves the file alone. `.gitattributes` keeps it LF (and `linguist-generated`).
  - **The allow-list** is P5.5's, `ALLOWED` and `ALLOWED_EXCEPTIONS` (Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception). An SPDX expression is read (`OR`, `AND`, `WITH`, parentheses, the old `/` as `OR`, `+`) and must be satisfiable with allowed licences alone; otherwise every crate is refused at once by name, version and the licences it needs (exit 1), before any file is written. A crate with no expression, or one that does not read, is refused too.
  - **A package with no licence file anywhere in it** (two today: `objc2-core-foundation` and `objc2-io-kit`, `Zlib OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT`, macOS only) gets the standard text of a licence its expression offers alone, from `STANDARD_TEXTS`: only Apache-2.0's, `xtask/licenses/Apache-2.0.txt`, apache.org's `LICENSE-2.0.txt` byte for byte (a test holds its SHA-256, `cfc7749b...`), copied from a crate in the registry that ships it unchanged. Only a text that names no copyright holder is held, so nothing is written in a crate's name that it did not write. A crate whose expression no held text meets is refused, naming what to add.
  - **`--check`** regenerates in memory and compares bytes; out of date, it names the crates added and removed (the index lines), or the first line that differs (exit 1). rust.yml's lint job runs `cargo fetch --locked` then `cargo xtask licenses --check`, and both events' path filters take the file (`cargo xtask check`'s triggers rule now counts it among the files rust.yml must run on, and its test plants the lines away).
  - **package.yml's `notices` job** (needs `meta`; no cache, no environment): prepare, the toolchain, `cargo fetch --locked` and `cargo xtask licenses --check`, then the file uploaded as **`pkg-third-party-licenses`**, the release's `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` asset (`release.py assets`' notices group). release.yml calls no rust.yml, so a tag's or a dry run's own run proves what it ships.
  - **The wheels:** `pyproject.toml`'s `license-files` gains `packaging/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`; maturin puts it at `<dist-info>/licenses/packaging/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` (PEP 639 keeps the path) and `License-File: packaging/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` in METADATA, and the sdist at its path. `check_dist.py` reads the fields through `licence_fields()`, which also refuses a glob in `license-files` (the check takes the entries as paths). **The packaged helpers:** `helper_release.py`'s `ARCHIVE_TEXTS` gains `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` from `packaging/`, so each archive holds four files.
  - **Docs:** NOTICE.md's "Third-party dependencies" (the crates, the file, where it travels, the allow-list; the Python packages and the bundle's `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt`; nothing vendored); CONTRIBUTING's Rust section ("Third-party notices": any `Cargo.lock` change, a Dependabot one included, regenerates the file) and the helper archives' fourth file; the wiki's Contributing page regenerated (never pushed).
  - **Tests:** xtask 18 new in `licenses.rs` (the cfg and SPDX readers and their refusals, the closure on a synthetic resolve with Windows-only, Unix-only, WebAssembly-only, dev and build edges, the layout and its dedup, a GPL crate refused naming it and a dev-only one not, a crate without files, the difference, the wrap, the Apache text's hash, the targets equal package.yml's, and the real closure: Windows-, Mac-, Linux- and Unix-only crates on their targets, the other platforms' crates out, and **every crate `cargo tree -e normal --target T` builds for each target in the closure on that target**), and the triggers test; Python: `test_ci_scripts` 3 new (a flattened copy, the glob refusal, the real pyproject) with the fixtures carrying the notices, `test_helper_release` (four members, a three-file archive refused, the archived notices the checkout's bytes), `test_workflows` 1 new (the notices job) and 2 plants (rules 13 and 3 in it).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **The closure is cargo metadata's, per target, and errs on the side of more.** metadata's resolve unifies features over the whole workspace, dev-dependencies included, so it names seven crates no release build compiles in (ed25519-dalek's `pkcs8` chain: `pkcs8`, `der`, `spki`, `const-oid`, `base64ct`; `rand_core` 0.6; `zerocopy-derive`), which gate 1's cross-check against `cargo tree` found. It can never miss one, which is the direction that matters; resolving features per root and per target would mean re-implementing cargo's resolver. The header says so, and the test holds the closure to `cargo tree` in the other direction. The platform conditions, though, are evaluated per target (P5.5's "every release target"), so a crate for a target CITAR does not build is out.
  - **Texts printed once.** P5.5 asks for each crate's texts; printed every time, the closure's 333 texts would come to about 1.7 MB, most of it the same Apache-2.0 and MIT texts. Each crate still lists its files, and a repeated text names where it is printed in full.
  - **The standard-text fallback** for packages that ship no licence file (the objc2 crates), Apache-2.0 only, rather than refusing them or copying texts from their repositories by hand.
  - **package.yml's `notices` job** is new: P5.5 asks the release to carry the file, and release.yml runs no rust.yml, so the release's own run proves and uploads it (5-08's `assemble` downloads `pkg-third-party-licenses`).
  - **`--metadata` and `--out`**, for gate 2's planted copy and gate 1's repeated runs; the targets come from rustc, not a table, so a toolchain bump that changes a target's configuration shows in `--check`.

## P5.6 The Windows bundle and installer

**From the wheel, never the checkout.** package.yml's `windows` job (needs `wheels`):
1. a clean Python 3.12 venv (the bundle's Python, as 0.1.5's), `pip install --only-binary=:all: "<windows wheel>[all]"`, PyInstaller at a pinned version, pillow for `make_icon.py`; `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` written by `installer/python_licenses.py` (P5.5);
2. `installer/citar.spec` collects `citar` **from site-packages**: no checkout on `sys.path` or `pathex` (the entry scripts are still read from `installer/`, whose folder holds no `citar/`); PyInstaller runs as `python -P -m PyInstaller <absolute path to citar.spec>` **from a working directory outside the checkout** (`$RUNNER_TEMP/bundle`), because `-m` from the checkout's root would put its `citar/` ahead of site-packages; `citar._engine` named in `hiddenimports`, and the spec fails unless the analysis resolved `citar` and `citar._engine` to the installed wheel's files (no module from the checkout's `citar/`); data from the installed package; `LICENSE`, `NOTICE.md`, `README.md`, `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` and `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` at the root; `--distpath` and `--workpath` under `$RUNNER_TEMP`;
3. **the bundle runs:** `citar.exe --version` (V), `where`, `doctor --quiet` with every state variable in a temporary folder; `_engine.pyd` present; `build_info()`'s label; and the job host started **from the bundle** (P5.0.11 item 1's fix);
4. **Inno Setup:** `ISCC.exe` from the runner image when present, else installed on the runner (`choco install innosetup`, a pinned version); `/DAppVersion=V /DAppVersionNumeric=N /DBundleDir=<the bundle's absolute folder>` (Inno's `VersionInfoVersion` must be numeric, so a prerelease such as `0.1.6-rc.1` no longer breaks the compile; `Source: "{#BundleDir}\*"` replaces the hard-coded `..\dist\CITAR\*`, whose default stays for a hand build);
5. **a silent per-user install on the runner:** `CITAR-V-setup.exe /VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /CURRENTUSER /DIR=<temp> /TASKS="" /LOG=<temp>`, then the installed `citar.exe --version` and `doctor --quiet`, the uninstall key `{9C1D2A54-...}_is1` present under HKCU, then `unins000.exe /VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES` and the folder and key gone (the data prompt's default is No, so data stays);
6. **the portable zip** `CITAR-V-windows-x64.zip` (the folder `CITAR/`, for Scoop), each file with its `.sha256`, uploaded as `pkg-windows`.

**Product fixes that ship with it** (5-05):
- **The job host in a frozen app** (`citar/jobs/host.py`): when `sys.frozen`, the command is `citar.exe` beside the running executable with a hidden `jobs-host` command (added to `citar/cli.py`, calling `engine_api.run_job_host()`), started with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, so the windowed `citar-play.exe` starts its lab's host through the console `citar.exe` without a flashing window; otherwise **`python -P -m citar.jobs host`**: `-P` (Python 3.11, the floor) keeps the working directory off `sys.path`, so under a unit whose working directory is the state directory (P5.13) no file there can shadow the package.
- **`citar doctor` gains a "jobs" line**: it starts the job host for a moment and reports `verifiable` (the canary and the self-check) or why not. Every smoke test (wheels, sdist, bundle, image) then proves the job host on its platform through one command.
- **`/api/meta`** returns `version`, `label` and `mcp_command`, the command that starts this install's MCP bridge (a bundle: its `citar.exe mcp`; a wheel: the `citar-mcp` script beside its interpreter; a checkout: `python citar_mcp.py`), and in server mode no filesystem path at all (a remote player runs `citar-mcp` from their own install); `lobby.js` builds its commands from `mcp_command`.
- **`installer/citar.iss`:** the version only from `/DAppVersion` (its default held by `release.py check`), `VersionInfoVersion={#AppVersionNumeric}`, the bundle's folder from `/DBundleDir` (`test_installer_script.py` holds the three defines).

Code signing stays out (KNOWN_ISSUES): SmartScreen warns, every asset has its SHA-256 and an attestation (P5.12).

**As built in 5-05: the Windows bundle and installer, and the installed copies' fixes** (Appendix F, 5-05). One agent, from 2026-10-20 09:26 to 11:08 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-05` from `5513b72`; its fix round from 11:26 (below, "Fix round").

- **What was built.**
  - **The kind of install** (`citar/paths.py`): `install_kind()` is `frozen` (PyInstaller's `sys.frozen`), `checkout` (the folder holding the package holds `pyproject.toml` or `DESIGN.md`, writable or not) or `wheel`; `bundle_program()` is `citar.exe` beside the running executable (the windowed `citar-play.exe` included); `mcp_command(server_mode, kind)` is the command that starts the install's MCP bridge.
  - **The job host's command** (`citar/jobs/host.py`): `command(kind)` is `citar.exe jobs-host` in the bundle, started with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` as before, and `python -P -m citar.jobs host` otherwise. `command_env(kind)` puts a checkout's own folder first on the host's `PYTHONPATH`, and `JobHost` takes it whenever it runs its own command: `-P` drops the working directory a checkout found its package by, so without it CI's test jobs (run from the checkout, no `.pth`) would start no host, and `C:/dev/venv/py312-check`, whose `citar-dev.pth` names the main checkout, would start one of another checkout's code (S1b runs exactly that). A wheel's site-packages and a bundle need nothing. `citar/cli.py` has the hidden `jobs-host` (not in `--help`; any argument is a usage error, exit 2), which runs `engine_api.run_job_host()` as `python -m citar.jobs host` does.
  - **`citar doctor`'s jobs line** (`_check_jobs`, between the ruleset and the providers): a job host of one thread, 25% of the machine and 256 MB, started from this install as the server starts it (`JobHost`'s own command), ready within 60 s, then closed: `ok` `verifiable` with its start time, jobs build and how its limits hold; `warn` with the host's `reason` when it is not verifiable (a modded ruleset does that; the server still plays its own lab); `FAIL` with the `HostError`'s code and message when it did not start; `warn` "not checked" when the engine does not load.
  - **`/api/meta`** (`app.meta_payload`): `version`, `label` (`build_info()`'s), `mode`, `server_url` (as before) and `mcp_command`; `project_root`, `python` and `mcp_script` are gone. A local server gives its own command: the bundle's `<folder>\citar.exe mcp`, a wheel's `citar-mcp` script (the scheme's scripts folder or beside the interpreter; else the interpreter with `-P -m citar.agents.mcp_server`), a checkout's `python <root>/citar_mcp.py`. **In server mode no folder of the server's:** the bundle's `citar.exe mcp` by its name alone, else `citar-mcp`. The lobby (`citar/web/js/mcpcommand.js`, tested by `mcpcommand.test.mjs`) builds the Claude Code command and the client configuration from `mcp_command` (quoting a word only where a shell would split it, falling back to `citar-mcp` when a server gives none) and, for a server-mode server, says the command runs from the player's own install. `scripts/server_soak.py` records the version and label (it read an `engine` key `/api/meta` never had).
  - **`installer/citar.spec`**, from the installed wheel: no checkout on `sys.path` or `pathex`; its helpers loaded by path from `installer/` (`spec_rules.py`, `python_licenses.py`); `citar._engine` named in `hiddenimports`; **every data file of the installed package** (`collect_data_files("citar")` less the `.pyi` stub, and the migrations' `.py` files). `installer/spec_rules.py` holds it to P5.6: before anything is collected it refuses `--distpath` or `--workpath` inside the checkout, a `CITAR_EXT_DIR` (the dev loop's engine, which `citar/__init__.py` puts first), and a `citar` or `citar._engine` that does not resolve to the installed package; after the analysis it fails unless every `citar` module, extension and data file came from that package, exactly one `citar/_engine.pyd` was taken, and every data file the package has is there (`package_data`). After `COLLECT` it puts the notices **at the bundle's root**, beside the executables (PyInstaller 6 puts data under `_internal/`): `LICENSE`, `NOTICE.md` and `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` from the installed wheel's `dist-info/licenses/` (the checkout's `packaging/` only for a wheel without the third), `README.md` from the checkout, and `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt`, written there from the venv the bundle is built in.
  - **`installer/python_licenses.py`** (`python -P installer/python_licenses.py --out FILE`, also what the spec runs): the installed closure of `citar[all]` through `importlib.metadata` (each `Requires-Dist` read by `packaging`, or pip's copy of it where `packaging` is absent, as in CI's test jobs; markers evaluated for this interpreter with no extra or an extra asked of that distribution; a distribution's extras followed as a requirement names them, `citar[all]`'s own `citar[anthropic,openai,mcp,oauth,keyring]` included); each distribution's name, version, licence (`License-Expression`, else the `License` field's first line and the licence classifiers), project page, and every licence or notice file in its installed metadata (PEP 639's `licenses/`, or an older wheel's files beside `METADATA`), a text printed once and named after; then CPython's `LICENSE.txt` (from `sys.base_prefix`, else beside the standard library) and the licence PyInstaller ships for its bootloader (`COPYING.txt`, the GPL with the bootloader exception). No path, time or machine detail, so two runs over one venv write the same bytes. A requirement that holds and is not installed, a missing CPython licence or no PyInstaller are refused (exit 1).
  - **`installer/citar.iss`**: `#ifndef BundleDir` (a hand build's `..\dist\CITAR`) and `Source: "{#BundleDir}\*"`; `PrivilegesRequiredOverridesAllowed=commandline dialog`, so a silent install's `/CURRENTUSER` is taken; the build comment names the three defines and `/O`. The uninstaller's question about the person's data is now **`SuppressibleMsgBox(..., MB_YESNO or MB_DEFBUTTON2, IDNO)`**: a plain `MsgBox` is shown even under `/VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES`, so winget's and CI's silent uninstall would have waited for a click; suppressed, it answers No and the data stays.
  - **`scripts/ci/check_bundle.py`** (what the `windows` job and this package's gate run; standard library): `bundle` (`citar.exe --version` V, `where` with every state folder under `--work`, `doctor --quiet` and `doctor` exit 0, the engine line's label and V, the jobs line `verifiable` with the jobs build, `jobs-host now` exit 2, `citar.exe mcp --help`, exactly one `citar/_engine.pyd`, the five notices at the root with `LICENSE`, `NOTICE.md` and `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` the checkout's bytes, `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` naming CPython and the bootloader and no build tool; with `--python`, every data file of the venv's package in the bundle and `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` byte for byte a fresh run's; and the bundle's files unchanged by every run, so the installer and the zip carry what PyInstaller made), `installed` (the installed `citar.exe` and `doctor --quiet`; the per-user uninstall key from the `.iss`'s AppId, read only, its `DisplayVersion` and `InstallLocation`), `uninstalled` (key and files gone within `--wait`), `zip` (`CITAR-V-windows-x64.zip`, the bundle under `CITAR/`, entries in name order dated 1980-01-01, so the same files make the same bytes) and `sums`. Every run of the bundle's programs drops the `CITAR_*` variables and has its state, key file, no credential store and the per-user bases under `--work`, as `gate_run.py server` gives a server.
  - **package.yml's `windows` job** (needs `meta` and `wheels`, on `windows-latest`, `!cancelled()` and `meta` passed, 60 minutes, no cache): checkout, Python 3.12, prepare, `pkg-wheel-windows-x64`; a venv in `$RUNNER_TEMP` with `--only-binary=:all: "<wheel>[all]"`, `pyinstaller==6.22.3` and `pyinstaller-hooks-contrib==2026.8` (both pinned in the job's `env`); `python -P -m PyInstaller "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/installer/citar.spec"` from `$RUNNER_TEMP/bundle`, output there; `check_bundle.py bundle` with the meta job's label; Inno Setup from the image (windows-2025 has 6.7.1) or else `choco install innosetup --version 6.7.1`, its version printed; `ISCC /DAppVersion /DAppVersionNumeric /DBundleDir /O$RUNNER_TEMP\installer`; the silent per-user install into `$RUNNER_TEMP\installed\CITAR` (`/VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /CURRENTUSER /TASKS=""`), `check_bundle.py installed`, a file planted in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`, `unins000.exe /VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART`, `check_bundle.py uninstalled`, the planted file still there (then removed); the zip and the sums. Uploads: **`pkg-windows`** (exactly `CITAR-V-setup.exe`, `CITAR-V-windows-x64.zip` and their `.sha256`, `release.py assets`' Windows group; rule 9 refused a `windows/*` path) and **`pkg-windows-report`** (`windows-bundle.json`: version, label, build id, jobs build, the engine and the notices' hashes; and the install's two reports). check_workflows: 8 workflows, 37 jobs.
  - **The wheels' smoke** (`smoke_wheel.py`) runs `citar doctor` too and requires its jobs line `verifiable` (5-02b's "Left for later").
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_install_kinds.py` (11: the kinds, `mcp_command` per kind and mode, `/api/meta` per mode and kind with no folder in server mode, the route); `tests/test_installer_script.py` (24: the `.iss` (CRLF, per user and `commandline`, the three defines and their use, `OutputBaseFilename` as `release.py assets` names it, the PATH task unchecked, `skipifsilent`, the data question suppressible and answered No, the AppId `check_bundle.py` reads), `python_licenses.py` on synthetic distributions (markers, extras, a package's own extras, licences, texts, dedup, determinism, refusals), `spec_rules.py` (each refusal, the data rule, the notices from the wheel or the checkout), `check_bundle.py` (the zip and sums against the release's Windows assets, the isolated environment, `uninstalled` on Windows)); `test_job_host.Commands` (4: the command per kind with `-P`, the bundle's command beside the running program, the hidden command serving as a ready, verifiable host, and **a `citar` planted in the host's working directory not imported, and imported with `-P` removed**); `test_engine_api.DoctorTests` (4 more: verifiable, a host that does not start, one not verifiable, no engine); `test_workflows` (the `windows` job); `mcpcommand.test.mjs` (6).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **`mcp_command` in server mode.** P5.6 says a remote player runs `citar-mcp` from their own install, and gate 2 says the bundle's `/api/meta` names `citar.exe mcp` and no path. Both hold: in server mode the install's own command is given by its program's name alone (the bundle's `citar.exe mcp`; a wheel's or a checkout's `citar-mcp`, which `install.sh`, pipx and pip put on a player's PATH), and the lobby says it runs from the player's own install.
  - **The spec writes `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` itself**, from the venv it builds in, rather than the job writing it first: a hand build is one command, the job and the gate run the same thing, and `check_bundle.py --python` compares it with a fresh run of the script in that venv.
  - **No pillow and no `make_icon.py` in the job** (P5.6's step 1 lists them): `installer/citar.ico` is committed, and `make_icon.py` writes into the checkout (`citar.ico` and `docs/assets/icon.png`), which a release build must not change. The local gate's venv has pillow, so that the closure is shown to leave it out.
  - **PyInstaller and its hooks both pinned**, 6.22.3 (2026-09-12) and 2026.8 (2026-09-30): the hooks decide what a bundle collects as much as PyInstaller does.
  - **Every data file of the package, checked**, not a list (gate 2 found the list's gap: below).
  - **`check_bundle.py`** is new (P5.6 names the checks, not a script): the job's steps and the local gate are one program, and 5-08 reads its report.
  - **An empty `--distpath` folder.** PyInstaller makes `--distpath` before it runs the spec, so a refused run that named a folder inside the checkout leaves that folder, empty; the gate's refusal demonstration removed the one it made.
  - **One package ships no licence file:** dnspython 2.9.0 (ISC, through email-validator) has none in its wheel's metadata, so its section names its licence and project page and says so; no text is written in its name. (Superseded in the fix round: the ISC licence asks for its notice in every copy, so CITAR keeps a verbatim copy of dnspython's licence.)
- **What the gates found, and the fixes.**
  1. **The bundle had no `citar/jobs/costs.json`** (gate 2's first run: the server logged "could not read the committed job costs" with its traceback). 0.1.5's spec listed the data it took, and Phase 4 added the lab's committed costs to the package after it. The spec now takes every data file and fails a bundle that lacks one, and `check_bundle.py` compares the bundle with the venv's package: run on the first bundle it fails naming `jobs/costs.json`.
  2. **A silent uninstall would have hung on the data question** (found writing the job's uninstall step, against Inno Setup's documentation: `/SUPPRESSMSGBOXES` suppresses `SuppressibleMsgBox`, never `MsgBox`). Fixed as above; `test_installer_script` holds it.

- **Left for later.**
  - **5-08's `assemble`** downloads `pkg-windows` (four files: the two assets and their `.sha256`) into `release/`, and holds `pkg-windows-report`'s `windows-bundle.json` (`version`, `label`, `build_id`, `jobs_build`) to the wheels', helpers' and images' lines.
  - **5-06** can prove the image's job host through the same line: `citar doctor` (with `--quiet`, a failure prints and exits 1) as uid 10001.
  - **5-12's docs:** AI_PLAYERS's bridge line (the bundle's `citar.exe mcp`; a public server's seats show `citar-mcp`, run from the player's own install), the CHANGELOG (the doctor's jobs line; `/api/meta`'s keys, `project_root`, `python` and `mcp_script` gone; the bundle's notices and `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt`; a silent uninstall keeps the data; the job host's `-P`), INSTALL.md's bundle section. (The fix round already gave ARCHITECTURE.md, BOTS.md, server/WORKERS.md and the CHANGELOG's job host entry the host's `-P` command.)
- **Fix round** (2026-10-20 from 11:26 (-04:00), one agent, after the review; logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-05/logs` (`gates.log`, `gate1-*`, `gate2.log`, `gate3.log`, `quick.log`, `fix-s1*.log`, `fix/`), the implementation's moved to `logs/impl/`; scripts in the session scratchpad's `p505/`). The review found six issues, two major and four minor. All six were valid and all are fixed, in commits `d9266ec`, `08f6d8b`, `42eaee4` and this note.
  1. **The bundle carried setuptools** (major). cffi's build-time shim (`cffi._shimmed_dist_utils`) imports setuptools. So the first bundle's archives held setuptools' 98 modules, `_distutils_hack`, packaging's 16 modules and setuptools' vendored `*.dist-info` folders, and both executables ran `pyi_rth_setuptools` at every start. None of it is in `citar[all]`'s closure or in `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt`. `check_bundle`'s build-tool test read only the file's index.
     - **The fix:** `citar.spec` excludes `setuptools`, `pkg_resources` and `_distutils_hack`. Nothing imports them at run time: cffi compiles nothing in the bundle.
     - **The rule behind it, at build time:** `spec_rules.check_sources` checks where every module, binary, data file and script of both analyses came from.
       - A file of the venv's site-packages must belong, by the installed distributions' `RECORD`, to `citar` or its closure (`python_licenses.shipped`), or be one of PyInstaller's run-time files (`RUNTIME_FILES`: its loader, its run-time hooks and modules, the community hooks' run-time hooks).
       - Any other file must come from the embedded Python (its own site-packages excepted), Windows or the work folder, and the entry scripts from `installer/`.
       - A distribution outside the closure is named once, with how many of its files were taken.
     - **The same rule on a built bundle:** `spec_rules.py audit DIR`, run with `-P` in the venv, reads the bundle's `_internal` files and its executables' archives (PyInstaller's `pkg_archive_contents`). `check_bundle.py bundle --python` runs it. Without the venv, `check_bundle` still refuses a Universal CRT DLL or a `*.dist-info` folder of a distribution the index does not list.
  2. **43 DLLs came from the build machine's PATH** (major): `ucrtbase.dll` and 42 `api-ms-win-*.dll` from this laptop's JDK folder (`C:\Program Files\Eclipse Adoptium\jdk-17.0.17.10-hotspot\bin`), which PyInstaller found first on `PATH`. The spec now does three things:
     - It sets the analysis's `PATH` to the embedded Python (its folder and `DLLs`) and Windows' `System32` and folder (`spec_rules.dll_path`). No tool's folder can lend a DLL, on CI's runner as on this laptop.
     - It leaves the Universal CRT out (`spec_rules.without_ucrt`). Windows 10 is the installer's `MinVersion`, and it always uses its own UCRT and ignores an application's copy (Microsoft's "Universal CRT deployment"; PyInstaller's `depend/dylib.py` notes the same).
     - It refuses any file from outside the allowed folders, naming the folder (the rule above).

     Otherwise the bundle's binaries are the same 54, from the same folders.
  3. **dnspython had no licence text** (minor). dnspython 2.9.0 (ISC, through email-validator) ships no licence file in its wheel, and the ISC licence asks for its notice in every copy.
     - `installer/licenses/dnspython.txt` is dnspython's `LICENSE`, byte for byte as its 2.8.0 wheel carries it (SHA-256 `c3ea3ff5...d741cd`). It was read against the `v2.9.0` tag's `LICENSE`, which has the same text.
     - `python_licenses.KEPT` names the file and records where it came from.
     - A shipped distribution with neither a licence file in its metadata nor a kept copy is refused (exit 1, and the spec refuses).
  4. **The lobby left Windows paths unquoted** (minor). `quoteArg` now quotes any word with a backslash. Git Bash, WSL and any POSIX shell drop a bare backslash; inside double quotes, a backslash before a letter is kept (checked in Git Bash).
  5. **The hint for a bundle's server-mode command named the wrong program** (minor). `mcpHint` now follows the command:
     - For `citar.exe`, it says the program is the Windows app's. It runs from the app's install folder, or by its full path, unless the installer added that folder to PATH. Any other install writes `citar-mcp` in place of `citar.exe mcp`.
     - For `citar-mcp`, the hint is as before.
  6. **Three docs named the old host command** (minor). ARCHITECTURE.md, BOTS.md, server/WORKERS.md and the CHANGELOG's job host entry now say `python -P -m citar.jobs host`, or `citar.exe jobs-host` in the Windows app. The wiki was regenerated, not pushed.
  - **Found while writing the rule: the executables carry two sets of run-time hooks.**
    - PyInstaller's own run-time hooks (Apache-2.0, in its `COPYING.txt`).
    - `pyinstaller-hooks-contrib`'s `pyi_rth_cryptography_openssl`, `pyi_rth_pythoncom` and `pyi_rth_pywintypes` (Apache-2.0, in its `LICENSE`).

    `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt`'s PyInstaller line is now "PyInstaller V's bootloader and run-time hooks: GPL-2.0-or-later WITH Bootloader-exception; Apache-2.0". A section "pyinstaller-hooks-contrib V's run-time hooks: Apache-2.0" with that licence follows it. `check_bundle` reads both lines.
  - **Tests:**
    - `test_installer_script`, 24 to 32: the kept copy and the refusal without one, dnspython's text, the run-time sections, the index as `check_bundle` reads it, what a bundle carries, the UCRT left out, the DLL path, the source rule (the closure, run-time files, an unowned file, another site-packages, a JDK's folder) and the audit.
    - `mcpcommand.test.mjs`, 6 to 7.
- **Fix round gates** (at `42eaee4`, the code's head; this note uncommitted):

---

## P5.7 The image

- **Base:** `python:3.12-slim-trixie` (Debian 13, glibc 2.41; bookworm's regular security support ended in mid-2026), pinned by digest with the tag in a comment (Dependabot's `docker` updates move it). The wheel's manylinux_2_28 tag runs there. Python stays 3.12, the VPS's, so the image and the deploy run one interpreter; Dependabot's open pull request #6 (3.14 on bookworm) is superseded, and the owner may close it.
- **Dependencies:** `requirements.py server` as now, installed `--only-binary=:all:` and, when `wheelhouse/constraints.txt` is present (CI copies the release's constraints there, P5.3.4), with `--constraint`, so the image runs the dependency set the release was tested with. Then `citar` from `wheelhouse/` with `--no-deps --no-index` (pip takes the wheel that matches the build's architecture).
- **Built natively, no QEMU:** package.yml's `image` matrix builds `linux/amd64` on `ubuntu-24.04` and `linux/arm64` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, each from its own wheel, `docker buildx build --load`. Each image is run: the health route answers within 60 s; `citar._engine` has no test operations; `citar --version` and `build_info()` give V and L; `citar doctor --quiet` (the job host verifiable) as uid 10001; a restart keeps the volume's state. `docker compose config` checks `docker-compose.yml`.
- **What a tag pushes is what was tested:** on a dry run and a tag, each job saves its image (`docker save`, zstd) as the artifact `pkg-image-<arch>`; release.yml's tag-only jobs load those, push them by architecture and join them in one manifest list (P5.11). Pull requests skip the save.

**As built in 5-06: the image from wheels, both architectures** (Appendix F, 5-06). One agent, from 2026-10-20 16:38 to 17:47 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-06` from `4dba759`; its fix round from 18:01 (below, "Fix round").

- **What was built.**
  - **`Dockerfile`.**
    - **The base** is `python:3.12-slim-trixie@sha256:a6e34c598f2467ed0e9a8d349809fcd8b5c603269512df273a0bb1784edc11b1`, the multi-architecture index Docker Hub served on 2026-10-20 (read from the registry, read only): Python 3.12.15 on Debian 13, built 2026-10-06; its linux/amd64 manifest is `2b4f19da...` and its linux/arm64 one `16bf2b5c...`. One reference serves both stages and both architectures. The tag is in the reference and the comment above it, so Dependabot's `docker` updates can move the digest.
    - **The builder** makes a venv and reads the `[server]` requirements with `scripts/ci/requirements.py server`. It installs them with `pip install --only-binary=:all:`, and with `--constraint /tmp/wheelhouse/constraints.txt` when `wheelhouse/constraints.txt` is there (a hand build without it is told it gets the newest wheels in `pyproject.toml`'s ranges). Then `citar` from `wheelhouse/` with `--only-binary=:all: --no-deps --no-index`, then `pip check`, then `import citar._engine` with `-P`.
    - **No `apt-get` in either stage.** Nothing compiles, so `build-essential` and `libffi-dev` went. The health check is Python, so `curl` went. pip, setuptools and wheel are no longer upgraded from the index. The build reaches PyPI for wheels at the constraints' versions and nothing else, and the image's system packages are the pinned base's.
    - **The runtime** has `groupadd --gid 10001` and `useradd --uid 10001 --gid citar`, and `/var/lib/citar` is `citar:citar` 0750. The venv is copied from the builder and stays root's. `USER 10001:10001` is given by number. `ENV`, `VOLUME`, `EXPOSE`, `WORKDIR`, `ENTRYPOINT ["citar"]` and `CMD ["serve"]` are as before. The `HEALTHCHECK` is `["python", "-I", "-c", "...build_opener(ProxyHandler({})).open('http://127.0.0.1:%s/api/auth/config' % CITAR_PORT...)"]`: the venv's Python, `-I` because the working directory is the state directory, on the port the server listens on, asking the server directly (the fix round's; the first used `urlopen`, which asks any proxy the environment names). The image is 280 MB (Podman's figure).
  - **`.dockerignore`** is an allow-list: `*`, then `!pyproject.toml`, `!scripts/ci/requirements.py` and `!wheelhouse`, exactly what the Dockerfile copies. No state, secret, configuration or source tree can reach a build, and an edit anywhere else changes no layer.
  - **`.github/dependabot.yml`**: the `docker` ecosystem ignores `python`'s major and minor updates. Its weekly pull request moves the digest, and the Python stays 3.12, the VPS's, until someone moves it by hand with the server. (Dependabot's open pull request #6, 3.14 on bookworm, is superseded; the owner may close it.)
  - **`scripts/ci/check_image.py`** (standard library; Docker or Podman; exit 0, 1 or 2):
    - `run --engine E --image REF --arch A --version V --label L --wheel F [--constraints C] --out R`.
      - **The image as built:** linux/A; `USER 10001:10001`; the state volume and `CITAR_STATE_DIR` at `/var/lib/citar`; port 8765; a health check; the label `org.opencontainers.image.version` equal to V.
      - **Then run as deployed:** a volume of its own, the port published on 127.0.0.1 only (any other address is a failure). The health route must answer within 60 s, and `/api/meta` must give V and L.
      - **Inside it, as uid and gid 10001:** `citar --version`; the image's own health check exits 0 (and, since the fix round, again with `http_proxy` at a dead address); the engine is the venv's and has no test operations; `build_info()` gives V and L, the build id and the jobs build; `citar doctor --quiet` exits 0 and `citar doctor`'s jobs line is verifiable; the state directory is `10001:10001:750`; `pip freeze --exclude citar` against C (every installed distribution pinned there at that version; pins not installed are noted).
      - **Then the wheels' smoke** (`smoke_wheel.py inside`) in a second container: no network, the checkout's `scripts/` mounted read-only, the state in its `/tmp`, the image's server variables unset (since the fix round, the run's named volume `citar-check-<random>-smoke-state` at the image's state path, so the engine makes it no anonymous volume). Its job host plays the seeded lab job, and the line's version, label, build id and jobs build must be the server's.
      - **Then the restart:** an account made with `citar admin create-user --password-stdin` (a throwaway passphrase) and the generated secret key's hash. The first container is stopped by SIGTERM (exit 0 within 30 s) and removed, and a second one is started on the same volume: its health within 60 s, the account listed, the key's hash equal, the state folder's entries kept, `/api/meta` again, then stopped the same way.
      - **Cleanup:** every container and the volume (named `citar-check-<random>`) are removed in a `finally`, and their absence is checked (since the fix round: each container with `rm -f -v`, then, once the containers are gone, both volumes). The report is written only when every check held; it holds the facts above, the smoke line and the timings.
    - `save --engine E --image REF --out F.tar.zst [--report R]`: the image must be the one the report tested. The engine's `save` (Podman's as `docker-archive`) is followed by `zstd -T0`. The image is removed by its id, the file is loaded again, and the same id is required; the report gains the file's name, size and SHA-256.
  - **package.yml's `image` matrix replaces the moved `docker` job.** It has `amd64` on `ubuntu-24.04` (`linux-x64`) and `arm64` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (`linux-arm64`), each the runner its platform's wheel is built on. It needs `meta`, `wheels` and `constraints`, and runs `!cancelled()` with `meta` passed. Its steps:
    - prepare; `uname -m` against the leg's, with `docker version`, `docker buildx version` and `zstd --version` printed;
    - `pkg-wheel-<platform>` into `wheelhouse/` and `pkg-constraints`, copied to `wheelhouse/constraints.txt`;
    - `docker buildx build --load --platform linux/<arch> --provenance=false --sbom=false` with the version and revision labels, tagged `ghcr.io/jprodgers/citar:<V>-<arch>`, the name release.yml pushes (nothing here pushes);
    - `check_image.py run`, then `check_image.py save` to `image/citar-image-<V>-<arch>.tar.zst`;
    - `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml config --quiet` on the amd64 leg, with stand-ins for its two required values;
    - uploads: `pkg-image-report-<arch>` (`report/image-<arch>.json`) on every run, and `pkg-image-<arch>` (the `.tar.zst`, `compression-level: 0`, 14 days) only when the mode is not `ci`.
    No cache, no QEMU, no `docker/*` action: `docker/setup-buildx-action` and `docker/build-push-action` left the workflows with the old job (`action_pins.py verify`: 145 references to 12 pins in 12 repositories, 0 problems). check_workflows: 8 workflows, 37 jobs.
  - **Tests.**
    - **`tests/test_image.py` (17 tests).** The Dockerfile: one base for both stages, pinned by digest; wheels only; constraints; `pip check`; no apt and no compiler; the runtime's one `COPY`, user, ids, state, volume, port, entry point and labels; the health check `python -I`, run against a stand-in server, passing on 200 and failing on 500 and 404. Also: the context allow-list equal to the Dockerfile's sources; Compose running the repository package.yml tags with the same state path; Dependabot's hold. And check_image's readings: the port on loopback alone; either engine's ids; the image's facts with each wrong field refused, Podman's health-check key included; the doctor's jobs line; the freeze comparison with PEP 503 names; the smoke line; a command's JSON answer; the smoke's script; usage errors exit 2.
    - **`tests/test_workflows.py` (76, 75 before).** `test_the_image_is_built_natively_for_both_architectures_checked_and_kept` holds the job to P5.7: legs, runners, downloads, step order, the build's flags, the checks' arguments, nothing pushed or cached, the two uploads and when. The rules are planted in the real job: rule 13 on its wheel download, rule 3 on `--push` and on `docker push`, rule 13 on an unprefixed upload, and rule 9 on a `runner.temp` path.
  - **CONTRIBUTING** names the Dockerfile's pin and how to build and check the image under Podman. The wiki is regenerated, never pushed.
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **No apt and no curl** (above): P5.7 asks for `--only-binary`, which leaves nothing to compile. A health check in the server's Python with `-I` removes the last package from Debian's archive and the moving mirror state with it.
  - **`pip check`** after the installs, so a constraints file that does not fit the requirements fails the build, not the run.
  - **The user by number, the group 10001, the state 0750**, as on the server's release layout (P5.13.1). A platform that admits only images it can tell are not root's (Kubernetes' `runAsNonRoot`) can tell. 0.1.5's group was a system gid; a volume from 0.1.5 stays writable, since its owner is uid 10001 (for 5-12's CHANGELOG).
  - **The wheels' smoke runs inside the image** (P5.7 names the doctor's job host; this adds the seeded lab job). The image's line carries the wheels' result hash, `4914910c...3e1358`, so 5-08 can hold the images to the wheels as well as to one build.
  - **The dependencies are read back** (`pip freeze` against the constraints). P5.7 says the image installs with the constraints; this proves it did.
  - **A restart is a new container on the same volume**, not `docker restart` of the same one: state left in a container's own layer would survive a restart and be lost by a redeploy.
  - **Pull requests save and load the image too, and upload nothing.** P5.7 says pull requests skip the save. The intent is no artifact on every push, and that holds; the save-and-load path release.yml relies on is proven on every pull request (P5.1.1: no failure left for the release to find). Here it took seconds.
  - **No cache.** The moved job read and wrote the GHA cache on every run. A release's image is now built from this run's files alone, as the wheels and the bundle are.
  - **`--provenance=false --sbom=false`:** the loaded image is one plain manifest, so the saved file holds the image and nothing else. release.yml's `attest-image` attests the pushed image (P5.12).
  - **Tagged as release.yml will push it** (`ghcr.io/jprodgers/citar:<V>-<arch>`), so 5-09 loads and pushes without retagging. The report's `saved.id` is the id it should find.
  - **`constraints` is a need of both legs:** the file from the linux-x64 wheel serves arm64 too. Every `[server]` dependency has the same markers on both Linux architectures, and the arm64 leg would show a gap as a `pip freeze` finding.
  - **`docker compose config` on one leg:** the file is the same for both.
  - **zstd in WSL** (apt, as root, recorded in `ops/p5-notes.md`), so the gate saves as the job does.
- **What the gates found, and the fixes.**
  - Gate 1's first run (`logs/wsl-gate1-first.log`, at `75ede57`) failed at the restart check: the account's address was `@example.invalid`, which the account rules (email-validator) refuse as a special-use name. Fixed in `4a18062` (`@example.com`). Every other check of that run passed, the three plants failed as they should, and nothing was left behind. The second run, at `4a18062`, passed whole (`logs/second/`).
  - Written while that ran, `4274c83`: a command's JSON answer that does not read is a check failure rather than a traceback; the health route and `/api/meta` are asked directly, never through a proxy the environment names; and the cleanup names what is left whether the run passed or failed.
  - Each `podman build` leaves its builder stage as a nameless image (Podman's cache). The gate script now removes those, and the two the first runs left were removed by hand: Podman holds only the three base images.

It checks the loaded id against the report's `saved.id`, pushes `ghcr.io/jprodgers/citar:<V>-<arch>` as tagged, and joins the two with `docker buildx imagetools create -t ...:<V>`. `attest-image` attests that list's digest.

- **Fix round** (2026-10-20 from 18:01 to 18:43 (-04:00), one agent, after the review; logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-06/logs/fix`; the scripts in the session scratchpad's `p506fix/`). The review found four minor issues. All four were valid and all are fixed, in commits `908c5e3`, `f90e8d3` and this note.
  1. **The health check asked any proxy the environment named.** `urlopen` follows `http_proxy`, which has no loopback exception, and on Windows the registry's proxy too. So a server run with `http_proxy` set for its model APIs' traffic and no `NO_PROXY` served normally and was reported unhealthy; the review reproduced it with `podman healthcheck run`. The `HEALTHCHECK` now opens the URL through `urllib.request.build_opener(urllib.request.ProxyHandler({}))`, an opener with no proxies.
     - `test_image` holds the command to that opener, with no `urlopen`. It runs the check with none of the developer's own proxy settings, then with `HTTP_PROXY` at `http://127.0.0.1:9`, where nothing listens: a 200 passes both ways and a 500 fails. The old command failed the proxied 200 here (`WinError 10061`, the dead proxy refusing).
     - `check_image.py run` runs the image's own check a second time, inside the container, with `http_proxy` and `HTTP_PROXY` at `http://127.0.0.1:9` and `no_proxy` empty (`exec -e`). An image whose check asks a proxy now fails the image job.
  2. **CONTRIBUTING's `check_image` command could not run**: it had no `--out`, which the parser requires, so it exited 2. It now ends `--constraints wheelhouse/constraints.txt --out /tmp/image-amd64.json` (leave out `--constraints` without a constraints file), and says the report is written when every check held. The wiki was regenerated, never pushed. `test_image` parses the documented command with `check_image`'s own parser; the old text fails it.
  3. **Two failure paths in `check_image`.**
     - **Its waits.** The smoke's limit was 2,400 s, the image job's whole `timeout-minutes: 40`, which also covers the build. So a hung smoke would have been cancelled by the runner: no message naming the step, and no cleanup. The smoke's and the save's limits are now 600 s, like `exec`'s. A test reads the job's `timeout-minutes` and holds every wait to a quarter of it: the four command limits, the health wait with its last `inspect`, a stop, and the cleanup's wait. A command that times out now shows its output as text. On Linux `TimeoutExpired.stdout` is bytes whatever `text=` says, so the old message showed a `b'...'` literal with its newlines escaped.
     - **Anonymous volumes.** The smoke's container mounted nothing at the image's `VOLUME`, so the engine gave it an anonymous volume. If that container outlived its `--rm` (its command timed out), the cleanup's `rm -f` removed it without `-v` and left the volume, and the leftover check, which looks by name, could not see it. Now the smoke mounts the run's named volume `<prefix>-smoke-state` at `/var/lib/citar`, and every `rm` has `-v`. The cleanup removes the containers, waits for them to be gone (at most 30 s, so a container still being removed lets go of its volume), then removes both volumes and lists what is left. `test_image` holds the smoke's mounts, the cleanup's commands and their order, and that what is left is named.
  4. **The as-built note's end time** said 17:50, an estimate written before the last checks; the real end was 17:47. It is fixed above, in `ops/p5-notes.md`'s heading and in the STATUS row.
  - **Tests:** `test_image` has 23 tests, from 17. The two health check tests are extended. New ones cover the waits, a hung command's message, `exec`'s environment, the smoke's volume, the cleanup, and CONTRIBUTING's command. Each failed on the code before its fix (`fix/wsl-oldcheck.log` runs them on Linux against `d085452`'s script). The one exception is the hung command's message on Windows, where the exception already holds text; on Linux it failed as expected.

## P5.8 The install scripts

**Wheels only.** `install.sh` and `install.ps1` install `citar[...]` with `--only-binary=citar` (the game's own sdist is never built on a user's machine) and `--prefer-binary` (each dependency at its newest version with a wheel for the machine; added at 5-07's merge, below). Before pip runs, each checks the platform against the wheel matrix and says plainly when there is no wheel: Linux x86_64 or aarch64 with glibc 2.28 or newer (`getconf GNU_LIBC_VERSION`; musl is named), macOS 11 or newer on arm64 or x86_64, Windows with an x64 Python (an ARM64 Python is told to use an x64 one or the installer), and a CPython with the GIL (`sysconfig.get_config_var('Py_GIL_DISABLED')` set means a free-threaded build such as 3.13t or 3.14t, which cannot load an abi3 wheel; it is told to use the regular build of the same version). Anything else gets one message: no prebuilt CITAR for this platform; `pip install citar` builds it from source with a Rust toolchain (rustup.rs). Python 3.11 or newer as before; pipx is documented as the alternative route (INSTALL.md), not used by the scripts, whose own venv does the same job with one dependency fewer.

**The helper (`install.sh --worker`, `install.ps1 -Worker`):** installs the helper alone, with no game: the platform's **self-updating** build (`citar-helper-<platform>`) from `releases/latest/download/` (or `releases/download/vX/` with `--version X`), its `.sha256` checked before anything is written, then `citar-helper[.exe]` in the bin folder, made executable, and the next step printed (`citar-helper setup`, then `citar-helper service install` for a service). The helper's platform check is the game's (glibc 2.28, P5.4.1), so one table covers both. Curl and `Invoke-WebRequest` leave no quarantine attribute, so macOS runs it without `xattr`. A package-managed helper is the packaged build (P5.9); this one updates itself.

**Testable without touching anything real:**
- `install.sh`: `--prefix`, `--bin-dir`, `--no-profile` (no shell profile line), `--from FILE|DIR` (a wheel, or a folder used as `--find-links`; dependencies still from PyPI), and `CITAR_RELEASE_URL` (the base for helper downloads; `https`, or `http` to a loopback host only).
- `install.ps1`: `-Prefix`, `-BinDir`, `-NoPath` (the user's PATH and the registry untouched), `-From`, the same `CITAR_RELEASE_URL`; `-Uninstall` honours `-Prefix` and `-BinDir`. On this laptop the script runs only through the gate harness (`scripts/ci/gate_run.py install-ps1`, P5.18.1), which refuses a run without `-Prefix` and `-BinDir` under the package's target folder and `-NoPath`.
- Each script's platform check has a test hook (an environment variable that substitutes the probed facts), so musl, an unknown architecture, an old glibc, an ARM64 Windows Python and a free-threaded Python are each tested without such a machine.

**`--server`** keeps installing a server into the release layout of P5.13 (5-10a moves it there).

**As built in 5-07: the install scripts, wheels only, the helper download** (Appendix F, 5-07). One agent from 2026-10-20 20:34 until the owner's weekly usage limit cut it off near its end (its last log at 22:49), then a second from 2026-10-22 23:13 to 2026-10-23 00:26 (-04:00), which finished it and ran every gate again; on branch `p-5-07` from `e6bd114`; its fix round from 2026-10-23 01:05 (below, "Fix round").

- **What was built.**
  - **`install.sh`** (bash 3.2 or later, so macOS's own bash runs it).
    - **The platform check**, before anything is made: Linux on x86_64 or aarch64 with glibc 2.28 or newer (`getconf GNU_LIBC_VERSION`; musl named, from `ldd --version` or `/lib/ld-musl-*`; no glibc found is named too), or macOS 11 or newer (`sw_vers`) on arm64 or x86_64. Anything else gets one message: "There is no prebuilt CITAR for this platform: <what>. 'pip install citar' builds it from source, with a Rust toolchain (https://rustup.rs)." The same table serves `--worker`, whose message names the helper's source build instead. Then the Python: 3.11 or newer, CPython, with the GIL (`Py_GIL_DISABLED`: a free-threaded build is told to use the regular build of the same version, `python3.14` not `python3.14t`), 64-bit.
    - **The test hook** `CITAR_INSTALL_TEST_FACTS` (`name=value ...`) replaces any probed fact: `system`, `machine`, `libc` (`glibc-2.39`, `musl`, `unknown`), `macos`, and the Python's `python`, `implementation`, `free_threaded`, `bits`.
    - **Wheels only:** CITAR is installed with `--only-binary=citar` (its dependencies as pip chooses). `--from FILE` takes a `.whl` only; `--from DIR` takes a folder of wheels: pip picks this machine's wheel from the folder alone (`pip download --no-index --only-binary=:all: --find-links DIR citar==V`), which is then installed as a file, with its dependencies from PyPI. A folder holding no wheel is refused with the no-wheel message, naming any sdist in it; a folder holding two releases needs `--version`. pip is brought up to date in the new venv first (Ubuntu 20.04's ensurepip knows no `manylinux_2_28`).
    - **`--prefix`, `--bin-dir`, `--no-profile`, `--python`.** The prefix and bin folder are made absolute. The prefix is marked (`.citar-installer`, written first, so an install that fails half way can be run again or removed); an install into a non-empty folder that is not a CITAR install is refused, and so are `/` and `$HOME` as a prefix.
    - **`--worker`** installs the helper alone (no Python): `citar-helper-<linux|macos>-<x64|arm64>` from `$CITAR_RELEASE_URL/latest/download/` (or `download/v<X>/` with `--version X`; the default base is `https://github.com/jprodgers/CITAR/releases`), its `.sha256` first, both into a temporary folder; the digest checked (64 hex characters, then equal) before anything is written; then copied into the bin folder, made 0755 and renamed into place, and the next steps printed (`citar-helper setup`, then `citar-helper service install`). `CITAR_RELEASE_URL` is refused before any request unless it is `https://`, or `http://` to `localhost`, `[::1]` or `127.a.b.c`; a URL with a user name in it is refused. Downloads use curl with `--proto` and `--proto-redir =https`, so a redirect cannot leave https.
    - **`--uninstall`** removes only what is the script's, within the given prefix and bin folder: the prefix if it holds the marker or a venv with `bin/citar` (0.1.5's), else it refuses and removes nothing; the links in the bin folder that point into the prefix (`citar`, `citar-admin`, `citar-mcp`, and 0.1.5's `citar-worker`); and `citar-helper` with its update leftovers (`.old`, `.citar-helper.new`; since the fix round also `.failed`, `.old.<pid>`, `.failed.<pid>` and the script's own `.citar-helper.install`). It refuses, removing nothing, while the helper runs as a service (a systemd unit or LaunchAgent that names that binary; since the fix round, by the bin folder's path as given or with its links resolved). `--uninstall --worker` removes the helper alone.
    - `--server` is unchanged apart from the platform check and `--only-binary` (5-10a moves it to P5.13's layout).
  - **`install.ps1`** (Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7; `PositionalBinding = $false`).
    - The same checks for Windows: an x64 CPython 3.11 or newer with the GIL (`sysconfig.get_platform()`); an ARM64 Python is told to install the x64 build (Windows 11 on ARM runs it) or use the installer; a 32-bit or non-CPython one gets the one message. The candidates (the py launcher's 3.14 to 3.11, then `python3`, `python`) are tried in order, the first that fits is taken, and the first new enough one says why when none fits. The hook's facts are `system`, `machine`, `python`, `implementation`, `python_platform` and `free_threaded`.
    - `-Prefix`, `-BinDir`, `-NoPath`, `-From` (a wheel or a folder, as `install.sh`), `-Python`, `-Worker` (`citar-helper-windows-x64.exe` into the bin folder as `citar-helper.exe`, checked the same way; an x86 machine is refused, an ARM64 one warned it runs under emulation; a redirect that leaves https is refused by the response's final address), `-Version`, `-NoSetup`, `-Uninstall` (the marked prefix, the shims that start its commands, the helper unless a Run value names it; the default bin folder removed when empty). (The fix round: run as a script block, its one-liners' way, it ends with `$LASTEXITCODE` rather than `exit`; the uninstall takes the helper's update leftovers too.)
    - **The user's PATH**, only without `-NoPath`: read from `HKCU\Environment` as stored (`%VARIABLES%` unexpanded) and written back with its kind kept, then `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` broadcast. The entry is appended as `;<dir>` and taken away the same way, so an install and its uninstall leave the value byte for byte as it was (0.1.5's `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable` turned a `REG_EXPAND_SZ` PATH into `REG_SZ` with every variable expanded).
  - **`scripts/ci/gate_run.py install-ps1 [--under DIR] [--env NAME=VALUE]... [--shell powershell|pwsh] [--timeout S] -- ARGS`** (P5.18.1 rule 3): the checkout's `install.ps1` with `-NoProfile` and `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass` (this process only). Refused unless `-Prefix` and `-BinDir` are each given once, strictly inside `--under`, and `-NoPath` is given; every parameter is written in full (PowerShell binds `-Pre` to `-Prefix`), a switch takes no `:value`, and no bare word or common parameter passes; an install needs `-NoSetup`; `--env` may give only `CITAR_RELEASE_URL` (a loopback address, or a documentation address, RFC 5737, for a refusal's test) and the hook, and `-Worker` needs the former. The run gets no inherited `CITAR_*` variable, the state variables, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `APPDATA`, `TEMP` and `TMP` inside `--under`, and no OS credential store; the user's PATH as stored is compared before and after, and a change exits 1. Its own options take no abbreviation (`allow_abbrev=False`, for `helper` and `server` too). (The fix round: each folder absolute, with no `~`, provider or PowerShell drive, and passed to the script as resolved; `--as-scriptblock`.)
  - **`scripts/ci/install_check.py sh|ps1 --wheel W --helper H --version V --label L --work DIR [--under DIR] [--python PY] [--path-route] --out FILE`**: either script against a release's own wheel and helper, the helper served by its own loopback HTTP server laid out as a release (`latest/download/`, `download/v<V>/`, and `bad/` with a wrong `.sha256`), every run under `--work` (claimed: missing, empty or an earlier run's), install.sh with a temporary `HOME`, `--prefix`, `--bin-dir` and `--no-profile`, install.ps1 only through `gate_run.run_install_ps1`, the helper only through `gate_run.py helper`. Its checks are the docstring's seven (the hook's refusals; wheels only; the game installed and run, `citar doctor` naming V and L, the installed CITAR the wheel's in the prefix; the helper from latest and from `v<V>`, byte for byte, its `--version` line; a wrong checksum and plain http to a documentation address refused before anything is written; the uninstall, nothing else touched; on a runner only, `--path-route`). It writes a JSON report. (The fix round adds two ps1 checks, run as a script block: 25 on Windows, 28 with the path route.)
  - **package.yml's `install` matrix replaces the moved `installer` job:** five legs, each on the runner its wheel and helper are built on (`windows-latest` with `ps1`, `macos-15`, `macos-15-intel`, `ubuntu-24.04`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm` with `sh`); needs `meta`, `wheels` and `helpers`, runs `!cancelled()` with `meta` passed; prepare, the architecture check (with Python's and curl's versions), `pkg-wheel-<platform>` and `pkg-helper-<platform>`, then `install_check.py "$SCRIPT" ... --under "$RUNNER_TEMP" --path-route`, and `pkg-install-report-<platform>`. check_workflows: 8 workflows, 37 jobs.
  - **`citar/helper_pointer.py`:** `citar worker`'s message names the install scripts' `--worker`/`-Worker` one-liners.
  - **Tests.** `tests/test_install_scripts.py` (48; 55 after the fix round): install.sh in bash (on Windows Git Bash's, found beside git, never `System32\bash.exe`, WSL's) with a temporary HOME and `--no-profile`, against stand-in helpers on a loopback server: every refusal through the hook, nothing made; the floors; the four POSIX names; the checksum; `--version`; the address rules; the uninstall's rules (the links and the service guard on Linux and macOS); the profile line on a runner only. install.ps1 through `gate_run.run_install_ps1` (Windows): the Python refusals, wheels only, the helper, the address and checksum refusals, the uninstall's rules, the PATH unchanged. `gate_run install-ps1`'s refusals and environment on every OS. The scripts' text: one `--only-binary=citar` install each, the helper names equal to `release.py`'s, the floors equal to the wheel tags. `install_check`'s parts. `tests/test_workflows.py` (77, 76 before): the matrix held to P5.8 and the wheels' and helpers' runners, and four plants in it (rules 3, 5, 9, 13). `test_one_protocol`'s allowance for `install.sh`'s `citar-worker` line still matches.
  - **Docs:** the four sentences this changed (INSTALL, WORKERS, the CHANGELOG's 0.1.6 line, KNOWN_ISSUES' install.sh note) say the scripts install the helper; CONTRIBUTING names `gate_run.py install-ps1`, the hook, the tests and `install_check.py`; the wiki is regenerated (never pushed).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **The uninstall removes only what is the script's** (the marker, the links and shims that name the prefix): the old script ran `rm -rf "$PREFIX"` on whatever `--prefix` named and removed four names from the bin folder whatever they were, so a mistyped prefix could have removed a home folder. The prefix is removed whole (the gate's "empty" read as gone), the bin folder kept, emptied of CITAR's files.
  - **`--uninstall` removes the helper too** (gate 1: the bin folder empty), unless it runs as a service; `--uninstall --worker` removes it alone. A service is found by its definition naming the binary, read only: the script never runs `service uninstall` itself.
  - **The scripts never run the helper they install:** `citar-helper --version` of a release build would use the per-user folder; the gates run it through `gate_run.py helper`, and the scripts print the next steps.
  - **`--python`/`-Python`**, so a chosen interpreter is used (the gates', CI's, a machine with several), and the candidates are searched for one that fits before anything is refused.
  - **Beyond the design's list**, a non-CPython or 32-bit Python is named (both would fail at pip), an unknown libc and an x86 Windows for the helper too.
  - **curl only** for `--worker` (the one-line install already needs it): wget cannot refuse a redirect that leaves https.
  - **`--from DIR` picks from the folder alone**, so a folder whose wheel does not fit this machine fails with the no-wheel message rather than pip taking another build of the same version from PyPI.
  - **`gate_run.py` allows a documentation address** (192.0.2.0/24, 198.51.100.0/24, 203.0.113.0/24) as `CITAR_RELEASE_URL`: nothing answers one, so the script's own refusal of plain http to another machine can be tested through the harness without any request leaving.
  - **`--path-route` on CI only:** the runner's profile (a temporary HOME's) and PATH are its own (P5.18.4), so the default route is proven there, including install.ps1's PATH restored byte for byte and its uninstall refusing while a Run value names the helper.
  - **The four doc sentences** are corrected now, not left false until 5-12's pass.
- **What the gates found, and the fixes.**
  - **Gate 1's first run** (`wsl-gate1-first.log`, at `2a35827`) passed its 27 checks. Its S9 step could not import the test modules: `tests/__init__.py` seeds a database, and WSL's system Python has none of the game's dependencies. The script now runs the two modules from `tests/` as top-level modules, which need neither. Its shellcheck pass (not a gate) found SC2206 (a dotted version split by an unquoted expansion) and SC1087 (`$SOURCE[...]`, which a reader takes for an array); `ea373ca` reads the version with `read -a` and writes `${SOURCE}`, with no change in behaviour. The second run, at `ea373ca`, passed whole.
  - **The unit tests' first run** found a test wrong, not the script: `https://` loses its last slash to the base's trimming, so it is refused as not an https address rather than as naming no host; the test now gives `https:///x`.
