- **Fix round** (2026-10-23 from 01:05 to 02:36 (-04:00), one agent, after the review; logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-07/logs/f`, gate 1's as `logs/wsl-f-gate1.log`; the scripts in the session scratchpad's `p507f/`). The review found five issues, two major and three minor. All five were valid and all are fixed, in commits `3dc2d55`, `fd96a7a` and this note.
  1. **install.ps1 ended the user's PowerShell when it ran as a script block** (major). Both documented routes run it that way: `iwr -useb URL | iex` (the header's and packaging/README.md's) and `& ([scriptblock]::Create((iwr -useb URL))) -Worker` (`citar worker`'s message). There `exit` ends the session itself, not the script: a console window closed before the platform, no-wheel or checksum message could be read, and Windows Terminal closed the tab on a success before "Next: citar-helper setup" was read. The gates ran the script only with `-File`, where `exit` is right. Now:
     - `$AsFile = [bool]$PSCommandPath`. Run as a file, `Fail` and the four ends still `exit`. Run as a script block, `Fail` throws `$Stopped` after its message, and the script's flow (from its arguments to its end, indented once) is in `try { } catch { }`: the catch turns `$Stopped` into `$global:LASTEXITCODE = 1` and lets any other error through as PowerShell shows one; an end sets `$LASTEXITCODE` 0 (or the wizard's) and `return`s. The session goes on, quietly. Rehearsed first on Windows PowerShell 5.1 (`probe*.ps1`): `exit` in a script block from `[scriptblock]::Create` or `iex` ends the process; a top-level `return`, and a `throw` from a function inside `foreach` and `switch`, caught at the top, do not; `exit` is never caught by `catch`. (A `trap { return }` also returned quietly on 5.1, but what `return` does in a trap is not documented, so the documented `try`/`catch` was used.)
     - The header gives both one-liners in the script-block form, which takes parameters and keeps the script's `$ErrorActionPreference` out of the user's session (`iex` runs in the caller's scope), and says what `$LASTEXITCODE` means. packaging/README.md's table and INSTALL.md's helper sentence give the same form.
     - **Tested:** `gate_run.py install-ps1 --as-scriptblock` (`run_install_ps1(as_scriptblock=True)`) runs the checkout's script as the one-liner does, `& ([scriptblock]::Create([IO.File]::ReadAllText(<install.ps1>))) ARGS` with each value a single-quoted literal, inside `try`/`catch`, then prints `gate_run: the session continues (install.ps1 as a script block, status N)` and exits N, naming any error the script block let out. It is passed with `-Command`: `-EncodedCommand` makes Windows PowerShell write its output again as CLIXML on stderr. `InstallPs1.test_as_a_script_block_it_ends_without_ending_the_session` runs a refusal (`-Version bad`), a refusal deep in the download (a wrong `.sha256`), `-Worker` and `-Uninstall -Worker` that way, under `powershell` and under `pwsh` where it exists (CI's Windows runner), and asserts the status, the marker, no escaped error and no "CITAR's installer stopped". `install_check.py ps1` adds two checks (25 here, 28 with the path route on a runner): `-Worker` as a script block installs the helper byte for byte, and a wrong `.sha256` there ends the script, not the session, with nothing written. `TheScripts.test_install_ps1_exits_only_when_run_as_a_file` holds every `exit` to `if ($AsFile) { exit ... }` and the header's and packaging/README.md's one-liners to the script-block form.
  2. **gate_run.py install-ps1 checked -Prefix and -BinDir as Python reads them** (major). PowerShell resolves the same text with `GetUnresolvedProviderPathFromPSPath`, which expands a leading `~` to the real home folder and takes a provider's path (`FileSystem::C:\x`) or a drive of its own (`Temp:\x`); from a working folder inside `--under`, `-Prefix ~\citar-p` was accepted and the script would have installed into the home folder. Now `ps1_folder` refuses a value with a leading `~`, a `:` other than a drive letter's, or one that is not absolute (on Windows, `X:\` or `X:/`: a root-relative `\x` and a drive-relative `C:x` are refused), then requires it strictly inside `--under`; and `run_install_ps1` gives PowerShell the arguments rebuilt from what was checked (`ps1_arguments`: each name as the script declares it, `-Prefix` and `-BinDir` the folders resolved), never the text. Tests: the five forms refused from a working folder inside `--under` (and on Windows the root- and drive-relative ones), the arguments rebuilt (`..` resolved, names in their declared case), the script-block command's quoting (an apostrophe and a typographic quote doubled), and, with `start` stubbed, the argv a run gives PowerShell in both forms. The tests now resolve their temporary folders (a runner's `TEMP` is written short, `RUNNER~1`), as the folders passed to the script are.
  3. **The uninstall left the helper's update leftovers** (minor). The updater (`crates/citar-helper/src/update/files.rs`) also leaves `<name>.failed` (a binary rolled back while it ran) and `<name>.old.<pid>` and `<name>.failed.<pid>` (set aside), which only a later start of the helper clears; after an uninstall none comes, and on Windows the bin folder (and its PATH entry), removed only when empty, stayed for good. Both scripts now remove `citar-helper[.exe]`, `.old`, `.failed`, `.old.<digits>`, `.failed.<digits>`, `.citar-helper[.exe].new` and their own half-copied `.citar-helper.install`, and nothing named like them (`.old.bak`, `.failed.x9`, `.older` stay); install.sh does it whether or not the binary is there. The uninstall tests plant all of them (both scripts, and `--uninstall --worker` with no helper).
  4. **install.sh's service guard missed a bin folder reached through a link** (minor). `citar-helper service install` names the path the helper found for itself, which on Linux has every link resolved (`/proc/self/exe`), so with `~/.local/bin` a link (a dotfile manager's) the unit named the resolved path, the guard's `grep -F "$BIN_DIR/citar-helper"` missed it, and `--uninstall` removed the binary under an enabled unit. `helper_service` now looks for both forms, `$BIN_DIR/citar-helper` and `$(cd "$BIN_DIR" && pwd -P)/citar-helper`. `test_the_service_guard_sees_a_bin_folder_through_a_link` (Linux and macOS): exit 1 and the helper kept, then removed once the unit is gone, the link itself kept.
  5. **The test of `http://127.1.2.3` connected to it** (minor). On macOS (test.yml's `macos-latest`) lo0 answers only 127.0.0.1, so the connection would likely wait out curl's 30 s timeout and retries against the test's 120 s. Off Windows the test now puts a stand-in `curl` first on PATH, which records its arguments and fails as an unreachable host does (exit 7), and asserts that the script asked it for `http://127.1.2.3:9/x/latest/download/citar-helper-linux-x64.sha256` (the address let through as this machine) and said "Could not download". On Windows (Git for Windows' bash puts its own folders first on PATH) the real curl runs, and Windows refuses all of 127/8 at once. `localhost:9` and `[::1]:9` still use the real curl everywhere.
  - **Tests:** `test_install_scripts` has 55 tests, from 48. Each new or changed test of a behaviour failed on the code before its fix, run in a copy of the files under the target with `ba45480`'s install.ps1, install.sh or gate_run.py (`red.log` on Windows, `wsl-f-red.log` in WSL), and passed with the fixed ones:
     - the script-block test failed in all four cases, without the marker: the old script's `exit` ended its PowerShell;
     - the uninstall tests (both scripts', with and without the helper, `--uninstall --worker`) listed the leftovers still there;

     - `test_install_ps1_exits_only_when_run_as_a_file` found `Fail`'s bare `exit 1`;
     - the folder tests found `~`, `~\p`, `FileSystem::...`, `Temp:\p`, `p`, `\dev\...` and `C:dev\...` accepted, and the old `run_install_ps1` had no rebuilt arguments to give.
     - The 127.1.2.3 test passes on both (finding 5 is the test's own behaviour on macOS, which no machine here has): what it now proves without a connection is that the stand-in curl was asked for that address.

## P5.9 Package managers

| Manager | Package | What it installs | Manifest |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scoop | `citar` | the game: the portable zip (as 0.1.5) | `packaging/scoop/citar.json` |
| Scoop | `citar-helper` (new) | the packaged helper: `citar-helper-V-windows-x64.zip` | `packaging/scoop/citar-helper.json` |
| winget | `JimmieRodgers.CITAR` | the game: the Inno installer, per user (as 0.1.5) | `packaging/winget/game/` (three files) |
| winget | `JimmieRodgers.CITAR.Helper` (new) | the packaged helper: the zip, `NestedInstallerType: portable`, command alias `citar-helper`, per user | `packaging/winget/helper/` (three files) |
| Homebrew | `citar-helper` (new; the tap's only formula) | the packaged helper for macOS arm64 and x64 and Linux x64 and arm64 (`on_macos`/`on_linux` × `on_arm`/`on_intel`, each its archive's URL and SHA-256); `bin.install "citar-helper"`; the notices into `doc`; a `service` block running the helper in service mode (`brew services start citar-helper` keeps it running through the `opt` path, which survives upgrades, where `citar-helper service install` would point at a Cellar path that the next upgrade removes; the caveats say so); `test do` runs `--version` and `--self-test` | `packaging/homebrew/citar-helper.rb` |

- **The old `citar` formula goes:** `packaging/homebrew/citar.rb` is deleted here, and Phase 6 removes `Formula/citar.rb` from the tap with the tap's README saying why. Nobody else uses it (the owner's own copy is uninstalled with `brew uninstall citar`); the CHANGELOG's Compatibility says how to move (P5.16).
- **Packaged builds only** in package managers: their updates come through the manager (`brew upgrade`, `scoop update`, `winget upgrade`), and the helper refuses `auto`.
- **`scripts/release_checksums.py`** is rewritten around templates (`packaging/**/*.in`, P5.10's names and a `{{version}}`/`{{sha256:<asset>}}`/`{{url:<asset>}}` vocabulary): `--from-dir DIR` hashes built files (CI's route, before anything is published), `--base-url URL` points the manifests elsewhere (a dry run's loopback server), `--out DIR` writes them (`--write` writes into `packaging/` for review), and `--verify V` downloads the published release and checks every asset against `SHA256SUMS` and PyPI's JSON API hashes (Phase 6, after publishing). **`--verify` has a dry-run form:** `--base-url URL` (the dry run's `pkg-release-assets` behind a loopback server) and `--pypi-json FILE` (a file in PyPI's JSON API shape; the tests and 5-13 make one from the built wheels and sdist), so Phase 6 does not run it for the first time. No PyPI sdist URL remains in any manifest, so the 404 lag is gone.
- **Validated in CI** (package.yml's `managers` job, P5.10), each tool found or installed in the job and its version printed:
  - **Windows:** Scoop installed with `-RunAsAdmin` (Windows runners are elevated, and Scoop's installer refuses an elevated shell without it), `scoop install` of both manifests from a loopback server (`citar --version`, `citar-helper --version --home <temp>`); the winget YAML validated against Microsoft's JSON schemas named in each file's header (fetched in the job) with `jsonschema`, which is the gate; `winget validate` and `winget install --manifest` only where the image has winget, otherwise a `::notice::` naming what was not run (never a silent skip).
  - **macOS arm64, macOS x64 and Linux x64:** `brew tap-new --no-git citar/dryrun`; `brew audit --strict` and `brew style` on the formula **with the real release URLs** (offline: the files are not published yet, so no download is checked); `brew install` and `brew test` on a copy pointed at the loopback server. Linux arm64's formula branch is checked by its structure test only.
- **Submission** stays Phase 6's (P5.15): copies into clones of `jprodgers/homebrew-citar` and `jprodgers/scoop-citar`, and `wingetcreate submit` for each winget package. Microsoft has not merged the earlier New-package pull requests; submitting costs minutes, so Phase 6 submits both and records the PR numbers.

## P5.10 Assembling a release

**The asset list** (`release.py assets V`; `P` is V's PEP 440 form; every file but the `.sha256` files and `SHA256SUMS` has a `.sha256` beside it):

| Group | Files |
|---|---|
| Python | `citar-P.tar.gz`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-win_amd64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-macosx_11_0_arm64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-macosx_11_0_x86_64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-manylinux_2_28_x86_64.whl`; `citar-P-cp311-abi3-manylinux_2_28_aarch64.whl`; `citar-P-server-constraints.txt` |
| Windows | `CITAR-V-setup.exe`; `CITAR-V-windows-x64.zip` |
| The helper, self-updating (unversioned, P4.10) | `citar-helper-windows-x64.exe`; `citar-helper-macos-arm64`; `citar-helper-macos-x64`; `citar-helper-linux-x64`; `citar-helper-linux-arm64`; `citar-helper-manifest.json` (and `citar-helper-manifest.json.sig` on a signed tag) |
| The helper, packaged | `citar-helper-V-windows-x64.zip`; `citar-helper-V-macos-arm64.tar.gz`; `citar-helper-V-macos-x64.tar.gz`; `citar-helper-V-linux-x64.tar.gz`; `citar-helper-V-linux-arm64.tar.gz` |
| Notices and sums | `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`; `SHA256SUMS` |

**package.yml's `assemble` job** (needs every build) downloads the `pkg-` artifacts it needs by exact name into `release/` under exactly these names and fails on a missing or an extra file, naming it (`scripts/ci/assemble_release.py`). It then checks that **every artifact is one build**: the five wheels' smoke lines, the ten helpers' `--version` lines, the bundle's and both images' report one version V, one label L, one build id and one jobs build (so the VPS's job host and every released helper can share CPU work, P4.4.5), and the five wheels played the seeded lab job to one result hash; the sdist build's line must report V, the same build id, jobs build and result hash, and the label `unknown` (a user's build has none). It runs `twine check` over the wheels and the sdist, writes `SHA256SUMS`, the **release notes** (`release.py notes V`, then the checksums, `gh attestation verify` instructions and the install routes), the attestation subject list (every file of `release/`), and the package managers' manifests from the files (`release_checksums.py --from-dir release --out managers`, the real release URLs). It uploads `pkg-release-assets` (every file), `pkg-python-dists` (the wheels and the sdist, for PyPI) and `pkg-release-preview` (notes, sums, manifests, the subject list, the asset list with sizes). The `managers` job (P5.9) uses `pkg-release-assets` behind a loopback server.

**As built in 5-08: the package managers and assembling a release** (Appendix F, 5-08). One agent, from 2026-10-23 06:00 to 08:47 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-08` from `8d36c30`.

- **What was built.**
  - **The templates** (`packaging/**/*.in`, held LF by `.gitattributes`): `scoop/citar.json.in` (0.1.5's manifest, its URL and hash now placeholders) and the new `scoop/citar-helper.json.in` (the packaged helper's zip, `bin` `citar-helper.exe`, notes saying Scoop updates it and that a service names this version's folder); `winget/game/` (0.1.5's three files, moved) and the new `winget/helper/` (`JimmieRodgers.CITAR.Helper`: `InstallerType: zip`, `NestedInstallerType: portable`, `citar-helper.exe` with the alias `citar-helper`, `Scope: user`); `homebrew/citar-helper.rb.in` (`class CitarHelper`, no `version` line (Homebrew reads it from the URL), `license`, the four `on_macos`/`on_linux` × `on_arm`/`on_intel` blocks each with its archive's `url` and `sha256`, `bin.install`, the three notices into `doc`, caveats on `citar-helper setup`, `brew services` and why not `service install`, a `service` block running `opt_bin/"citar-helper" run --service` with `keep_alive` and a log under `var`, and a `test` of `--version` and `--self-test`, each with `--home` under `testpath`). `packaging/homebrew/citar.rb` and the five hand-edited 0.1.5 manifests are deleted; the manifests `--write` puts beside the templates are ignored by git.
  - **`scripts/release_checksums.py`**, rewritten (standard library only): the vocabulary `{{version}}`, `{{date}}`, `{{url:NAME}}`, `{{sha256:NAME}}` and `{{SHA256:NAME}}` (upper case, winget's), `NAME` read after `{{version}}` is filled in and refused, with its template and line, unless it is one of `release.py assets V`; any other `{{…}}` refused the same way. `V --from-dir DIR` (hashes of the files), or without it the `SHA256SUMS` at the base URL; `--base-url` (https, or http to a loopback address; no user name, query or fragment); `--out DIR` or `--write`; `--date` (else V's CHANGELOG section's date); `--write-pypi-json FILE` (with `--from-dir`: the wheels and sdist in the shape of PyPI's JSON API); `--verify V [--base-url] [--pypi-json FILE]`, which reads `SHA256SUMS` (signed or not, by whether it lists the manifest's `.sig`), requires it to list exactly the release's assets, downloads every asset (hashed as it streams) and its `.sha256`, and requires PyPI to hold exactly the release's wheels and sdist with the release's hashes, every problem naming its file.
  - **`scripts/ci/assemble_release.py`** (standard library only): `assemble` (P5.10's steps: the folder's set, the reports' one build, the `.sha256` files the builds do not write, `SHA256SUMS`, the final set, the preview) and `sums --signed` (P5.10's last two steps over a folder that also holds the signature, for release.yml).
  - **`scripts/ci/check_managers.py`**: `structure` (every manifest of `MANIFESTS` there and no other; Scoop's keys, URL and hash; each winget file's first line its schema header, its type and version the header's, the fields its type requires, the package's identifier and version, `Documentations` and no `Documentation`, the installer's URL and SHA-256, the helper's portable nesting; the formula's four blocks, each one URL and one SHA-256 of its platform's archive, and its install, notices, service and test; no PyPI URL; no placeholder left; with `--assets` every hash the file's), `winget-schemas` (each winget file against the JSON schema its header names, fetched from that URL or read from `--schemas`, the YAML read with dates kept as strings, as winget reads them against the schema), and `scoop`, `winget` and `brew`, which install packages and refuse to run unless `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`.
  - **package.yml** (8 workflows, 39 jobs): `assemble` needs every other job, downloads 20 artifacts into `release/` and 24 into `reports/` by exact name, runs `assemble_release.py assemble` and `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS`, uploads `pkg-release-assets` (the folder) and `pkg-python-dists` (the sdist and `citar-P-cp311-abi3-*.whl` only), both at `compression-level: 0`, and `pkg-release-preview`, and only then installs twine and PyYAML (wheels only) for `check_managers.py structure --assets release` and `twine check` over the sdist and the five wheels (the fix round's order, below); time limit 45 minutes. `managers` (needs `meta` and `assemble`) on `windows-latest`, `macos-15`, `macos-15-intel` and `ubuntu-24.04`, each the runner its platform's wheel is built on: on Windows, Scoop's installer from `ScoopInstaller/Install` at `1e2f3340…` (2026-08-28), run with `-RunAsAdmin` only once its SHA-256 is `94f983b1…`, the shims folder added to the path, then `check_managers.py scoop`, `winget-schemas` and `winget`; elsewhere `check_managers.py brew`; each leg uploads `pkg-managers-report-<platform>`. The `windows` job's installer step now takes Inno Setup's version from ISCC's own "Compiler engine version" line and warns when it is not `INNO_SETUP_VERSION` (5-05's merge saw ISCC.exe's version resource read 0.0.0.0).
  - **Docs:** `packaging/README.md` rewritten (the routes as package.yml builds and checks them, the order of a release, the templates and the commands, Homebrew's helper-only tap, Scoop's and winget's two packages, the helper's ten builds, code signing); a CONTRIBUTING paragraph (the wiki regenerated, never pushed).
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_release_assembly.py` (38: assemble on a fake release, each fault named, the preview, `sums --signed`; the templates and the vocabulary; the manifests from `--from-dir`, from the published sums and with a loopback base, with seven structure plants; the schemas' path with a stand-in schema; `--verify` against a loopback copy, with a changed byte, a PyPI hash, PyPI's missing and extra files, the sums' missing and extra lines, an asset gone and a signed release; the managers refused off a runner). `tests/test_workflows.py` (79, 77 before): `ThePackageWorkflow` holds `assemble` (its needs, its 44 downloads, each uploaded by a job of this workflow on every run, its steps and its three uploads) and `managers` (its legs and runners, its downloads, the pinned installer checked before it runs, which steps run where, its reports), and eight plants in the real file (rules 2, 3, 9 and 13 in both jobs).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **More than one build checked.** Beyond the wheels, helpers, bundle and images, `assemble` reads the five install scripts' reports (every check passed; `citar doctor`'s and the installed helper's build, label and version) and the images' smoke lines (their result hash and spec hash with the wheels'); it holds the spec hash as well as the result hash, the helpers to one protocol, each smoke line to its platform, interpreter and the release's own wheel, and each helper report's two SHA-256s to the very files released, so what is published is what was tested. `assemble` therefore needs every job, the checks included: a failed check leaves nothing to assemble.
  - **The one value** of a build id, jobs build, protocol, result or spec hash is the most common across the reports; every report saying another is named with both values, so one stray helper names that helper, not the other twenty-three reports.
  - **The reports' set is exact** (the 24 the job downloads, and the bundle's two companions), so a report renamed or added by a later package fails `assemble` until it is read here.
  - **The manifests are written twice from one generator:** in `assemble` with the real URLs (the preview, and what Phase 6 copies), and in `managers` pointed at the loopback server; gate 2 checks the two `--from-dir` routes give the preview's bytes.
  - **Where each check runs:** `structure` in `assemble` (one platform suffices, and it fails before the managers start); the JSON schemas on the Windows leg, fetched from the URL in each file's header (P5.9), never vendored; `winget validate` and `install` only where `winget` is on the path, else a `::notice::`.
  - **Scoop's installer is pinned** by commit and SHA-256, as rustup-init is (P5.4.1); Scoop itself then comes from its default branch, as a user's does.
  - **winget's `ManifestVersion` stays 1.12.0:** 1.28.0's schemas exist, but current submissions to `winget-pkgs` still use 1.12.0 (Git.Git 2.55.0.5's, read on 2026-10-23), and the README says to move the headers and `ManifestVersion` together when they do not.
  - **The formula's service has no `--home`:** `brew services` runs as the user, whose default home is the one `citar-helper setup` writes (`~/Library/Application Support/CITAR`, `~/.local/share/citar`).
  - **`sums --signed`** is for 5-09: release.yml's signature joins the folder after `assemble`, and the sums and the notes' checksum block must be rewritten with it before `attest-files` attests them.
- **Gates** (logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-08/logs`; the scripts in the session's scratchpad `p508/`):

- **Fix round** (2026-10-23 09:05 to 09:54, -04:00). The review found three minor gaps. Each was confirmed and is fixed:

## P5.11 release.yml end to end

**The Phase 5 gate goes.** release.yml keeps its triggers (`push` of `v*` tags; `workflow_dispatch` with `version`), workflow permissions `contents: read`, and gains `concurrency: release-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: false` (a release is never cut off mid-publish). **It builds nothing itself**: every build is package.yml's, every test test.yml's, both called, so a release can contain nothing a pull request did not build and test. The two calling jobs pass **no `secrets:`** (never `secrets: inherit`, which would hand every called job the repository's secrets, `WIKI_TOKEN` among them) and set `permissions: contents: read` (rule 12).

**The tag guard.** Every job that publishes, names an environment, holds a secret or writes anywhere has the job-level condition `github.event_name == 'push' && startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')` (a dispatch on a tag ref is a dry run too), never under `always()` or `!cancelled()`. `check_workflows.py` proves it before any dispatch (P5.17).

**Environments by construction.** A job's `if:` is evaluated at run time, and whether GitHub creates a missing environment for a job it then skips is platform behaviour nobody here can check without risking exactly that. So every environment in release.yml except `pypi` (which exists, and which PyPI's trusted publisher names) is written as the tag expression, `environment: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v') && 'helper-signing' || '' }}`: on a dispatch it is empty and names nothing, whatever the `if:` does. `check_workflows.py` requires that form (rule 14), with a planted literal `environment: helper-signing` that must fail. **If Actions refuses an empty environment name** (the first dry run, 5-09, shows it as a startup failure, which names nothing and creates nothing), the fallback is to split the paths: release.yml keeps only `push: tags` (so the publishing code stays where PyPI's trusted publisher expects it), and a dispatch-only `release-dry-run.yml` calls the same test.yml, package.yml and summary and names no environment at all; the dispatch command in P5.18.2 then names that file.

**Nothing in a job that holds power installs or builds.** A job that reads a secret other than `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or holds `id-token: write` or any write permission, runs no cargo, pip, npm, maturin or `docker build`, and its `uses:` are on rule 15's allow-list (the pinned checkout, download and upload actions, and the publishing actions the job exists for); its checkouts set `persist-credentials: false` (rule 15). A step in any workflow that reads such a secret runs no package tool and is not a `uses:` step. So the PyPI job does no `twine check` (assemble did) and the signing job runs a prebuilt binary (P5.4.2).

| Job | Needs | On a tag | On a dry run | Writes / holds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `meta` | | `release.py meta` with the tag; `prepare --mode tag` (the tag, the source and the changelog agree) | `meta` with the input; `prepare --mode dry-run` | nothing |
| `tests` (calls test.yml) | `meta` | yes | yes | nothing |
| `package` (calls package.yml) | `meta` | yes | yes | nothing |
| `sign-helper-manifest` | `tests`, `package` | yes: environment by the tag expression, `helper-signing` (its reviewer approves); P5.4.2's four rules by the prebuilt xtask; uploads `rel-helper-signature` | skipped | the secret |
| `check-signature` | `sign-helper-manifest` | yes: `verify_manifest.py` (`cryptography` installed from hashes) against each committed key line; with an empty `update-keys.pub`, that no `.sig` exists | skipped | nothing |
| `attest-files` | `check-signature` | yes: `actions/attest-build-provenance` over every file of `pkg-release-assets` and the signature (the subject list `assemble` wrote). It publishes provenance only, so a first-run failure here stops the release before anything irreversible | skipped | `id-token`, `attestations: write` |
| `publish-pypi` | `attest-files` | yes: environment `pypi`, `pkg-python-dists`, trusted publishing (`skip-existing`, so a re-run after a partial upload completes it; PyPI's own attestations on) | skipped | PyPI (OIDC) |
| `publish-image` | `attest-files` | yes: loads `pkg-image-amd64` and `pkg-image-arm64`, pushes `:V-amd64` and `:V-arm64` and joins them as `:V` (`docker buildx imagetools create`); no `:latest` and no `:<major.minor>` yet | skipped | `packages: write` |
| `attest-image` | `publish-image` | yes: provenance for the `:V` manifest-list digest, `push-to-registry`, after its own ghcr login | skipped | `packages: write`, `id-token`, `attestations: write` |
| `github-release` | `publish-pypi`, `attest-image` | yes: a **draft** with every asset and the signature, the list compared with `release.py assets V`, the notes from `pkg-release-preview`; then published, and marked latest unless a prerelease | skipped | `contents: write` |
| `image-tags` | `github-release` | yes: `:<major.minor>` and (not for a prerelease) `:latest` pointed at `:V`'s digest | skipped | `packages: write` |
| `dry-run-summary` | `tests`, `package` | skipped | yes: the job summary: V, L, the asset list with sizes and hashes, the attestation subjects, the image tarballs loaded and their architectures, what each tag-only job would do and with which environment | nothing |

- **Order on a tag:** nothing becomes public until the signature passes its rules and both verifiers, and nothing irreversible happens until the files' provenance is made: a signing failure (the wrong key, a missing public key) or an attestation failure stops the release with PyPI, the registry and the release page untouched. Then PyPI and the image's version tags; then the image's provenance; then the GitHub release, published last and as a whole, so `releases/latest/download/<helper>` never points at a release that lacks a helper; then the moving image tags, so `:latest` never names an image whose release does not exist. `check_workflows.py` holds this `needs` graph and each job's permissions (rule 16), and RELEASING.md says which job to re-run after a failure at each point (P5.15).
- **PyPI's trusted publisher** names release.yml and `pypi`, so publishing stays in release.yml (a reusable workflow cannot publish under it).
- **Prereleases** (`-rc.N` and the like) publish everything but are never `latest` on GitHub or in the registry.
- **The notes** are the CHANGELOG section (P5.2) with the checksums and verification lines (P5.10).

## P5.12 Attestations

- **Made only for a tagged release**, in two jobs: `attest-files` (every release file and the signature) **before** anything is published, and `attest-image` (the `:V` manifest-list digest, pushed to the registry) after the image's push, with `packages: write` and its own ghcr login, by `actions/attest-build-provenance` (pinned) with the run's OIDC identity. Attestations of a public repository go to Sigstore's public transparency log, which is publishing: **a dry run makes none.** It proves the inputs instead: `assemble` computes the subject list (every file of `pkg-release-assets`), `dry-run-summary` shows it, and `check_workflows.py` holds each attest job's permissions and that its `subject-path` covers the folder the asset list is downloaded into (rule 16).
- **PyPI** adds its own attestations (PEP 740) through trusted publishing.
- **Verification** is documented (INSTALL.md, the release notes) as `gh attestation verify <file> --repo jprodgers/CITAR`, and Phase 6's deploy bundle runs it on the wheel before anything reaches the VPS (P5.13.5).

**As built in 5-09: release.yml end to end, the checklist, the first dry run** (Appendix F, 5-09; P5.11, P5.12 and P5.15). One agent, from 2026-10-23 15:38 to 17:40 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-09` from `c724ff0`; its fix round from 18:33 (below, "Fix round").

- **What was built.**
  - **`release.yml`** (12 jobs; the gate is gone): `meta` (a pushed `v*` tag is mode `tag` with the tag's version, refused unless the push is a `v*` tag; a dispatch is mode `dry-run` with its input, on any ref; `release.py meta`, then `prepare` with meta's own date); `tests` and `package` calling test.yml and package.yml with `mode`, `version`, `date` (and `label`), `permissions: contents: read`, no `secrets:`; then, each with the tag guard as its job-level `if:`, `sign-helper-manifest` (environment the tag expression; the checkout without credentials, `pkg-helper-manifest` and `pkg-xtask-linux-x64` by name, one step whose only `env:` is `CITAR_UPDATE_SIGNING_KEY` running `python3 scripts/ci/release_sign.py sign` on the runner's Python, and the upload of `rel-helper-signature`), `check-signature`, `attest-files` (`id-token`, `attestations`), `publish-pypi` (environment `pypi`, `id-token`; `pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish` v1.14.3, `skip-existing`), `publish-image` (`packages`), `attest-image` (`packages`, `id-token`, `attestations`), `github-release` (`contents`) and `image-tags` (`packages`); and the dispatch-only `dry-run-summary`. `concurrency: release-${{ github.ref }}`, never cancelled. Two new pinned actions, by `action_pins.py pin`: `actions/attest-build-provenance` v4.2.2 and `pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish` v1.14.3 (14 pins, 0 problems; 235 references at the code's head, `85d9e41`: the 234 S8 counted below were read before the last two release.yml commits, as the review found).
  - **`scripts/ci/release_sign.py`** (standard library): `sign` checks the xtask binary against the SHA-256 beside it, reads `update-keys.pub` as citar-jobs does (blank and `#` lines past, each line a public key's form, at most two distinct, a line never echoed), and gives P5.4.2's four outcomes: the notice (`::notice` on a runner), "commit the public key first", "would trust a key no release signs with", or `xtask sign-manifest --key-env` then `xtask verify-manifest --pub` against the committed lines, refused as "the secret is not the committed key" (the signature removed) unless it verifies. It writes `signing.json` either way and the `.sig` when signed. `expect` holds a run's signature to the committed key file: present exactly when a key is committed, then copied into the release folder.
  - **`scripts/ci/assemble_release.py`**: `sums --out DIR` (the sums, `notes.md` with them, and the signature with its `.sha256` when signed), `final` (copies them into a fresh download of `pkg-release-assets`, then the folder must be exactly `release.py assets V [--signed]`, each `.sha256` its file's and `SHA256SUMS` their sums; `--list` writes the upload list), and `summary` (the dry run's summary; `TAG_ONLY_JOBS` holds each tag-only job's environment and what it would do). **`scripts/ci/check_image.py load`**: the file's SHA-256 and the loaded image's id, architecture and version held to its report before `publish-image` pushes it (and, since the fix round, the report's image to the tag pushed, `--image`).
  - **`scripts/ci/check_workflows.py` rule 16** (`RELEASE_JOBS`, P5.11's table): exactly the table's jobs; each one's direct needs (`meta` besides where it reads its outputs); the tag guard on the tag-only jobs, `github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'` on `dry-run-summary`, no condition on `meta`, `tests` and `package`; each job's write permissions exactly the table's; `helper-signing` by the tag expression on the signing job and `pypi` on `publish-pypi`, no other environment; one secret in release.yml, `CITAR_UPDATE_SIGNING_KEY`, read in one run step whose `env:` holds it alone and which runs `release_sign.py sign`; `gh release` writes in `github-release` alone, every `create` with `--draft`, `--draft=false` only after every create and upload; image pushes in `publish-image` and `image-tags` alone, `publish-image`'s naming the version and never `latest` (made exact in the fix round); `attest-files` attesting the folder its `pkg-release-assets` download fills, after `assemble_release.py final --release` that folder; `attest-image` attesting `publish-image`'s digest with `push-to-registry`; the concurrency.
  - **`scripts/ci/verify-manifest-requirements.txt`**: the independent verifier's dependency, hash-pinned (`cryptography` 50.0.2, `cffi` 2.1.1, `pycparser` 3.11, each the SHA-256 of every wheel PyPI has of that version), installed with `--require-hashes --only-binary :all:`.
  - **`scripts/release/managers.sh V [--plan | --push] [--manifests DIR] [--tap URL] [--bucket URL] [--keep]`**: the manifests checked first (present, V's URLs and version, no placeholder; else nothing is cloned), then the tap and the bucket cloned into a temporary folder of its own, `Formula/citar-helper.rb` copied in and `Formula/citar.rb` removed, `bucket/citar.json` and `bucket/citar-helper.json` copied in, the staged diff shown whole; `--plan` (the default) pushes nothing, `--push` commits "CITAR V" and pushes each repository that changed; winget's commands printed.
  - **`packaging/RELEASING.md`** (P5.15's ten steps, each a command, with the jobs' table, the re-run table and how to delete and re-make a tag before anything is public); CONTRIBUTING's "Releasing" and `packaging/README.md` point at it; the wiki's Contributing is regenerated (never pushed).
  - **Tests**: `tests/test_release_sign.py` (20: the four states with xtask stood in, `expect`, the binary's hash, usage, the script standard-library-only and never naming cargo; and with `CITAR_TEST_XTASK` the real binary's eight, a failing `cargo` first on `PATH`), `tests/test_managers_script.py` (5, against local bare stand-ins with git's own configuration set aside), `tests/test_release_assembly.py` (+8, `TheTagOnlyJobsFiles`), `tests/test_image.py` (+2, `CheckImageLoad`), `tests/test_workflows.py` (+24: `TheReleaseWorkflow`, and `ReleasePlanted` with gate 1's plants and rule 16's others).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **The sums are written once, where nothing is held.** The signature joins the release after `assemble`, so `SHA256SUMS` and the notes' checksum block change on a signed tag. `check-signature` (no secret, no write permission) writes them (`sums --out`) and uploads them as `rel-release-sums` before anything from PyPI runs there; `attest-files` and `github-release` only copy them into their download (`final`), so the bytes attested are the bytes published. `check-signature` then installs the verifier's `cryptography` from hash-pinned wheels into a venv of its own.
  - **`rel-helper-signature` is uploaded on every tag**, with `signing.json`, and the `.sig` only when signed, so "nothing was signed" and "nothing arrived" differ; `check-signature` holds the signature to the committed key file whatever the signer reported (`release_sign.py expect`).
  - **`github-release` uses the `gh` CLI**, not a third-party action: `create --draft --verify-tag` with every file and the notes, or, on a re-run, `upload --clobber` into its draft; the release's files compared by name and size with `final --list`; then `edit --draft=false` with `--latest` (or `--latest=false` for a prerelease). A release already published is left as it is.
  - **The registry login is `docker login --password-stdin` with `GITHUB_TOKEN`** in each of the three jobs that push, not `docker/login-action`: no third-party code where `packages: write` is held. `attest-image` sets `create-storage-record: false` (a storage record needs an organization's repository).
  - **A prerelease moves neither moving tag.** P5.11's table writes `:<major.minor>` and "(not for a prerelease) `:latest`"; `image-tags` moves neither for a prerelease, so `:0.1` never names a release candidate (P5.11's "never latest in the registry" read as covering the series tag). Recorded as a deviation.
  - **`image-tags` needs `publish-image` as well as `github-release`** (its digest output; GitHub gives a job only its direct needs' outputs), and jobs reading meta's values need `meta`; rule 16 allows `meta` beside any job's needs.
  - **`dry-run-summary` rehearses the tag-only file steps** on the run's real files (`sums --out`, `final --list`, the list against `release.py assets V`, the sums and notes against the preview's byte for byte) and loads both image tarballs as `publish-image` does (`check_image.py load`); it first runs `release.py prepare` with meta's date, since its checkout is the branch at the old version (found by this package's own rehearsal: the unbumped checkout has no CHANGELOG section for V, so the notes could not be written). It uploads `rel-dry-run-summary`.
  - **Rule 16's job set is exact**, so a job planted in release.yml by the older rules' tests is also named by rule 16; those tests set rule 16 aside where they add a job outside the table (`assertPasses(tree, besides=(16,))`), and the plants that went after the old gate's `exit 1` now go after `meta`'s last step.
  - **`managers.sh` shows the staged diff and commits only with `--push`**; it never runs against the real repositories in a test (the defaults are read from its text).
  - **RELEASING.md step 1 carries the whole signing-key setup** (keygen, the environment with its required reviewer, the secret, the public key in the bump's pull request, and the checks), so the owner's one-time setup is one list.
- **Gates** (logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-09/logs`; the scripts in the session's scratchpad `p509/`):
  1. **`check_workflows.py`** (`gate1/gate1.log`, the command line on copies of the workflows, at 16:14, before `7418757` and `85d9e41` changed release.yml; the fix round ran it again at its head): the tree passes, 8 workflows and 50 jobs, rules 1 to 16 hold. Each of the gate's eleven plants in release.yml exits 1 naming its job and rule: `publish-pypi` guarded by `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/')` alone (rule 2, and rule 16's tag-only check); `publish-pypi` not needing `attest-files` (16); `attest-files` after `publish-pypi` (16, on both); `:latest` pushed in `publish-image` (16); `attest-image` without `packages: write` (16); a `docker build` step (8, and 15 in a job holding `packages`); `github-release` without `--draft` (16); the signing job with a `cargo` step (15 and 8), a `setup-python` step (15) and a checkout keeping its credentials (15); a calling job with `secrets: inherit` (12). `tests/test_workflows.py`'s `ReleasePlanted` repeats them and plants rule 16's other parts: a job outside the table and one missing, the conditions, the environments, permissions beyond the table, the secret at the job's level, beside another variable or in another job, the attested folder and its order, the concurrency.

No `citar-managers-test-*` folder was left after this round's S1.
  - **The cargo trap on Windows.** A program started without a shell on Windows (Rust's `Command::new("cargo")` among them) looks for `cargo.exe` alone, so the trap's `cargo.cmd` caught only what a shell starts (reproduced: with the trap first on `PATH`, `subprocess.run(["cargo", "--version"])` ran the real cargo 1.98.1). `trapped()` now also names the stand-in in `CARGO` (xtask runs `$CARGO` before a bare `cargo`) and sets `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` to a toolchain that is not installed, with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0`, so rustup's `cargo.exe` proxy refuses to run ("toolchain 'citar-trap-no-such-toolchain' is not installed"); each run asserts that neither mark appears, and `test_the_trap_is_set` holds all three ways: without a shell, through one, and by `CARGO`.
  - **The image's name, held across the two workflows.** Nothing tied release.yml's `IMAGE_NAME` to package.yml's image job's `TAG`, and `check_image.py load` checked only the tag's ending, so a renamed image would have passed every check and the dry run and failed only at the tag's `docker push`, when `publish-pypi` (beside it, after `attest-files`) may already be public. `load` now takes `--image`, the tag pushed, and refuses a report that names another; `publish-image` and `dry-run-summary` both pass `--image "$IMAGE_NAME:$VERSION-$arch"`, so the dry run proves the exact name the tag pushes. `TheReleaseWorkflow.test_the_image_pushed_is_the_one_package_yml_tags` holds `IMAGE_NAME` with meta's version and the architecture equal to package.yml's `TAG`, and `CheckImageLoad` plants another repository on either side.
  - **Rule 16's pushes.** `image_refs` knew only a bare `docker push`, and any tag holding `${VERSION` counted as the version. It now reads `push`, `image push` and `manifest push` of docker, podman and buildah, after global options (`docker --config DIR push`), past a push's own options and their values, with podman's destination. In `publish-image` every tag must be exactly `:$VERSION` or `:$VERSION-<arch>` (so `${VERSION%.*}`, `${VERSION}-rc` and a repository without a tag are refused); every repository `publish-image` and `image-tags` push must be `$IMAGE_NAME`; `publish-image`'s `VERSION` must be meta's output, set at the job's level alone (no step's `env:`, assignment, `export`, `read` or `for` sets it or `IMAGE_NAME` again); and other registry tools (`skopeo`, `crane`, `oras`, `regctl` and the like) are refused anywhere in release.yml. `ReleasePlanted` plants each form, the review's two among them; on the old rule they gave 11 failures.
  - **RELEASING.md step 1**, and WORKERS.md's "Signing helper updates" to match (the wiki regenerated, never pushed): the key folder as `"$HOME/citar-update-key"`, which Git Bash and PowerShell both expand (Windows PowerShell 5.1 passes a `~` to a program as it is, which named a folder inside the repository, refused), and `%USERPROFILE%` in cmd; `helper-signing` made before the first tag (a tag's run names it, and GitHub makes a missing environment by itself, unprotected) and limited to `v*` tags under "Deployment branches and tags" besides its required reviewer; the hand check signs a copy of the test manifest in a scratch folder, never `tests/data/helper_manifest/`, whose `.sig` the tests hold byte for byte.
  - **RELEASING.md step 2** stages by name: `git status --short`, `git add -u -- . ':!.claude'`, `git commit -m`, never `commit -a`, which would carry a checkout's own local changes (the owner's `.claude/launch.json`) into the release.
  - **Gates rerun** at `b23df1b`, the code's head (logs `fix-*` under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-09/logs`; the scripts in the scratchpad's `p509/`, gate 1's with the review's two plants added). Gate 1: the tree passes (8 workflows, 50 jobs, rules 1 to 16), and each of 13 plants exits 1 naming its job and rule. Gate 2: `test_release_sign` 20 of 20 on Windows with the real xtask (the trap now holding without a shell) and in WSL. Gate 3: the WSL rehearsal of release.yml's steps, 61 checks, 0 failures, now with `publish-image`'s load refusing an `IMAGE_NAME` apart from the report's; `managers.sh 0.1.6 --plan` against the stand-ins showed the diff and pushed nothing. S1 (3.14.6, so S1c): 2,249 run, OK, 11 skipped, first run (2,245 and the round's four new tests). S1b (3.12.10, `TZ=UTC0`): 2,249 OK, 11 skipped, first run. ## P5.13 The deploy from the release wheel

### P5.13.1 The layout on the server

```
/opt/citar/releases/<pep440>/venv/            a venv per release, root-owned, read-only to the service
/opt/citar/releases/<pep440>/wheel, freeze.txt, SHA256SUMS line
/opt/citar/current -> releases/<pep440>       the release that runs (switched atomically)
/var/lib/citar/                               all state (citar:citar 0750); the units' working directory
/etc/citar/citar.env                          configuration and secrets (root:citar 0640), unchanged
/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf                 the other sites' URLs, one a line (made on the box, never tracked)
/usr/local/sbin/citar-deploy                  the deploy script (installed by itself)
/usr/local/bin/citar-admin                    the admin wrapper
/var/log/citar-deploy.log                     one line a deploy: version, time, previous, backup
```

The units run console scripts from `/opt/citar/current/venv/bin` with `WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/citar`; a console script puts its own `bin/` folder, not the working directory, first on `sys.path`, and the job host starts with `python -P -m` (P5.6), so neither a source tree nor the service-writable state directory is ever on the import path (P2.9). `install.sh --server` and `citar setup --server` use the same layout.

### P5.13.2 The units and wrappers (`deploy/`)

- **`citar.service`:** `ExecStart=/opt/citar/current/venv/bin/citar serve` (settings read `CITAR_HOST` and `CITAR_PORT` from the env file, `citar/settings.py:265-266`), `WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/citar`, the sandbox as today, and the priorities 0.1.5's lab unit had and 0.1.6 lost (P5.0.9): **`CPUWeight=` and `IOWeight=`** in that unit's range (20 to 50; 5-10a starts at 50, and 5-11's measurement settles it, so the web server is not starved while the other sites still win the CPU under contention), `OOMScoreAdjust=500` as today. `MemoryMax`/`MemoryHigh` come from the staging measurement under one rule: **the server's measured peak plus the job host's memory figure stays under `MemoryHigh`** with 40 MB to spare (`MemoryHigh` throttles the whole cgroup, the web server included, so sizing against `MemoryMax` would throttle the server whenever a lab game ran); kept at 400M/320M unless the measurement needs more; the measured peaks and the rule are recorded beside them. The wizard renders this file's directives (a test holds the two equal), so there is one definition. Running the job host in a delegated child cgroup (`Delegate=`, its own `cpu.max`, `cpu.weight` 20 and `memory.max`) would make its CPU share hard; it is the fallback if 5-11's response-time gate fails, after checking how systemd 255 combines `Delegate=` with `ProtectControlGroups=yes` (which makes `/sys/fs/cgroup` read-only to the unit).
- **No `citar-lab` unit.** The lab's job host is a child of the server, inside its `MemoryMax`, under the host machine's CPU settings; Phase 6 sets those from the staging measurement after the fresh database exists (P5.15).
- **`citar-backup.sh`:** the database by `sqlite3 .backup` with its integrity check (as today), plus **the whole state directory** as a tarball without the live database files, the env file, and the release that wrote them (`readlink /opt/citar/current`); pruning after the new backup, as today.
- **`citar-admin.sh`** (tracked; installed as `/usr/local/bin/citar-admin`): `systemd-run --pty --wait --collect --quiet` as `citar` with the unit's `EnvironmentFile`, `WorkingDirectory` and file-system sandbox, so an admin command parses the env file exactly as the service does and can write nothing the service cannot. Like `citar-deploy`, it passes `--slice=$CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` to every `systemd-run` when that variable is set (staging sets it, P5.14; unset on the VPS).
- **`citar-secrets.sh`**, **`nginx-citar.conf`** unchanged in substance; the deploy never rewrites an installed nginx site (certbot edited it) and shows a difference instead.

**As built in 5-10a: the server's release layout: units, backup, wrappers** (Appendix F, 5-10a; P5.13.1 and P5.13.2). One agent, from 2026-10-23 23:13 to 2026-10-24 00:37 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-10a` from `39efe13`; its fix round (one agent, the review's eight findings, all taken) from 00:52 to 02:31.

- **What was built.**
  - **`deploy/citar.service`:** `ExecStart=/opt/citar/current/venv/bin/citar serve`, `WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/citar`, `Environment=CITAR_STATE_DIR=/var/lib/citar` before `EnvironmentFile=/etc/citar/citar.env` (a default the env file may override, so a server whose env file names no state directory still keeps it here and not under the account's home), `CPUWeight=50` and `IOWeight=50`, `MemoryMax=400M`/`MemoryHigh=320M`/`OOMScoreAdjust=500` with the rule written beside them (the server's measured peak plus the job host's figure under `MemoryHigh` with 40 MB to spare; the peaks are 5-11's to record there), the sandbox as before.
  - **`deploy/citar-backup.sh`:** the database by `sqlite3 .backup` and `PRAGMA integrity_check`; `state.tar.gz`, the whole state directory without the live database files (`citar.db`, `-wal`, `-shm`, `-journal`, anchored, so a `citar.db` elsewhere in the tree is kept; the backup folder too if it was put inside); `citar.env` (0600); and `RELEASE` (`made`, `release` = `readlink /opt/citar/current`, `version`, `state`, `data`, `saves`, `config`, `benchmarks`, `archives`, `database`, `revision` = the snapshot's `alembic_version`). The directories and the database are found as the service finds them (`citar/paths.py`, `citar/settings.py`): the env file's `CITAR_STATE_DIR`, `CITAR_DATA_DIR`, `CITAR_SAVE_DIR`, `CITAR_CONFIG_DIR`, `CITAR_BENCH_DIR` and `CITAR_DB_URL`, read as systemd 255 reads them (probed: the last assignment wins, whitespace around the key and the `=` is dropped, comment lines and `export KEY=` are not assignments, one pair of quotes comes off), a relative path taken from the unit's working directory, else the unit's defaults; a directory moved outside the state directory is archived as its own `data.tar.gz`, `saves.tar.gz`, `config.tar.gz` or `benchmarks.tar.gz` (one not made yet holds nothing); a database that is not SQLite is named (`database=external (<scheme>)`, no credentials) and the rest backed up. Anything that fails (no sqlite3, `.backup` failing, the integrity check, tar's fatal exit, the env file's copy, no state directory) is written to `UNTRUSTWORTHY`; such a backup prunes nothing and the script exits 2, so `systemctl start citar-backup` fails. A failed offsite copy (`CITAR_BACKUP_RSYNC_TARGET`) exits 3 after a whole, checked backup; `backup complete: <folder>` is the last line of exit 0 alone. Folders are named by UTC time, with `-N` for a second one in the same second. Run as root, the `-wal` and `-shm` files sqlite3 may make beside the database (the service stopped, as citar-deploy takes it) are given to the database's owner, or the service could not open it again. `CITAR_DEPLOY_ROOT` (tests only) prefixes every path. `citar-backup.service`: the description and a documentation link that exist after a wheel install.
  - **`deploy/citar-admin.sh`** (new; installed as `/usr/local/bin/citar-admin`): `systemd-run --quiet --wait --collect --pty|--pipe --service-type=exec --expand-environment=no [--slice=$CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE]` with `-p` for the unit's `User`, `Group`, `WorkingDirectory`, `Environment`, `EnvironmentFile` and every directive of its file-system and privilege sandbox, then `-- /opt/citar/current/venv/bin/citar-admin ARGS`. `--plan` prints that command and runs nothing; otherwise it needs root, systemd-run, the env file and the program, each refusal naming what is missing. `CITAR_DEPLOY_ROOT` prefixes its paths and lifts the root check (tests, and 5-10b's stubs).
  - **`deploy/citar-secrets.sh`:** shellcheck's three findings only (a quoted `$SERVICE`, `[:upper:]`/`[:lower:]`, a directive on the `select` variable).
  - **`citar/wizard/units.py`** and **`citar/wizard/deploy/`**: the package carries a byte-for-byte copy of `citar.service`, `citar-admin.sh`, `citar-backup.sh`, `citar-backup.service` and `citar-backup.timer` (a wheel carries nothing from outside `citar/`; a test holds the copies equal); `units.py` renders them for this server: `set_directives` replaces named directives in a section, each required exactly once (a renamed directive in deploy/ is an error, not a unit written without it), and `set_variables` the scripts' `ENV_FILE`, `STATE_DIR`, `PROGRAM` and `CURRENT` lines. With the layout's own paths it returns deploy/'s files unchanged. `this_install()` finds the commands: in the release layout (`<root>/releases/<v>/venv` with `<root>/current` resolving to that release) `<root>/current/venv/bin/citar` and `citar-admin`, so the unit and the wrapper follow a switch; else this venv's console scripts (or `python -P -m citar`, and no wrapper without a `citar-admin`). A path with a space, a quote, `$`, `%` or a backslash is refused.
  - **`citar setup --server`** (`citar/wizard/server.py`): its own unit (P5.0.9's second definition) is gone; `_install_systemd` writes the rendered unit, `citar-admin`, `citar-backup` and the backup's two units, each whole and renamed into place (never written through a link; install.sh linked `citar-admin` to the venv's own command before 0.1.6), makes the state directory `citar:citar 0750`, and enables `citar` and `citar-backup.timer` (DEPLOY.md's "The installer sets up a nightly backup timer" was not true before). As root, the state directory and env path are validated as above when asked, and the state directory must be CITAR's alone (new, empty, or already holding CITAR's state; never one overlapping the install), checked again before anything is written, since an unattended run takes `--state-dir` unasked. **Fixed:** the first administrator, created in-process as root, could leave the database, its write-ahead log or a folder owned by root, which the service could then not write; what that creation made, and the database's files, are given to `citar` afterwards, and nothing that was there before, nor the env file. A failed creation, and the invite hint, name `sudo citar-admin` once the wrapper is installed. **`--refresh-units`** writes the installed unit, `citar-admin` and the backup again from the running release, with the paths the installed unit names, asking nothing and starting nothing (install.sh's switch runs it).
  - **`install.sh --server`** on the layout: the wheel is chosen first by pip in a temporary venv (`--from FILE|DIR`, `--version`, or the newest; a venv cannot be moved, so the release's folder must be named before it is made), then `releases/<P>/venv` (root's), the wheel, `freeze.txt` and `SHA256SUMS` beside it, `.complete` last (a folder without it is a half-finished install, made again, unless it is the release `current` points to, which is never removed: it is marked complete when its own `citar --version` names it, and refused otherwise), `current` switched by one rename (Python's `os.replace`, portable, where `ln -sfn` removes the old link first), and `citar` and `citar-mcp` linked through `current`. `citar-admin` is not linked any more: the venv's own command, run as root, reads no configuration and opens a database of its own; the wizard installs the wrapper. A newer release goes beside the running one and keeps the configuration; with the citar service installed it is P5.13.3's switch: `systemctl stop citar`; `systemctl start citar-backup`, a new folder without `UNTRUSTWORTHY` (exit 3, a failed offsite copy, accepted through `ExecMainStatus`), else citar is started again and nothing is switched; `current` switched; `citar setup --server --refresh-units` from the new release; `daemon-reload`; `systemctl start citar`; `/api/meta` on the env file's `CITAR_HOST`/`CITAR_PORT` answering as the new version within 60 s; on failure citar is stopped, the database restored from that backup when its revision moved, `current` switched back (its files written again), the old release started and checked, and exit 1 naming what happened. Without a service `current` is switched alone. An older release is refused before anything changes. **Fixed:** the wizard now runs through `sudo` for a non-root caller, so it can install the service it asks about (it ran as the caller, which skipped every system step). `--plan` (server and personal) prints each step the run takes, with every path, and changes nothing (an older release: the run's refusal, exit 1; the running one: kept, and the setup; a newer one: the stopped switch step by step, or the switch alone without a service); `--plan` with `--worker` or `--uninstall` is refused. `--uninstall --server` also disables and removes the backup units and removes the two scripts the wizard wrote, recognised by a line of their own.
  - **Retired:** `requirements.txt`, `scripts/sync_requirements.py` and test.yml's step (S2 is ruff alone from here).
  - **`env.example`:** every comment on a line of its own. **Fixed:** systemd reads everything after `=` as the value (checked with systemd 255's own parser), so the documented copy into `/etc/citar/citar.env` set `CITAR_SMTP_SECURITY` to `starttls          # starttls | ssl | none` and `CITAR_SESSION_DAYS` to `30                 # absolute lifetime`, and the server refused to start.
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_deploy.py` (44 after the fix round; standard library only; the fix round's are listed under it): the copies byte for byte and LF; the layout's paths render deploy/ unchanged; the wizard's `[Service]` directives equal deploy/'s, and another server's paths change only the five that name them; the unit's values; the scripts' variables; refused paths; a lost or repeated directive; the layout found from `sys.prefix` (symbolic links); the wizard's systemd step with temporary targets (files, modes, the three `systemctl` calls, no staged file left, a link replaced not written through, no wrapper without `citar-admin`); the ownership walk; citar-admin's properties equal the unit's (and none of its limits); its `--plan` with and without `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE`; a run against a stand-in `systemd-run` handing over `$5`, `100%`, `${HOME}` and quotes as they are; an older systemd refusing a `$`; the refusals; the backup (below); `install.sh --server --plan` and the personal plan; the uninstall's marks; env.example's values.

  - **The package's copies, not Python directives.** P5.13.2 has the wizard render "this file's directives"; the wizard writes deploy/'s own files with its paths put in, comments included, so a server installed by the wizard carries the same explanations, and the wrapper and the backup come by the same route.

  - **install.sh does its own stopped switch** (fix round, finding 1), not "finish with citar-deploy": a server installed with install.sh has no deploy bundle, and 5-10b does not exist yet. It is P5.13.3's steps 4 and 5 without the bundle's checks, the staged verification, nginx or the other sites, which stay citar-deploy's.
