### P5.13.3 `deploy/citar-deploy.sh`

One script, run as root on the box from an unpacked deploy bundle (P5.13.5) or from its installed copy. Every command has `--plan`, which prints each step it would take, with every path and command, and changes nothing (5-10b holds the plans to committed expected texts, as 4-14 held the service plans). When `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` (or `--slice`) is set, every `systemd-run` it starts, scopes included, runs in that slice (staging sets it, P5.14; the VPS leaves it unset).

**The server migrates on every start and refuses a database newer than its scripts** (P5.0.9), so a rollback after a migration must restore the database. `install` therefore takes its snapshot with the service stopped, immediately before the switch, and restores it itself when a failed release has moved the revision.
- **`install --bundle DIR`** (an ordinary release):
  1. **Preflight:** root; the bundle's files against its `SHA256SUMS`; the wheel's name, version and platform tag (a version older than the running one is refused: going back is `rollback`'s); `python3.12` with `venv`; `sqlite3`; at least 1.5 GB free under `/opt` and `/var`; the running release (if any); every URL of `deploy-checks.conf` answers 200 **before** anything changes (a box already in trouble is not deployed to), and CITAR's own health.
  2. **Stage:** `releases/<P>/venv` made fresh (an existing one is refused without `--reinstall`); `pip install --no-cache-dir --only-binary=:all: --constraint <constraints> "<wheel>[server]"` inside `systemd-run --scope -p MemoryMax=500M -p CPUWeight=20` (in the slice when one is set) so the install cannot starve the other sites; `pip freeze` kept.
  3. **Verify the staged release** as `citar`, with the env file's non-state settings only and every state setting (`CITAR_DATA_DIR`, `CITAR_DB_URL` and the directories the env file names) pointed at a fresh temporary directory, removed afterwards, so the new code never opens the live database while the old release serves: `citar --version` is V; `citar._engine` loads from that venv without test operations; `citar doctor --quiet` passes, its job host verifiable (a FAIL line stops the deploy before anything has changed). A cutover verifies the same way.
  4. **Switch:** the live database's `alembic_version` read (`sqlite3`, read-only) and recorded; `systemctl stop citar`; `systemctl start citar-backup` (oneshot, so it waits; with the service stopped the snapshot is consistent and current), and the new backup must exist without `UNTRUSTWORTHY`; `current` replaced atomically (`ln -sfn` to a temporary name, `mv -T`); the units, `citar-admin`, `citar-backup`, `citar-secrets` and the script itself installed from the bundle into `/etc/systemd/system`, `/usr/local/bin` and `/usr/local/sbin` when they differ (the difference shown), so the box never keeps 0.1.5's backup, which skips most of the state directory; `daemon-reload`; `systemctl start citar`. The downtime (the backup and the start) is measured and recorded.
  5. **Health:** `/api/auth/config` 200 on 127.0.0.1 and through nginx (with the site's `Host`), and `/api/meta`'s version is V, within 60 s; a WebSocket handshake through nginx is answered by the app (an `Upgrade` the proxy passes). **On failure:** `systemctl stop citar`; if the database's revision differs from the one recorded in step 4, the database is restored from step 4's snapshot (the restore drill's path, P5.14); `current` goes back to the previous release; `systemctl start citar`; the health is checked again (the version on `/api/meta` is the previous one); and the script exits 1 naming what failed and whether the database was restored.
  6. **The other sites:** every URL again; a failure is reported loudly with the rollback command and exit 3, and nothing is done automatically (the cause may not be CITAR's; Phase 6 is attended).
  7. **Record** the deploy (version, time, previous release, revisions before and after, the backup's path); prune releases beyond three, never `current` or the one before it.
- **`cutover --bundle DIR`** (Phase 6's first deploy, from 0.1.5's layout): the preflight, which also recognises the 0.1.5 layout (`/opt/citar/citar/__init__.py`, `/opt/citar/.venv`, `citar-lab.service`); stop `citar` and `citar-lab`; 0.1.5's own backup; copy the installed unit files and scripts into the archive; disable and remove `citar-lab.service`; move `/opt/citar` to `/opt/citar-0.1.5`; move `/var/lib/citar` to `/var/lib/citar-archive-<stamp>` and make a fresh `/var/lib/citar` (the owner's "archive everything and start fresh"; the 0.1.6 server makes its database on first start); list the env file's keys that 0.1.6's `env.example` does not name (shown, not changed); then `install`'s steps 2-7, its switch without the snapshot (the archived state is the backup), and `uncutover` as its automatic rollback on any failure from the staging on; print the next steps (P5.15). A cutover's downtime includes the staging (the old tree must move before the new venvs exist, and a venv cannot be moved), a few minutes on the box; staging measures it.
- **`rollback [--to P] [--restore-backup DIR]`:** `current` to the previous (or named) release, restart, health. The database's revision is first checked against the target release's migrations (`citar/migrations` in its venv): a revision the target does not know is refused without `--restore-backup`, and the refusal names the backup the migrating deploy took (from the deploy log). With a backup, the service is stopped and the database and state restored from it first.
- **`uncutover`:** the cutover undone: the new layout and state moved aside, `/opt/citar-0.1.5` and the archived state put back, the saved units restored (`citar-lab` included), both started, health and the other sites checked.
- **`status`**, **`prune --keep N`**.

### P5.13.4 Limits on a 1 vCPU, 961 MB box

| Thing | How it is held |
|---|---|
| The server and its games | `citar.service`'s `MemoryMax`/`MemoryHigh` and `OOMScoreAdjust=500` (CITAR dies first, never the other sites' services); the job host's `RLIMIT_DATA` and `nice` inside it |
| CITAR against the other sites | `citar.service`'s `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` (20 to 50, from 5-11's response-time measurement), so under contention the other sites' services win; the delegated child cgroup is the fallback (P5.13.2) |
| The lab | the host machine's CPU settings (server mode: one thread, 50%, the memory figure Phase 6 sets from the staging measurement so that the server's peak plus the host's figure stays under `MemoryHigh` with 40 MB to spare) |
| A deploy's pip install | a transient scope, `MemoryMax=500M`, `CPUWeight=20` |
| Releases on disk | three kept (a release's venv is about 200 MB) |

### P5.13.5 Where the wheel comes from

The laptop, never the box, fetches and checks it: `scripts/deploy/bundle.py V` downloads the release's linux-x64 wheel, `citar-P-server-constraints.txt` and `SHA256SUMS` (`gh release download vV`), checks the hashes, runs `gh attestation verify` on the wheel, adds `git archive vV deploy/ env.example`, and writes `citar-deploy-V.tar.gz` with its SHA-256 under `C:/dev/target`. `--from-dir DIR` builds a bundle from a dry run's artifacts or a local wheel (no attestation exists; it says so) for the staging run. Copying it to the box uses the operator's SSH details from the private log.

**As built in 5-10b: `citar-deploy` and the deploy bundle** (Appendix F, 5-10b; P5.13.3 and P5.13.5). One agent, from 2026-10-25 15:28 to 16:54 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-10b` from `17eee59c`.

- **What was built.**
  - **`deploy/citar-deploy.sh`** (installed by itself as `/usr/local/sbin/citar-deploy`): `install --bundle DIR [--reinstall]`, `cutover --bundle DIR`, `rollback [--to P] [--restore-backup DIR]`, `uncutover`, `status`, `prune [--keep N]`; every command takes `--plan` (every check runs, each step is printed with its paths and commands, nothing changes; `status` changes nothing anyway); `--slice NAME` or `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` puts every `systemd-run` it starts (the pip scope and the verification's three units) in that slice; `--site-url URL` names the site as visitors reach it (default the env file's `CITAR_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`). Exit 0 done, 1 refused or failed (a failed install or cutover undone first, saying so), 2 a usage error, 3 deployed but another site does not answer since.
    - **The preflight**, before anything changes and in the plan alike: root; `systemctl`, `systemd-run`, `sqlite3`, `sha256sum`, `flock`, and `python3.12` making a venv with pip; the `citar` account; the env file; the bundle (every line of its `SHA256SUMS` matched, every file listed and nothing more, its `BUNDLE`, the files citar-deploy needs, one wheel and its constraints); the wheel's tags for this machine (a manylinux tag of its architecture no newer than its glibc, a CPython tag this Python satisfies) and its version the bundle's; the running release, a version older than it refused (rollback's), the same refused, a staged folder refused without `--reinstall`; the installed `citar.service` naming the layout's `ExecStart`, `WorkingDirectory` and `EnvironmentFile` (citar-deploy deploys the release layout and nothing else); 1,536 MB free under `/opt/citar` and `/var` (`df -P`); the live database (found as citar-backup finds it) and its revision; every URL of `/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf` answering 200 (the file must exist; an empty one serves a box without other sites); CITAR's own health as below, as the running version.
    - **Stage** `releases/<P>`: the wheel and constraints copied in, `python3.12 -m venv`, `systemd-run --scope --quiet [--slice] -p MemoryMax=500M -p CPUWeight=20 -- <venv>/bin/pip install --disable-pip-version-check --no-cache-dir --only-binary=:all: --constraint <constraints> "<wheel>[server]"`, `pip freeze` into `freeze.txt`, the wheel's `SHA256SUMS` line.
    - **Verify** as `citar`: `systemd-run --quiet --wait --collect --pipe --service-type=exec [--slice] -p User=citar -p Group=citar -p WorkingDirectory=<v> -p Environment=CITAR_STATE_DIR=<v>/state -p EnvironmentFile=<v>/citar.env -p ReadWritePaths=<v>` with the unit's sandbox (19 properties, `SANDBOX`, citar-admin's list), in `<v>` = `/var/lib/citar-deploy/verify.XXXXXX` (citar's, 0700, under a 0711 root folder; removed afterwards, on any exit). `<v>/citar.env` is the env file without the seven state settings (`CITAR_STATE_DIR`, `_DATA_DIR`, `_DB_URL`, `_SAVE_DIR`, `_CONFIG_DIR`, `_BENCH_DIR`, `_KEYS_FILE`), each set inside `<v>`, and without any other setting whose value names a live state path (named in the output). Then `citar --version` is V; `python -P -c` with `smoke_wheel.py`'s `EXTENSION_FACTS`: the engine from that venv, without the test operations; `citar doctor` exits 0 and its jobs line says `verifiable`. Only then `.complete` (`verified by citar-deploy: CITAR V`), the mark install.sh reads. (Fix round, findings 4 and 5: also the home inside `<v>`, the live state `InaccessiblePaths=`, and the deploy's share of the box.)
    - **Switch** (install): the live revision read (`sqlite3 -readonly`); `systemctl stop citar` (a service that will not stop is started again and nothing switched); `systemctl start citar-backup`, a new folder without `UNTRUSTWORTHY` and with `RELEASE` (exit 3, a failed offsite copy, accepted through `ExecMainStatus`), else citar is started again on the old release and nothing switched; `current` by `ln -sfn` to `current.switching` and `mv -T`; the units and scripts from the bundle where they differ (`diff -u` shown; each written beside its target and renamed over it, so the running script keeps its file), `/etc/nginx/sites-available/citar` compared and never written; `daemon-reload`; `start citar`. (Fix round, finding 1: from the stop to the record, a hang-up is ignored and Ctrl-C or a TERM undoes the switch; the backup is the folder that was not there before it ran.)
    - **Health**, within 60 s: `/api/auth/config` 200 and `/api/meta`'s version V at the env file's `CITAR_HOST`/`CITAR_PORT`; through the site URL connected at 127.0.0.1 with its own Host and SNI, `/api/auth/config` 200 and a WebSocket handshake on `/ws/worker` answered by CITAR (101 with the accept key for this handshake; a proxy that drops the Upgrade gets the app's 404). **On failure:** stopped; the revision read again; moved from the one recorded, the backup's `citar.db` put back (owner and mode kept, `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal` removed) before anything starts; a database the release made where there was none moved aside as `citar.db.made-by-<P>`; `current` back; the replaced files put back and the added ones removed; `daemon-reload`; started; the previous version's health checked; exit 1 naming what failed, whether the database was restored and the backup.
    - **The other sites** again: a failure is printed loudly with the way back and exits 3, nothing done automatically. **Record**: one line in `/var/log/citar-deploy.log` (`install version= previous= result=ok|undone revision_before= revision_after= backup= downtime=`, the downtime from the stop to the health); then prune beyond three.
    - **`cutover`:** the preflight with 0.1.5's layout recognised (`/opt/citar/citar/__init__.py` with its `__version__`, `/opt/citar/.venv`, `citar-lab.service` if there, no `current`, no earlier cutover) and the settings no longer read listed; stop `citar` and `citar-lab`; 0.1.5's own backup (a new folder without `UNTRUSTWORTHY`); each file the cutover replaces or removes saved in `/var/lib/citar-deploy/cutover/files` with the record `cutover/CUTOVER` (the old version, `/opt/citar-<old>`, `/var/lib/citar-archive-<stamp>`, the backup, the lab, which files were there and which were not); `disable` and remove `citar-lab.service`; `/opt/citar` to `/opt/citar-<old>`, `/var/lib/citar` to `/var/lib/citar-archive-<stamp>`, a fresh `/var/lib/citar` (citar:citar 0750); then the stage, the verification, the switch without a snapshot (`enable citar citar-backup.timer` beside it), the health, the other sites, the record. Any failure from the first move on undoes it by itself (uncutover's steps) and exits 1.
    - **`uncutover`:** stop; `/opt/citar` and `/var/lib/citar` moved aside (`-<P>-undone-<stamp>`, kept) only where the record's archive is still there to take their place, so a cutover that failed part way is undone exactly as far as it went; the archives back; the saved files back and the ones the cutover added (`citar-secrets`, `citar-deploy`) removed; `daemon-reload`, `enable citar-lab`, start both; 0.1.5's health (`/api/auth/config` directly and through the site; its `/api/meta` names no version); the record kept as `cutover-undone-<stamp>`.
    - **`rollback`:** to `--to P` or the newest complete release older than the running one; the live revision against the target's migrations (`revision = "..."` in its venv's `citar/migrations/versions`): unknown there, refused without `--restore-backup`, naming the backup the migrating deploy took (as the fix round's finding 2 made it: the last `install` line recorded `ok` that moved the revision, whose backup is trustworthy and at a revision the target knows, else the newest such backup folder); with a backup (its revision one the target knows): stopped, each folder its `RELEASE` names moved aside (`<dir>.before-restore-<stamp>`, kept), its tarball extracted into a fresh folder with the old owner and mode, the database put back; then the switch, the start, the health and the other sites.
    - **`status`** (current, each release and its mark, the service, the database and its revision, the health, a cutover's record, the last five deploys) and **`prune --keep N`** (N at least 2: never the release that runs or the one before it).
    - **Tests only:** `CITAR_DEPLOY_ROOT` prefixes every path, lifts the root check, skips every change of owner and requires stand-ins for `systemctl` and `systemd-run` first on `PATH` (it refuses the system's own); `CITAR_DEPLOY_PYTHON` and `CITAR_DEPLOY_HEALTH_SECONDS`.
  - **`scripts/deploy/bundle.py`** (standard library only): `V` (from the release: `gh release download vV --repo jprodgers/CITAR --dir <tmp> --pattern <wheel> --pattern <constraints> --pattern SHA256SUMS`, both files held to the release's `SHA256SUMS`, then `gh attestation verify <file> --repo jprodgers/CITAR` for the wheel and the constraints) or `V --from-dir DIR` (a dry run's assets, held to its `SHA256SUMS` or each file's `.sha256`, or a local build with nothing to check, said so; no attestation, said so); `--arch` (x86_64 by default), `--ref` (the tag, or `HEAD` with `--from-dir`), `--out` (default `C:/dev/target` on Windows, `~/citar-deploy` elsewhere, refused inside a git working tree). The bundle `citar-deploy-V/`: the wheel, the constraints, `deploy/` and `env.example` from `git archive` at the ref, `BUNDLE` (version, PEP 440 form, wheel, source, attestation, the commit), `SHA256SUMS` over every other file; packed sorted, owned by root, dated by the commit, gzip without a time, so the same inputs give the same bytes, with `citar-deploy-V.tar.gz.sha256`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_citar_deploy.py` (31: 5 text checks on every platform, 26 on Linux with bash, sqlite3, flock, GNU tar and venvs: CI's Linux legs and WSL) and `tests/test_deploy_bundle.py` (8, every platform); the expected plans `tests/data/deploy/*.txt` (install and cutover with and without a slice, rollback, rollback with a backup, uncutover, prune; `@PORT@` and `@ARCH@` put in; LF by `.gitattributes`). The runs use stand-in releases (pure-Python wheels named as the release's, whose `citar` answers `--version`, `doctor` and `serve`, migrating a real SQLite database to its own revision and refusing one it does not know), installed by the script into real venvs offline, a stand-in `systemctl` that starts a unit's `ExecStart` from the unit file under the root and runs the real `citar-backup` there, a stand-in `systemd-run` that gives a service only its properties' environment (logged), and stand-in sites.
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **The proxy check is a URL reached at 127.0.0.1** (`--site-url`, default `CITAR_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`): the site's own Host and, for https, its SNI and certificate, never DNS. The VPS's TLS site and staging's loopback nginx take the same code; staging, whose doctor needs an `https` origin in server mode, passes `--site-url` for its plain-HTTP nginx.
  - **The verification is the service's**: as `citar`, with the unit's sandbox, in a folder of citar-deploy's own under `/var/lib` (`/tmp` and `/var/tmp` are private to such a unit). It leaves out every setting that names a live state path, not only the state settings, so the new code cannot reach the live state through, say, a file kept in the state directory. `citar doctor` runs whole rather than `--quiet`: its exit status is `--quiet`'s pass, and the whole output holds the jobs line to `verifiable` (under `--quiet` a not-verifiable host is a warning, which passes).
  - **A failed release leaves the box as it was:** the units and scripts the switch replaced are put back and the ones it added removed (beyond `current` and the database).
  - **The settings shown at a cutover** are the env file's keys that neither the bundle's `env.example` nor the release's own code names: `env.example` does not name `CITAR_STATE_DIR` or the other directory settings 0.1.6 reads. They are listed in the preflight, so the plan shows them.
  - **citar-deploy's own files** are in `/var/lib/citar-deploy` (the lock, the verification folders, the cutover's record and saved files) beside P5.13.1's layout. The lock (`flock`) is taken after the preflight, so a refusal writes nothing, and a second deploy stops at it.
  - **The release's tags are checked by the preflight**, not left to pip: a wheel for another machine is named before anything changes.
  - **bundle.py** verifies the constraints' attestation as well as the wheel's, and its tarball is reproducible.
- **Gates** (logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-10b/logs`; scripts in the session's scratchpad `p510b/`):

Also: a verification that fails (doctor's FAIL, a not-verifiable job host, an engine with the test operations) changes nothing that runs; rollback without a migration, `status`, `prune --keep 2`; a cutover from a stand-in 0.1.5 (its server, lab and backup) and its `uncutover` giving back 0.1.5's tree exactly (every file's hash); a cutover whose release exits, and one whose verification fails, each undone by itself to the same tree. **83 run (with `test_deploy`'s 44), OK, none skipped** (Python 3.12.3, sqlite3 3.45.1, GNU tar 1.35, bash 5.2.21; 181 s; also 82 of 82 at `8746edc`).
  4. **bundle.py** (`tests/test_deploy_bundle.py`, 8, on Windows and in WSL): `--from-dir` makes a bundle whose `SHA256SUMS` checks (in Python, and `sha256sum -c` on Linux), with `deploy/` byte for byte `git show HEAD:`'s, the same bytes twice, and no work folder left; a tampered wheel is refused against the folder's `SHA256SUMS` and against its `.sha256`, nothing written; a local build says it has nothing to check against, and a partial set of `.sha256` files is refused; the published path, against a stand-in `gh`, passes exactly `release download v0.1.6 --repo jprodgers/CITAR --dir <tmp under --out> --pattern <wheel> --pattern <constraints> --pattern SHA256SUMS` and `attestation verify <file> --repo jprodgers/CITAR` for both files, the download folder removed; a download that does not match the release's sums is refused before any attestation is asked for, and so is an attestation `gh` does not verify; never into a git working tree; a bad version is a usage error.

- **Fix round** (2026-10-25 from 17:33 (-04:00), one agent; the review found eight issues, one major and seven minor, all valid, all fixed; commits `dabddf4` (citar-deploy, its tests and the re-blessed plans), `de145f1` (bundle.py), `f84b411` (a text check) and this note). Each new or extended test fails on the script before the fix (`logs/fix-old-script.log`: 10 tests, 13 failures, each on its finding).
  1. **(major) An interruption during the switch left the box half switched.** A dropped SSH session (SIGHUP, which sudo passes on), Ctrl-C or a TERM between the stop and the record killed the script: no undo, no log line, CITAR stopped or on a migrated database, and the EXIT trap deleted the only copies of the replaced units and scripts (a `/tmp` folder). Now, from the stop until the deploy is recorded (`guard`), a hang-up and a closed output (`PIPE`) are ignored, so the command finishes or undoes itself; INT and TERM undo an install (`undo_install`) or a cutover (before its record: 0.1.5 started again and the partial record removed, `cutover_abort`; after it: `cutover_failed`), as a failed release is undone; an undo itself ignores every one of them, so a second Ctrl-C cannot cut it short. A rollback, an uncutover and a prune ignore them once begun (each is the way back). A signal is acted on once the command it arrives during ends (Ctrl-C ends that one too, a TERM sent to citar-deploy alone waits for it: at most the 60 s health wait). The copies live in `/var/lib/citar-deploy/run.<stamp>.XXXXXX` (root's, 0700), made when the first file is replaced and removed only once the deploy is recorded or its undo has put every file back. The usage says to run it inside tmux or screen. `Install.test_an_interrupted_install_is_undone`: a release that never answers, then a TERM to citar-deploy alone, an INT and a TERM to the whole job, and a HUP to the whole job: each exits 1 with `current` back on 0.1.6, the database restored to r2, the replaced `citar-backup` back, the added `citar-deploy` gone, 0.1.6 serving, no `run.*` left (the HUP's by the health check's own undo, the others' "interrupted (SIG...)"); `Cutover.test_an_interrupted_cutover_undoes_itself` gives back 0.1.5's tree exactly.
  2. **The deploy log's backup could never be found** (the pattern used up the space before `backup=`), so every refusal named the fallback, and a fixed pattern would have named a two-hop deploy's backup at a revision the target does not know. The log's lines are now read field by field (`backup_for`): the last `install` line recorded `ok` that moved the revision, whose backup is a `citar-backup` folder (RELEASE, citar.db, no UNTRUSTWORTHY) at a revision the target knows (`usable_backup`); else the newest such folder. `Install.test_rollback_names_the_backup_the_migrating_deploy_took`: with a newer trustworthy backup planted at r2, the install's own backup is named; over 0.1.7 (r3) and 0.1.8 (r4), `--to 0.1.6` names 0.1.7's backup (r2) and `--to 0.1.7` names 0.1.8's (r3), the planted one never.
  3. **The prune after an install could delete the rollback target** (a running release without `.complete`), and its plan said it kept it. `prune_releases` now takes the release before the one that runs: after an install, the release that ran until the switch, in the plan and the run alike (`prune` passes the newest complete one older). And the install marks that release complete when its own `citar --version` names it (install.sh's rule, so a rollback can go back to it), refusing otherwise. `Install.test_the_release_that_ran_is_kept_by_the_prune_whatever_its_mark`: refused while its `citar` is gone; then the plan's and the run's prune lines are the same (`rm -rf /opt/citar/releases/0.1.4`, `kept: 0.1.7 0.1.6 0.1.5`), 0.1.6 marked, and a rollback goes back to it.
  4. **The verification ran with citar's home, the live state directory** (systemd gives a unit with `User=` that account's home, and `citar setup --server` makes it the state directory; CITAR's fallbacks read it). The verification's `citar.env` now ends with `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `_DATA_HOME`, `_CACHE_HOME` and `_STATE_HOME` inside `<v>/state` (an environment file wins over `Environment=` and over the account's home, systemd.exec(5)), the same five are left out of the copied settings, `-p Environment=HOME=<v>/state` says it on the command line, and every live state path (`live_paths`, but `/`, one that holds `/var/lib/citar-deploy` or `/opt/citar`, and one inside another) is `-p InaccessiblePaths=-<path>`, so the new code cannot reach the live state at all. The `systemd-run` stand-in now builds a service's environment as systemd does (the account's home from `STUB_HOME_<user>`, then `Environment=`, then the environment file), so the existing "no live state path" assertion caught the old script (`HOME=.../var/lib/citar names the live state`).
  5. **The verification's units had no resource properties.** They now get `VERIFY_LIMITS`: `CPUWeight=20`, `MemoryMax=500M` (the pip scope's) and `OOMScoreAdjust=800` (above `citar.service`'s 500, as 0.1.5's lab unit had), so under memory pressure the verification dies first. Asserted on all three units in the migrating install's test.
  6. **A second citar-deploy changed the lock folder's mode before finding the lock held** (`chmod 0700`), cutting citar off from a first one's verification. `lock` now makes the folder, takes the lock, and only then sets 0711; the verification and the cutover no longer touch its mode, and the cutover's record folder is 0700. `Refusals.test_a_second_deploy_stops_at_the_lock_and_changes_nothing` (the lock held with `fcntl.flock`): refused, the folder still 0711; the cutover's test holds `/var/lib/citar-deploy` at 0711 and `cutover/` at 0700.
  7. **bundle.py accepted any workflow's attestation of the repository.** Each file is now held to `--repo jprodgers/CITAR --signer-workflow jprodgers/CITAR/.github/workflows/release.yml --source-ref refs/tags/vV --source-digest <the commit deploy/ is archived from> --deny-self-hosted-runners` (gh 2.83's `attestation verify`), so only release.yml's tag-push attestation passes and the wheel and `deploy/` are one commit's. The commit is resolved before anything is downloaded; a ref not in the checkout is refused naming `git fetch --tags`. `BUNDLE`'s attestation line names all of it.
  8. **`rollback --restore-backup` overwrote a database outside the restored folders** (a `CITAR_DB_URL` elsewhere), losing what was written since the backup. Such a database (with its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal`) is now moved to `<db>.before-restore-<stamp>` first, in the plan and the run, and the backup's takes its owner and mode. `Install.test_a_restore_keeps_a_database_outside_the_state` (`/srv/citar/citar.db`): the plan names the move, the database is back at r2, and the one moved aside holds r3 and the row 0.1.7 wrote.
  - **Found beside them, fixed:** `take_backup` took the newest folder name as the new backup, which a folder dated ahead (or a clock set back) would win; it now takes the folder that was not there before `citar-backup` ran. A cutover that failed before its record (a failed stop, backup or save) left a partial record that refused the next cutover; `cutover_abort` removes it (`Cutover.test_a_cutover_whose_backup_fails_changes_nothing_but_the_stop`). The `systemd-run` stand-in applied `Environment=` after the environment file (systemd does the opposite).
- **Fix round gates** (logs under `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-10b/logs`, `fix-*`; scripts in the session's scratchpad `p510b/`; at `f84b411`, the code's head, with this note uncommitted):
  0. **Each new or extended test fails on the old script** (`fix-old-script.log`, `iter_old.sh`: the tests at the fix, `deploy/citar-deploy.sh` at `c423839`): 10 tests, 13 failures (the signal test's four sub-tests), each on its finding: the lock folder 0700 where 0711 is held; killed by SIGTERM (exit -15) with no undo, for the install and the cutover; no refusal or mark of the unmarked running release; the planted backup named; no move of the outside database in the plan; `HOME=.../var/lib/citar names the live state`; no `CPUWeight=20` on the verification; the cutover's record folder 0755; the old abort's message. The new text check fails on each of three plants (the OOM score at 400, the memory at 900M, `as_service` without the properties).
  1. **shellcheck** 0.9.0 in WSL on a clone of `f84b411` (`fix-wsl-gates-head.log`): `deploy/citar-deploy.sh` at `--severity=style` and deploy/'s other three scripts, no finding; `scripts/deploy/` holds no shell script; the planted unquoted expansion refused with SC2086.
  2. **The plans** (`Plans`, 6): the four install and cutover texts re-blessed for the verification's new properties (`-p Environment=HOME=...`, `-p InaccessiblePaths=-/var/lib/citar`, the three limits; the home settings in its list) and the record folder's mode, nothing else in them changed; rollback, rollback with a backup, uncutover and prune unchanged. **The refusals** (`Refusals`, 11): the first round's 10 and the lock.
  3. **The revision logic and the fix round's behaviour, in WSL as `claude`** (`Install`, 11; `Cutover`, 5): the first round's, and the tests of findings 1 to 4, 6 and 8 above. **91 run (with `test_deploy`'s 44 and `test_deploy_bundle`'s 8), OK, none skipped** (Python 3.12.3, sqlite3 3.45.1, GNU tar 1.35, bash 5.2.21; 281 s; also 90 of 90 at `de145f1`, `fix-wsl-gates.log`).

  5. **Standard gates.** No Rust changed (the extension `cargo xtask develop`'s, build `0b412a18fe95`), so S7 is not owed.

The tracebacks: the known kinds (the teardown race's `games` and `owned_items`, "disk on fire", the registry import's JSON error) and once the helper update end-to-end tests' logged "a helper's connection failed" (WebSocket not accepted), which earlier merges' runs show too (`merge-5-10a-r2-s1.log`); the ledger's hand-off 12.7 ms.

WSL: no `citar` user, server folder, unit, nginx, process or container, the three base images, `~/target/citar-p5` 35 GB; only `~/p5`'s package folders changed (their times). No apt install.

---

## P5.14 The staging dry run in WSL

**`scripts/deploy/staging.sh setup|run|cleanup`**, run as root inside WSL only (`wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u root -- bash <copy in /root>`), on a copy of the branch and a deploy bundle, never under `/mnt/c`.

**Held to the VPS, not to WSL.** WSL has 12 CPUs, 7.8 GB, swappiness 60 and a faster core, and its `system.slice` delegates only memory and pids (P5.0.12), so a slice that merely names the VPS's figures would measure another machine. Staging therefore makes each difference explicit and proves each limit binds before it measures anything:
- **setup:** the apt packages a fresh VPS image lacks here (`python3-venv`, `sqlite3`, `nginx`), recorded.
  - **The slice:** `citar-staging.slice` with `CPUQuota=` scaled to the VPS's vCPU (`--cpu-quota`, default 60%: the review's estimate that a laptop core is about 1.6 times the VPS's; the figure used is recorded, and Phase 6's first measurement on the box corrects it for the next rehearsal), `MemoryMax=961M` and `MemorySwapMax=2G`; every staged unit joins it through a drop-in (`Slice=`), and `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE=citar-staging.slice` is exported, so `citar-deploy`'s pip scope and `citar-admin`'s transient units land in it too, and the deploy script runs unmodified.
  - **`vm.swappiness=10`**, the VPS's, for the run (the original value recorded; cleanup restores it). It is WSL's own kernel setting, inside WSL only.
  - **The other sites' load:** a **ballast** unit in the slice that allocates and touches the other sites' resident memory (`--ballast-mb N`, required, no default: the figure comes from the private log's measurement and lives in `ops/p5-notes.md`, never in a tracked file), so CITAR gets what it gets on the box, not the whole 961 MB; and **two stand-in sites** (an nginx server block, and a small Python HTTP unit in the slice that does a fixed slice of CPU work per request, like php-fpm), listed in `/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf`, with a load generator calling the Python one at a steady rate during the game and lab steps and recording its response times.
  - **Probes, before anything is measured:** the slice's `memory.max`, `memory.swap.max` and `cpu.max` read back equal to what was asked; a transient probe unit in the slice with a small `MemoryMax` and no swap allocates past it and must be OOM-killed, with `oom_kill` rising in the slice's `memory.events`; a busy loop in the slice for five seconds must use the quota's share of a CPU (`cpu.stat`'s `usage_usec` within 10% of quota times wall time). A limit that does not bind fails setup. `IOWeight=` and `AllowedCPUs=` cannot be proven here (no `io` or `cpuset` controller in WSL): VPS-only, named so in the as-built note.
  - **A 0.1.5 deployment** as the VPS has it: `git archive v0.1.5` into `/opt/citar`, its `.venv` with its `requirements.txt`, its `citar.service` and `citar-lab.service` (CITAR on `127.0.0.1` only), started once to make its state, and an nginx site for CITAR on a loopback port with the `connection_upgrade` map.
- **run:** `citar-deploy cutover` with the bundle (a local wheel in 5-11, **the dry run's CI-built wheel** in 5-13); then, through `scripts/deploy/staging_check.py` (standard library only):
  - the first administrator made with `citar-admin`; **the host machine's settings set through the admin API** (`PUT /api/machines/{sid}/settings`) to `host_cpu_defaults(mode="server", cpus=1, ram_mb=961)`, as the VPS computes them, and the job host's `ready` frame must report about 240 MB (WSL's own defaults would give it 1,024 MB of `RLIMIT_DATA`, and under `MemoryMax` that turns a clean allocation failure into a cgroup OOM kill);
  - a 4-bot small game played for 40 rounds through the API; a lab experiment of three duel games submitted as `citar` and played by the job host under the unit's sandbox;
  - the measurements: `MemoryPeak` and `MemoryCurrent` of `citar.service` idle, with the game and with the lab; the job host's peak from its frames; the slice's `memory.events` `oom_kill`, which must stay 0; seconds per round; the pip install's time and peak inside the slice; the switch's downtime; **the loaded stand-in's response times** idle and with a lab game running, whose p95 must stay within twice the idle p95 plus 50 ms (the bound 5-11 records with the `CPUWeight=` it settles on);
  - **an upgrade that migrates:** `citar-deploy install` of `-rc.1`, a wheel built locally in the maturin container from a scratch clone bumped past the cut-over version that also adds an empty migration; then `rollback`, refused without `--restore-backup` (the revision moved) and accepted with the backup `install` took; then `install` of `-rc.1` again;
  - **a failed upgrade that migrated:** `-rc.2`, a planted release that adds a second empty migration and whose server exits after migrating, must come back by itself, healthy on `-rc.1` at `-rc.1`'s revision, with `install` exiting 1 and saying the database was restored;
  - `citar-backup` and a restore drill (the snapshot opened, integrity-checked, and a second CITAR started against a copy on another port, reading its accounts); `uncutover`, with 0.1.5 answering again.
  - The stand-in sites answer 200 before and after every step, and the CITAR site's WebSocket upgrade passes nginx.
- **cleanup:** units, drop-ins, the slice, the ballast, the load generator, the `citar` user, `/opt/citar*`, `/var/lib/citar*`, `/etc/citar`, the nginx sites and the stand-ins removed; `vm.swappiness` restored; nginx stopped and disabled (the packages stay, recorded); S10's WSL items checked. The logs and measurements are copied under the package's target folder (`C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-xx/staging/`), and the figures that matter go into the as-built note.

Phase 6 runs it again with the published wheel before touching the VPS (P5.15).

**As built in 5-11: the staging dry run in WSL** (Appendix F, 5-11; P5.14, P5.13.2, P5.13.4). One agent, from 2026-10-25 20:51 to 2026-10-26 00:02 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-11` from `c51eb6a`; its fix round from 00:30 to 01:50 (below, "Fix round").

- **What was built.**
  - **`scripts/deploy/staging.sh setup|probes|run|cleanup|status`** (bash, root, WSL only: it refuses a machine whose kernel is not WSL's, a missing systemd, and a copy under `/mnt`). **setup:** the apt packages a fresh box lacks (only nginx was missing here), nginx's default site disabled for the run; `vm.swappiness` 10 (the value before recorded); `citar-staging.slice` with `AllowedCPUs=` one CPU (`--cpu`, default the last), `CPUQuota=60%` (`--cpu-quota`) in 10 ms periods (`--cpu-period`), `MemoryMax=961M`, `MemorySwapMax=2G`; the probes; a ballast unit in the slice holding `--ballast-mb` (Type=notify once allocated and touched, read through every 30 s); two stand-in sites in `/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf`, an nginx server block on 127.0.0.1:8081 and a Python unit in the slice on 127.0.0.1:8082 doing 25 ms of CPU a request (`--work-ms`, calibrated at its start on the fastest of seven 100 ms batches); CITAR 0.1.5 as the box has it: `git archive v0.1.5` of `--repo` in `/opt/citar`, `.venv` from its `requirements.txt`, an env file with the box's keys (server mode, `https://citar-staging.test`, 127.0.0.1:8765, the four state directories, a generated secret key), 0.1.5's own `citar`, `citar-lab`, `citar-backup` units and backup script, a stand-in for the hand-made `citar-admin`, a drop-in (`Slice=`) for each staged unit, and an nginx site on 127.0.0.1:8088 with the `connection_upgrade` map; started, answering directly and through nginx. **probes:** the slice's `memory.max`, `memory.swap.max`, `cpu.max` and `cpuset.cpus.effective` read back; a transient unit with `MemoryMax=64M` and no swap allocating 256 MiB must end `oom-kill` with the slice's `oom_kill` rising; a 5 s busy loop in the slice must use the quota's share (`cpu.stat` within 10%) and may run on the one CPU alone (`Cpus_allowed_list`); `--without-cpu-quota`, the gate that can fail, takes the quota away for that probe alone, holds the probe to failing (its use printed as a figure), puts the quota back and reads the slice's files again, and like every command exits 0 only when all of that held (the fix round's: it first exited with the probe's own 1, so a quota left off passed). **run:** first the slice read back as setup left it (its files, the ballast held, the loaded stand-in running, `vm.swappiness` 10), nothing run on one that differs (the fix round's); then, with `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE=citar-staging.slice` exported, every step between `staging_check.py sites` before and after it, its output kept, its exit status held to the one wanted, and a timeline (`staging_check.py watch`, a transient unit outside the slice) sampling `citar.service`'s and the slice's memory, swap and `oom_kill`, every transient unit in the slice, and CITAR's `/api/auth/config` twice a second: `cutover` (the bundle's own `citar-deploy.sh`, `--site-url http://citar-staging.test:8088`), the WebSocket upgrade through nginx, `staging_check.py measure`, `install` of rc.1, `rollback --to` refused and parsed for the backup it names, then accepted with it, `install --reinstall` of rc.1, `install` of rc.2 and of rc.3 (each must exit 1, the first "restored: yes", the second "no (its revision is still"), rc.2 again with a TERM sent to citar-deploy once its health step has begun (exit 1, "interrupted (SIGTERM)", no `run.*` left), `citar-backup` and the restore drill (the newest folder whole, `PRAGMA integrity_check` ok, `state.tar.gz` and `citar.db` copied into a folder of citar's, a second `citar serve` on 127.0.0.1:8766 as a transient unit in the slice, `staging_check.py drill` signing the administrator in and reading `GET /api/admin/users`), `uncutover` (0.1.5 answering again, `citar-lab` running); after each install and rollback `/api/meta`'s version and the live revision against the release's own migration head; then `staging_check.py report`, the journal and the deploy log copied into the run's logs. **cleanup:** every unit, drop-in, runtime property, the slice and the implicit `citar.slice` its dashed name nests it in, the `citar*` files in `/usr/local/{bin,sbin}`, `/opt/citar*`, `/var/lib/citar*` (a restore's `.before-restore-<stamp>` folders included), `/etc/citar`, `/var/backups/citar`, the deploy log, the nginx sites (the default site enabled again), the `citar` account; swappiness restored; nginx stopped and disabled, then the CITAR site's own logs (`/var/log/nginx/citar.*`, the fix round's); then each of these checked gone.
  - **`scripts/deploy/staging_check.py`** (standard library only): `hog`, `burn`, `ballast`, `standin`, `sites`, `health`, `watch`, `measure`, `drill`, `report` (P5.14's checks and measurements; its module doc lists them). `measure`: the first administrator through the installed `citar-admin create-user --password-stdin` (systemd-run as citar, in the slice) and its sign-in (the Secure cookie and the CSRF token sent by hand over plain HTTP to 127.0.0.1); `host_cpu_defaults(mode="server", cpus=1, ram_mb=961)` computed by the release's own Python; the host machine found as the Servers page finds it (`GET /api/servers`'s `host_server_id`, asked again until a new server's registry names one) and its settings put; idle (60 s); a 4-bot small game to 40 rounds; three duels to the speed's last turn; then, at each `--weights` value and the unit's own `CPUWeight=` (set at run time with `systemctl set-property --runtime`, read back from the cgroup, removed afterwards), 40 duels while the stand-in is timed (4 requests a second throughout; CITAR's own `/api/auth/config` through nginx once a second); with `--host-mb`, three duels again at that memory figure. Each phase's memory is `citar.service`'s own `memory.peak` reset for the span through a descriptor of its own (Linux 6.12 and later; else sampled), systemd's `MemoryCurrent`/`MemoryPeak`, the server's and the job host's `VmHWM`; each run's peak, CPU and wall seconds and rounds come from the server's database (`cpu_job_runs`, read only).
  - **`scripts/deploy/staging_wheels.sh`** (as an ordinary user, rootless podman; neither `--clone` nor `--out` under `/mnt`, each checked as given and as it resolves, before anything is written): the cutover's wheel built from `--clone` in maturin's own container (`ghcr.io/pyo3/maturin` at package.yml's `MATURIN_VERSION` v1.15.0, pinned since the fix round by the index digest `sha256:1c897d72...` its tag names, and checked to say `maturin 1.15.0`) with the release's flags (`--release --locked --compatibility manylinux_2_28`, `~/target/citar-p5`'s caches), as the earlier packages' WSL gates build it. That is not the release's build: package.yml builds it through maturin-action in the digest-pinned quay.io manylinux_2_28 image, so a real release's rehearsal (5-13's, Phase 6's) takes CI's wheel with `--cutover-from DIR` (the dry run's wheel and constraints, or an unpacked `bundle.py` bundle, which holds both); X.Y.Z-rc.1 to rc.3 from scratch clones bumped by `release.py bump` (`--rc-base`, default the next patch), with `9000a_staging_upgrade` (rc.1), `9000b_staging_failure` (rc.2, rc.3, after it) and the plant in `boot.startup()` (rc.2 raises `BootError` right after `migrate()`, rc.3 right before it), each clone's change committed in it and the clone removed; `smoke_wheel.py constraints` for each; `bundle.py --from-dir` (deploy/ from `--clone`'s HEAD), unpacked and `sha256sum -c`; `--bundles-only` makes the bundles again from the wheels already built.
  - **`deploy/citar.service`** (and the package's copy): `CPUWeight=20`, `IOWeight=20`, `MemoryMax=400M`/`MemoryHigh=320M` kept, the measurement written beside each (below). `tests/test_deploy.py` holds the values and the two notes.
  - **`docs/server/RUNBOOK.md`:** "The staging rehearsal" (what makes WSL the box, the releases, the run, the table of what each step must hold, the logs).
- **Found and fixed in `citar-deploy` (5-10b's): the real cutover would have undone itself.** `install_files` ends by showing how `/etc/nginx/sites-available/citar` differs from the bundle's `nginx-citar.conf`; under `set -o pipefail`, `diff`'s exit 1 ("they differ", which a site certbot has edited always does) became the function's status, so the switch was undone: "the units and scripts could not be installed". The first staging cutover did exactly that (and its automatic undo put 0.1.5 back whole). The difference is now shown without becoming the step's status, there and for each replaced file. 5-10b's tests never had an nginx site; `Install`'s migrating install and `Cutover`'s cutover now plant one as certbot leaves it and hold it unwritten: both fail on the script before the fix (`fix-nginx-tests.log`: "1 != 0 ... could not be installed") and pass after it.
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **One CPU, in short periods.** P5.14 scales the slice's CPU with `CPUQuota=`; measured, that alone models neither the box's one vCPU nor its speed. On twelve CPUs the slice's units run side by side, so `CPUWeight=` decides nothing between CITAR and the stand-in (10 ms periods: stand-in p95 102, 105 and 104 ms at 50, 35 and 20); and the quota's default 100 ms period stops the whole slice for 40 ms in every 100, which dominated every response time (one run: 143 ms at 50 and 102 at 20; the next: 147, 147 and 146 ms at 50, 35 and 20). The slice now has `AllowedCPUs=` one CPU (the cpuset controller is there in WSL's kernel 6.18, which P5.0.12's reading at `51ce329` did not have) and `CPUQuotaPeriodSec=10ms`: one slower core, as the box has, where the weights separate (58, 63 and 66 ms). `IOWeight=` stays VPS-only: the io controller is there too, but the virtual disk's scheduler is `none` and the cgroup has no `io.weight`.
  - **The slice's path.** A dash nests a slice (systemd.slice(5)): `citar-staging.slice` is `/citar.slice/citar-staging.slice`; both scripts ask systemd (`ControlGroup`), and cleanup stops the implicit `citar.slice` too.
  - **The lab's load.** A duel plays in under a second on the Rust engine (990 rounds in 6.6 s of runs), so three duels give the stand-in a handful of requests: the three duels are gate 2's (played, the job host's `ready`, `RLIMIT_DATA`, its cgroup), and each weight is timed over 40 duels (about 110 s of runs, 440 requests). The bound is held at the installed unit's weight; the others are measured beside it and printed as figures, not failures.
  - **The host machine's row.** A new server has none until its registry is read, and makes it after it starts answering (a slow box surveys its hardware first); `GET /api/servers` (the Servers page's read) names it, asked again until it does.
  - **A second and a third planted failure.** Gate 3's "a planted release whose server exits before migrating" is rc.3; P5.13.5's "staging may also interrupt `-rc.2`'s install during its health wait" is run too.
  - **`--host-mb 128`:** the host memory figure recommended below is proven by playing the duels at it.
  - **0.1.5's requirements** come from PyPI as the box's did (`>=` pins; current versions installed and 0.1.5 ran on them). The 0.1.5 wrapper `/usr/local/bin/citar-admin` is a stand-in (`systemd-run` as citar with the env file), replaced by the cutover and put back by `uncutover`.
  - **The ballast figure** (from the private log's measurement on the box: its 961 MB less what was available and what CITAR and its lab game held) is kept in `ops/p5-notes.md` with its derivation, never in a tracked file (nor is anything here that gives it away); the slice then leaves CITAR what it had on the box.
  - **`staging_wheels.sh` is tracked** (P5.14 names the rc wheels, not a script), so 5-13 and Phase 6 build theirs the same way.
- **The measurements** (the final run, `run-20261026-025151`; one CPU at 60% in 10 ms periods, the ballast at `ops/p5-notes.md`'s figure, the stand-in 25 ms a request; logs in `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-11/staging/final-run/`):

  | | |
  |---|---|
  | `citar.service` memory (its cgroup) | idle 89 MB; with the 4-bot game 96 MB; with the lab 121 MB; with 40 duels 125 to 128 MB; `MemoryPeak` 147.4 MB (its first start, on the new database); swap 0 |
  | the server process | `VmHWM` 171 MB (resident, shared pages included) |

The review found six minor issues. All six were valid and all are fixed, in commits `f82dbf4`, `7b682a6`, `026cbd7`, `0614d12`, `81bdada` and this note:
  1. **The gate that can fail could pass for the wrong reason.** `probes --without-cpu-quota` exited with the probe's own status, 1 when it failed as wanted, and its "the quota is back" check only counted a failure: a quota that never came back still exited 1, and nothing in `run` read the slice again, so every figure and the response-time bound would have been taken on an unthrottled core. The command now holds its own verdict and, like every other, exits 0 only when all of it held: the quota taken away (`cpu.max` reads `max`), the probe outside its share (printed as a figure, not a failed check), the quota back and the slice's files read again; a refusal, a probe within its share or a quota left off exit 1. `run` begins by reading the slice back as setup left it (its four files, the ballast held, resident or swapped, the stand-in running, `vm.swappiness` 10) and runs nothing on one that differs. Proven by planting the fault, a persistent drop-in clearing `CPUQuota=` so the quota cannot come back: `probes --without-cpu-quota` exit 1 (`FAILED: cpu.max reads 'max 100000'` after the probe failed as wanted), `run` exit 1 before anything ran (no deploy log, no `/opt/citar/current`); the drop-in removed, `probes` exit 0 (`staging/fix/plant-*.log`, `probes-again.log`).
  2. **cleanup left the CITAR site's nginx logs** (`write_citar_site` names them). It now removes `/var/log/nginx/citar.access.log*` and `citar.error.log*` once nginx is stopped, and its checked-gone pass looks for `/var/log/nginx/citar*`. The fix round's first `cleanup` removed the earlier runs' two (274 KB and an empty one; `staging/fix/nginx-logs-*.txt`).
  3. **`staging_wheels.sh`'s `/mnt` guard looked only at the start of `$CLONE$OUT`**, so `--out /mnt/c/...` passed and the scratch clones, wheels and bundles would have gone to Windows' disk. Each path is now checked as given and as `realpath -m` resolves it, before anything is written, and again once resolved. On the reviewed script the new test reproduces the hole: for an absolute, a relative and a linked `--out` under `/mnt` it went on to `mkdir` there (stopped only by `/mnt`'s own permissions; `/mnt/c` would have taken it).
  4. **The tooling had no automated test.** `tests/test_staging.py` (14 tests, standard library; 3 of them Linux-only): `percentile` and `times`; `Load.between`/`failed_between`; `measure --weights` in both spellings; `report` over a planted timeline (each down span named by its step, one still down at the end reported `to_the_end`, the OOM check failing when the count rose); the planted releases, each script's own here-document run on copies of the real files (the plant's line once in `boot.py`, the raise right after or right before `migrate()` in `startup()`, `BootError` defined there, a boot without the line refused; `add_migration` and `head_revision` against Python's own reading of the migrations: one head today, each planted migration the next, two heads stop the plant); `MATURIN` equal to package.yml's `MATURIN_VERSION`, the image pinned by digest; on Linux both scripts parse, twelve usage errors exit 2 before anything runs, and `--out` (absolute, relative, linked) or `--clone` under `/mnt` is refused writing nothing. Against the reviewed scripts (`57349fb`) unittest reports five failures (subtests counted) and one error: the `/mnt` refusals, `setup --without-cpu-quota` accepted, the open down span dropped, no `MATURIN` (`fix/logs/wsl-gates.log`). `staging_check.py`'s report is now `summarize()`, a pure function, and `cgroup_of` falls back to systemd's path where there is no `systemctl`.
  5. **RELEASING.md's step 9.2 and section 10** now give each command of the rehearsal (the attested bundle unpacked on WSL's disk as `--bundle`, `staging_wheels.sh --cutover-from` that folder for the planted releases, `setup`, `probes --without-cpu-quota` exiting 0, `run ... --host-mb 128`, `cleanup --keep-logs`) and the host memory figure, 128 MB.
  6. **The cutover wheel's provenance** is now stated as it is (the script's header, RUNBOOK, this note): maturin's container with the release's flags, not package.yml's quay.io manylinux_2_28 image. The container is pinned by the index digest `sha256:1c897d72...` (the registry's tag `v1.15.0` still named it on 2026-10-26), the script checks it says `maturin 1.15.0` before building, and the test holds `MATURIN` to package.yml's. Not taken: building in the quay.io image as maturin-action does, since the planted releases only stand for "a later release" and 5-13 and Phase 6 cut over to CI's own wheel (`--cutover-from`). Also: `--cutover-from` takes exactly one wheel and names its bundle as the tag spells the version.

## P5.15 The release checklist Phase 6 follows

`packaging/RELEASING.md` (5-09; walked through in dry-run form by 5-13) holds this list, each step a command:

1. **Before:** Phase 6's soak, QA and security review done; the owner's go-ahead. The version number decided (owner question). If updates are to work in this release: the owner has made `helper-signing` (required reviewer, the secret) and merged the public key into `update-keys.pub` (WORKERS.md); `gh api repos/jprodgers/CITAR/environments` shows it.
2. **Bump** on a branch from `main` (after `v0.1.6` merged): `python scripts/release.py bump 0.1.6 --date <today>`; review the diff; `release.py check --version 0.1.6`; `scripts/check_links.py --strict`; a pull request; every workflow green on it.
3. **Dry run** of that branch: `gh workflow run release.yml --ref <branch> -f version=0.1.6` (or `release-dry-run.yml`, if P5.11's fallback was taken), watched to green.
4. **Merge** to `main`; `main`'s CI green.
5. **Tag** the merge commit: `git tag -a v0.1.6 -m "CITAR 0.1.6"`, `git push origin v0.1.6`.
6. **Watch** release.yml; approve `sign-helper-manifest` when it waits for its reviewer. **If a tag-only job fails:** before `attest-files` passes, nothing is public: fix, delete the tag (`git push origin :refs/tags/v0.1.6`, the owner's step) and tag again. From then on re-run the failed job and those after it (`gh run rerun <id> --failed`): `publish-pypi` completes with `skip-existing`; `publish-image` and `attest-image` push the same saved images; `github-release` finds its draft and finishes it; `image-tags` moves the two tags last. RELEASING.md lists each job's state after a failure and the re-run for it.
7. **Verify:** `python scripts/release_checksums.py 0.1.6 --verify` (every asset against `SHA256SUMS`, PyPI's hashes against the built files; rehearsed by 5-13 in its `--base-url`/`--pypi-json` form); `gh attestation verify` on a wheel and a helper; `pip install citar==0.1.6` in a fresh venv takes a wheel, not the sdist; the image answers its health route, checked with **`podman run` in WSL** (the laptop has no Docker; the command is RELEASING.md's); a helper from `releases/latest/download/` reports 0.1.6 (run with `--home` in a temporary folder).
8. **Package managers:** the `pkg-release-preview` artifact's manifests (or `release_checksums.py 0.1.6 --write`); `scripts/release/managers.sh 0.1.6` clones the tap and the bucket into a temporary folder, copies the files (the tap: `Formula/citar-helper.rb` added, `Formula/citar.rb` removed; the bucket: `citar.json`, `citar-helper.json`), shows the diff, and pushes only with `--push`; `wingetcreate submit` for `JimmieRodgers.CITAR` and `JimmieRodgers.CITAR.Helper` (`winget validate` first).
9. **Deploy** (with the owner's go-ahead): `scripts/deploy/bundle.py 0.1.6`; the staging rehearsal with that bundle (P5.14, with `--ballast-mb` from the private log and the recorded `--cpu-quota`); copy to the box; `/etc/citar/deploy-checks.conf` made from the private log; `sudo bash deploy/citar-deploy.sh cutover --bundle <dir> --plan`, read, then without `--plan`. Rollback: `sudo citar-deploy uncutover`.
10. **After:** the first administrator (`sudo citar-admin create-user ...`); the host's machine settings from the staging measurement; one lab game's seconds per round on the box compared with staging's, and the ratio recorded as the next rehearsal's `--cpu-quota`; helpers re-registered with the new helper; `ops/DEPLOY_LOG.md` written.

---

| `SECURITY.md` | The release chain: actions pinned by SHA, attestations (files before anything is published), `SHA256SUMS`, the update key's custody and its rules (P5.4.2: a job that builds and installs nothing, a dry run that never names its environment), the packaged helper; `/api/meta` no longer reveals paths; supported versions |

| `packaging/README.md` | Rewritten for 0.1.6: every route, what CI builds, what Phase 6 copies where |
| `wiki/` | Regenerated by `scripts/build_wiki.py` and committed |

**The CHANGELOG's "Installing and packaging" compatibility item** says, for someone coming from 0.1.5: CITAR installs from wheels for Windows x64, macOS 11+ (Apple silicon and Intel) and Linux x64 and arm64 (glibc 2.28+), Python 3.11 or newer; elsewhere `pip install citar` builds from the sdist with a Rust toolchain; `install.sh` and `install.ps1` install wheels only and `--worker`/`-Worker` install the helper; the Homebrew tap now ships only the helper (`brew uninstall citar`, then `install.sh` or pipx for the game, `brew install jprodgers/citar/citar-helper` for the helper); Scoop and winget gain a helper package; the Docker image is multi-arch and built from the wheels; the Windows installer upgrades in place (same AppId and data folder); release assets add the wheels, the packaged helper archives, `SHA256SUMS`, `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` and attestations, and the helper's download names are unchanged (its Linux builds run on the game's floor, glibc 2.28); a self-hosted server deploys the wheel into the release layout, `citar-lab` and `requirements.txt` are gone, the lab runs inside `citar.service`, which now carries the lab's lower CPU and IO weights, the backup covers the whole state directory, and a rollback across a migration restores the deploy's backup; release builds report their label (`v0.1.6`) in `citar doctor` and `citar-helper --version`.

**Checks:** `check_links.py --strict`, `mkdocs build --strict`, the wiki committed, and a stale-terms scan (`scripts/ci/stale_terms.py`, 5-12, run by test.yml's lint job) that refuses, outside the CHANGELOG's history and the Compatibility notes, the phrases of the old routes (the `git archive` deploy, `requirements.txt` installs, the game's Homebrew formula, `citar-lab`, `python -m citar.server` in a unit, the PyPI sdist in a manifest).

## P5.17 CI changes

| Workflow | Change |
|---|---|
| `package.yml` (new, 5-02b) | P5.3-P5.10: the wheels, the sdist and its build, the helpers and the manifest, the bundle and installer, the images, the install scripts, `assemble`, the package managers; `pull_request`, `push: main`, `workflow_dispatch`, `workflow_call`; every artifact `pkg-...` |
| `test.yml` | `workflow_call` with `mode`/`version`/`date`; `release.py prepare` first in every job; concurrency `tests-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}`; the Linux suite on **3.11, 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14** (the classifiers claim 3.14); `package`, `installer` and `docker` moved to package.yml; lint runs `release.py check`, `check_workflows.py` and (5-12) `stale_terms.py`; the `requirements.txt` step goes with the file (5-10a); its artifact names unchanged (`ext-<os>`, `helper-<os>`) |
| `release.yml` | 5-02a reduces it to its gate (one job that fails with the Phase 5 notice; no build, no publishing job, no environment) until 5-09 writes P5.11; the old jobs' work moves to package.yml (5-02b to 5-08) and the new tag-only jobs (5-09) |
| `rust.yml` | Its path filters gain `package.yml` (xtask reads it); lint runs `cargo xtask licenses --check` after `cargo fetch --locked` |
| `helper.yml` | Its `artifact` matrix stays equal to package.yml's `helpers` (the names test); its Linux builds move into the manylinux_2_28 containers with the glibc check (P5.4.1) |
| every workflow | Every remote `uses:` pinned to a commit SHA with its version in a comment (5-02a); Dependabot's existing weekly `github-actions` group moves the pins |
| `determinism.yml`, `nightly.yml`, `docs.yml` | The pins only; never dispatched by Phase 5 |

**`scripts/ci/check_workflows.py`** (5-02a, extended in 5-03 and 5-09; PyYAML, which the dev extra brings through mkdocs and the lint job installs) proves, before any dispatch and on every pull request:
1. every workflow parses (PyYAML reads the `on` key as `True`; the script knows) and sets `permissions: contents: read` at the top;
2. a job **publishes** if it names an environment, asks for `id-token`, `pages`, `packages`, `contents`, `attestations`, `deployments` or `pull-requests` write, reads a secret other than `GITHUB_TOKEN`, uses a publishing action (`pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish`, `softprops/action-gh-release`, `actions/attest-build-provenance`, `actions/deploy-pages`, `docker/login-action`) or runs a publishing command (`gh release`, `twine upload`, `docker push`, `imagetools create`, `git push`, a mutating `gh api`); every publishing job is either in release.yml, carrying the tag guard as a conjunct of its job-level `if:`, or in docs.yml, carrying 2-06b's guard to `refs/heads/main` (the site and the wiki, published from main only) either on the job or, as the `wiki` job has it, on exactly the steps that read a secret or push (so a branch run still checks that the pages generate); neither guard may be weakened by `||`, `always()`, `!cancelled()` or `failure()`, and no other workflow publishes;
3. package.yml and test.yml publish nothing and name no environment and no secret;
4. a called workflow's concurrency group contains `github.workflow` and differs from its caller's;
5. no `run:` block interpolates `inputs.*`, `github.event.inputs.*` or `github.head_ref` (they reach shells through `env:`);
6. every maturin-action step pins `maturin-version: ${{ env.MATURIN_VERSION }}`;
7. every job of package.yml and test.yml runs `release.py prepare` after the checkout and before its first build or test step;
8. release.yml has no build step (cargo, maturin, PyInstaller, ISCC, `docker build`);
9. no upload path can include a `*.key` file or the throwaway key folder;
10. release.yml calls neither nightly.yml nor docs.yml, and no workflow runs `build_wiki.py --push`;
11. **every remote `uses:` in every workflow is pinned** to a 40-character commit SHA with a version comment (`@<sha> # v7.0.1`); local `./` references are exempt; **every image** a workflow builds or runs in is pinned by a `sha256` digest (a `docker://` step, a job's `container:` and `services:`, and maturin-action's `container:` on Linux, each matrix leg's), and a maturin-action step whose `manylinux` asks for a container without naming one (it would take the action's own image, by a moving tag) fails (added in 5-02b's fix round); an image a script runs (`docker run "$IMAGE"`, whose words no check can read) is written where this rule reads it, a matrix leg's `container` or `image` or a matrix or `env:` key ending in either (`FEDORA_IMAGE`), and needs its digest too (added in 5-03);
12. **a job that calls a workflow** (`uses: ./.github/workflows/...`) passes no `secrets:` (never `inherit`) and sets `permissions: contents: read`;
13. **artifact names:** package.yml uploads only `pkg-...`, release.yml only `rel-...`, no other workflow either prefix; the upload names, matrix-expanded, are unique across test.yml, package.yml and release.yml; every download in package.yml and release.yml names one artifact exactly or the pattern `pkg-*` or `rel-*` (never no name, never another pattern);
14. **environments:** release.yml names `pypi` (its `publish-pypi` only) or the tag expression of P5.11, docs.yml names `github-pages` (its `deploy` only), and no other environment or workflow appears; a literal `environment: helper-signing` fails;
15. **nothing installs or builds where power is held:** a step that reads a secret other than `GITHUB_TOKEN` runs no package tool (cargo, pip, pipx, npm, npx, uv, maturin) and is not a `uses:` step; in release.yml, a job that reads such a secret or holds `id-token: write` or any write permission runs no package tool or `docker build`, its `uses:` are only on an allow-list (`actions/checkout` with `persist-credentials: false`, `actions/download-artifact`, `actions/upload-artifact` and the publishing actions of rule 2), and no `setup-*` action;
16. **release.yml's order and permissions** (5-09): the `needs` graph and each tag-only job's permissions are P5.11's table; `publish-pypi` and `publish-image` need `attest-files`, which needs `check-signature`; `image-tags` needs `github-release`, which creates a draft first; `attest-files`' `subject-path` covers the folder `pkg-release-assets` is downloaded into and the signature.

`tests/test_workflows.py` runs it on the real files and on a planted fixture for each rule, each of which must fail naming its job and rule.

**As built in 5-02a: the workflow guard, the pins and test.yml's call** (Appendix F, 5-02a). One agent, from 2026-10-19 06:46 to 08:11 (-04:00), on branch `p-5-02a` from `8d97d30`.

- **What was built.**
  - `scripts/ci/check_workflows.py`: rules 1 to 15 above. It reads the files with PyYAML, which the lint job installs and `uvicorn[standard]` brings to every suite environment. Each finding names its file, its job where it has one, and its rule. Exit 0 when every rule holds, 1 on a finding, 2 when it cannot run.
  - `tests/test_workflows.py` (53 tests, 58 after the fix round): the tree passes, and so does a small package.yml fixture in 5-02b's shape (`workflow_call` inputs, the `github.workflow` group, prepare, maturin pinned, `pkg-` names, downloads by name and `pkg-*`). Each rule fails on a copy of the tree with one thing planted. The expression reader, the shell reader, the matrix and the upload paths have tests of their own, and so has the pin tool, offline.
  - `scripts/ci/action_pins.py` (standard library; `gh api` GETs only), with three commands:
    - `list` prints every remote `uses:`;
    - `pin [--write]` resolves a tag (an annotated one dereferenced) or else a branch to its commit, and writes the most precise version tag on that commit as the comment;
    - `verify [--out]` asks GitHub that each SHA is a commit of its repository and that the commented tag, dereferenced, is that commit.
  - **Every remote `uses:` in the seven workflows is pinned:** 99 references to 13 commits in 13 repositories, each the commit its major tag named on 2026-10-19. They are `actions/checkout` v7.0.1, `setup-python` v7.0.0, `setup-node` v7.1.0, `upload-artifact` v7.0.2, `download-artifact` v8.0.2, `upload-pages-artifact` v5.0.0, `configure-pages` v6.0.0, `deploy-pages` v5.0.1, `Swatinem/rust-cache` v2.9.2, `taiki-e/install-action` v2.87.29, `PyO3/maturin-action` v1.51.0, `docker/setup-buildx-action` v4.4.1 and `docker/build-push-action` v7.4.0. Dependabot's `github-actions` group moves them.
  - **release.yml** is one job, `phase-5`, whose one step prints `::error title=release packaging is Phase 5::...` and exits 1, on a `v*` tag and on a dispatch alike. It has no checkout, no build, no publishing job and no environment. The old jobs went with it: the literal `environment: helper-signing`, the publishing under `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/')` alone, and `cargo xtask` beside the key. Run over the workflows as they were at `8d97d30`, the checker names 171 problems, these among them.
  - **test.yml:**
    - `workflow_call` with `mode`, `version` and `date`. The workflow's `env` carries them as `RELEASE_MODE` (`ci` by default), `RELEASE_VERSION` and `RELEASE_DATE`.
    - Every job's first step after its checkout and interpreter is `python scripts/release.py prepare --mode "$RELEASE_MODE" ${RELEASE_VERSION:+--version "$RELEASE_VERSION"} ${RELEASE_DATE:+--date "$RELEASE_DATE"}` in bash. The two jobs without setup-python (frontend, docker) use `python3`, Ubuntu 24.04's 3.12. build-ext now sets up Python before its toolchain, so prepare comes before every build step.
    - The group is `tests-${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}`.
    - The Linux suite gains Python 3.14.
    - lint installs PyYAML and runs `check_workflows.py` after `release.py check`.
    - The artifact names are unchanged.
  - `citar/web/tests/helper.test.mjs` reads helper.yml's `artifact` matrix in place of release.yml's `build-helper`: five lines, five targets, the card's names and the manifest's table. helper.yml's and `helperrules.js`'s comments follow. CONTRIBUTING names both scripts and `pin --write`; the wiki is regenerated (never pushed).
- **Decided beyond the text.**
  - **What publishes** (rule 2) is wider than the design's list:
    - any `write` permission, or `write-all`;
    - `actions/attest`, and `docker/build-push-action` with `push`;
    - gh's other writes: `workflow run`, and changes to `pr`, `issue`, `secret`, `variable`, `label`, `repo`, `run` and `cache` (`gh release view`, `list` and `download` are reads);
    - `docker build --push`, `podman push`, `npm`/`cargo`/`uv`/`poetry publish` and `maturin publish|upload`.
    A command is found past its global options (`git -C dir push`, `gh -R o/r release create`).
  - **A guard** is a conjunct of the condition's top-level `&&` chain, with parentheses flattened:
    - `(guard) || x` fails, and so does a guard inside a `!`;
    - `always()`, `cancelled()` and `failure()` fail anywhere in the condition; `success()` and further conjuncts are allowed;
    - the `startsWith` literal must begin `refs/tags/v`;
    - an `if:` with text around its `${{ }}`, or one the reader cannot parse, fails.
    The tag expression of rule 14 is read the same way: `(guards && '<name>') || ''`, with the name last.
  - **The other rules, beyond their wording:**
    - Rule 3 counts `GITHUB_TOKEN` too ("no secret"), whether written `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` or `github.token`, and refuses a `workflow_call` that declares secrets.
    - Rule 4 also checks a called workflow's job-level groups. "Differs from its caller's" is judged as the caller runs it: `github.workflow` is read as the caller's `name`. A workflow-level group has no job, so that finding names the file and the rule.
    - Rule 5 also refuses the pull request's title, body, head ref and label, and the head commit's message and author.
    - Rule 6 also requires the workflow or the job to define `MATURIN_VERSION`.
    - Rule 7: only the checkout, `actions/setup-*`, `docker/setup-*`, the caches, `taiki-e/install-action` and `download-artifact` may come between the checkout and prepare. Prepare carries no `if:` and no `continue-on-error`.
    - Rule 8 adds `python -m build`, `pip wheel`, `buildx build`, maturin-action and build-push-action.
    - Rule 9 reads "can include" as any of: a last path part that matches a key's name (`*`, `*.key`, `update-signing.key`); a `throwaway` folder; the runner's temporary folder whole; a path with `..`; the runner's own folders. So rule 9 already covers 5-03's key folder, and 5-03 only keeps its uploads outside it.
    - Rule 10 adds `gh workflow run nightly|docs` in release.yml.
    - Rule 11 reads the `uses:` lines as text, since YAML drops comments. It refuses a `uses:` written where its line cannot be read (a flow mapping), and a `docker://` image without a `sha256` digest.
    - Rule 13 expands each matrix as GitHub does (the product, `exclude`, then `include`) and fills in `matrix.*`, `runner.os` (from `runs-on`) and `strategy.job-index`. It counts `upload-pages-artifact` (default name `github-pages`) for the prefixes, and an unnamed upload is `artifact`.
    - Rule 14 accepts the tag expression only for `helper-signing` (`TAG_ENVIRONMENTS`), the one name P5.11 writes that way and P5.18.2 allows besides `github-pages` and `pypi`. A misspelt name would otherwise pass and create a new environment, with no reviewer and no secret, at Phase 6's tag push.
    - Rules 8 and 15 refuse a run step in a shell the check cannot read: anything but bash, sh, pwsh, powershell and cmd (`shell: python`, a `perl {0}` template, an expression), taken from the step's `shell:` or else the job's or workflow's `defaults.run.shell`. That is a rule 8 finding anywhere in release.yml, and a rule 15 finding in a step reading a secret or in a powered release.yml job.
  - **Shell is read word by word.** Quotes are removed, comments dropped, and commands split at `;`, `&`, `&&`, `|`, `||`, `(`, `)`, backticks and newlines, each remembering the operator that ended it; a `${{ }}` is a placeholder word. Redirections (`2>&1`, `&>log`) stay with their command, and launchers are seen through (`sudo` with its options, `env`, `timeout`, `nice`); `pip3`, `pip3.12` and `pip-3.12` are pip. A heredoc's body is data (`cat > f <<EOF`, `python3 - <<PY`), except when a shell reads it as its script: `bash <<EOF`, `bash -s <<'EOF'`, or `cat <<EOF | bash` through a pipeline (not `bash x.sh <<EOF`, whose stdin is data). Also read as scripts: `bash -c`/`-lc`, `eval`, a here-string given to a shell (`bash <<< '...'`), and the words after `pwsh -Command` (or `-c`) and `cmd /c`. A `<<` inside quotes or a comment starts no heredoc. So docs.yml's notice, `echo "... 'python scripts/build_wiki.py --push' ..."`, is text and not a push. A script written to a file and run later is not read; the permission and secret rules are the barrier there.
  - **Rule 5 covers every workflow** (its text says "no `run:` block"). So rust.yml's chaos step and nightly.yml's four chaos and soak steps, which wrote `${{ inputs.* || default }}` into their scripts, now pass each input through the step's `env:`. The defaults and commands are unchanged. P5.17's table says nightly.yml takes "the pins only"; this is the one exception.
  - **S1c:** the package's venv is Windows' Python 3.14.6, as every Phase 3 to 5 venv has been. So S1 is the 3.14 run, and no second venv was made.

  2. `action_pins.py verify`: 99 references to 13 pins in 13 repositories, 0 problems (`gate2.log`, and the rows in `gate2-pins.json`). Each SHA is a commit of its repository, and its commented tag is that commit.
  3. The seven workflows parse, `cargo xtask check` is all clear, and `helper.test.mjs` passes 4 of 4 reading helper.yml. In copies of the four files it reads (`gate3/`), it fails with a sixth matrix line, with a sixth name on the card, and with a matrix line out of step with the manifest.

Locally, test.yml's two prepare commands were run as the runners run them, from the YAML, in bash, in a scratch clone: Git Bash with the venv's Python, and WSL with Python 3.12 (`prepare-windows.log`, `wsl.log`).

- **Fix round** (2026-10-19 08:36 to 09:25, -04:00). The review found five minor gaps. Each was confirmed by a plant that gave 0 findings, and each is fixed:
  - **Heredocs a shell reads.** The shell reader dropped every heredoc body, so `bash <<'EOF'` or `bash -s <<EOF` hid its commands from rules 2, 3, 8 and 15. A heredoc's body is now read when a shell runs it.
  - **Shells the check cannot read.** A `shell: python` step's commands are unreadable. Such a step is now refused: in release.yml under rule 8, and under rule 15 in a step reading a secret or in a powered job.
  - **Versioned pip.** `pip3.12 install` passed rule 15; `pip3.12` and its kind are now pip.

  - `sudo`'s options (`sudo -E pip`) hid the command;
