## P5.18 Cross-cutting rules

### P5.18.1 Safety on this laptop (binding on every package)

P4.7.1, unchanged, with Phase 5's additions:
1. **Never** install a system service, a scheduled task or a startup entry on Windows, change a system or security setting (firewall, Defender, UAC, any registry value), or elevate to administrator. **Never install anything on Windows:** pip only into the package's own venv under `C:/dev/venv`; no Inno Setup, Docker, Podman, Scoop, winget package or Homebrew on Windows (those run on CI runners only; WSL's apt installs are rule 8's).
2. **Never** touch the owner's real CITAR data or configuration: every server, CLI, QA, smoke, bundle or install-script run uses its own data directory, database and configuration (`CITAR_DATA_DIR` and the state variables under the package's target folder), never `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`, the main checkout's `config/`, port 8799 or `C:/dev/citar-play`. Every helper run uses an isolated `--home`; a release or packaged helper (which has no development guard, so its home would default to the real `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR`) runs on the laptop only through the gate harness `scripts/ci/gate_run.py helper` (5-03), which refuses a run without `--home` under the package's target folder and sets `CITAR_DATA_DIR` there too; S10 records `helper.json` and the machine key around every such gate.
3. **Installer and install-script tests** run into temporary prefixes and never install into the owner's real Python, PATH, Start menu or registry: `install.ps1` only with `-Prefix`, `-BinDir` under the package's target folder and `-NoPath`, through `gate_run.py install-ps1` (5-07); `install.sh` only with `HOME`, `--prefix` and `--bin-dir` in a temporary folder and `--no-profile`; the Inno installer is checked by a generated-script test locally and run only on a CI runner.
4. **Never** generate a release signing private key into the repository or commit any private key; `crates/citar-jobs/update-keys.pub` stays empty until the owner commits the public key; throwaway keys live in temporary folders outside any git working tree (`keygen` refuses otherwise); S12 scans.
5. **Never** connect a test helper to `citar.jimmieprodgers.com` or any non-local server; downloads in tests come from loopback servers (`CITAR_RELEASE_URL`).
6. **Never** load, unload or swap an LM Studio model or change its settings (Phase 5 has no real-model gate).
7. **Never touch the VPS:** no SSH, no copy, no deploy. The deploy is Phase 6's, with the owner's go-ahead.
8. **WSL** is `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u claude`; root (`-u root`) only inside WSL, where a gate needs it (apt installs, the staging run), each recorded in `ops/p5-notes.md`; never the WSL password; builds in `~/` from a `git bundle` or clone, never under `/mnt/c`; **a WSL script never acts on the folder it starts in** (wsl.exe starts in Windows' current folder, for these sessions the main checkout: 5-04's incident, P5.5's as-built note): `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -u claude --cd /home/claude`, the script copied to WSL's own disk and run from there, `cd "$HOME"` its first command, then before any command that writes, `pwd -P` and `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` checked equal to its clone (stop otherwise), and every checkout named explicitly (`release.py ... --root "$CLONE"`, `git -C "$CLONE"`); no quote inside a `${VAR:?message}` message (it opens a string that swallows the script's next lines); `wsl ... -- bash -c '...$var...'` expands `$var` in an outer shell first, so such commands go in a script file; a server inside WSL binds `0.0.0.0` only after `wslinfo --networking-mode` says `nat`, else `127.0.0.1`; the staging run's system changes are removed by its `cleanup`, whose checks are S10's.
9. **The main checkout** is only read by implementers and reviewers; merge agents merge and push from it as in Phase 4, never staging, committing or reverting the owner's `.claude/launch.json`. Worktree agents never push.
10. **Never** push a tag, create a release, publish to PyPI or ghcr, push to `jprodgers/homebrew-citar` or `jprodgers/scoop-citar`, submit to winget, publish the wiki (`build_wiki.py --push`, which uses this machine's own git credentials), create an environment, a secret or a repository setting, add the `nightly` label to PR #5, or dispatch nightly.yml or docs.yml.
11. **Every server on Windows binds loopback explicitly:** server mode's default host is `0.0.0.0` (`citar/settings.py:265`), and a new, unsigned executable listening on all interfaces raises Windows Defender Firewall's prompt, whose dismissal adds block rules (a firewall change, rule 1). So every server run on Windows, server mode included, sets `CITAR_HOST=127.0.0.1` and a free port other than 8799, through `scripts/ci/gate_run.py server` (5-03, used from 5-05), which refuses to start one without them. S10 records the firewall rules naming `citar` or `python` (`Get-NetFirewallRule`, read only) before and after.

### P5.18.2 Dispatch rules

- **Who may dispatch release.yml:** the merge agent of 5-09 after its push (the first dry run); the merge agent of any later package that changed release.yml, package.yml or test.yml, after its push; and 5-13 (its implementation agent on the pushed head; its merge agent again after pushing any fix). Nobody else. test.yml's own `workflow_dispatch` may be used by the same agents when PR #5's run for the same head is missing or was cancelled.
- **Only** `gh workflow run release.yml --ref v0.1.6 -f version=0.1.6` (the owner's version number if they choose another; `release-dry-run.yml` instead if P5.11's fallback is taken), on the **pushed** `v0.1.6`; never a tag ref, never another branch.
- **Before every dispatch:** S8 at the pushed head (`check_workflows.py` green: no publishing job can run outside a tag push, and no environment but `pypi` is named outside the tag expression); `gh api repos/jprodgers/CITAR/environments --jq '[.environments[].name]'` is exactly `["github-pages","pypi"]`, or `["github-pages","helper-signing","pypi"]` once the owner has made `helper-signing` (the owner's answer of 2026-10-19: the key, the environment and its secret may be set up at any time; any other environment stops the phase); `git ls-remote --tags origin` and `gh release list` recorded.
- **Watched to the end:** `gh run watch <id> --exit-status`; afterwards the same three readings, unchanged (a new environment, tag or release stops everything and is reported to the owner).
- **Bounded:** at most three fix-and-push rounds per dispatching agent, each fix through the local gates first; after the third failure the agent stops and reports the failing job's log.
- **package.yml at a merge push** runs by itself (PR #5's `pull_request` event): the merge agents of 5-02a to 5-12 watch that run and test.yml's for their push (`gh run list --workflow package.yml --branch v0.1.6 --event pull_request`), with the same three-round bound. That is not a dispatch.

### P5.18.3 Disk

- **Every package builds its own inputs from its own head**, under `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-xx`: no gate uses a wheel, helper or bundle from an earlier package (each merge deletes them, and a later package's Python changes, 5-05's among them, would make them wrong anyway). maturin's `--out`, PyInstaller's `--distpath`/`--workpath`, sdist builds, scratch clones (5-01's, 5-11's and 5-13's bumped ones), deploy bundles, staging logs (`<target>/staging/`) and podman build contexts are all under that target folder. **Nothing builds into the OneDrive checkout** (no `target/`, `dist/`, `build/` or `installer/output/` there; `git status --ignored` checked at the end).
- **Venvs** are `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx` and, when a gate needs another (the smoke's 3.11 and 3.14, S1c's 3.14, the bundle's 3.12, a bumped clone's), `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx-<purpose>`; pip installs only into them.
- **The merge step deletes** `C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-xx` and every `C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx*` after the push, and lists `C:/dev/target` and `C:/dev/venv` to show nothing of the package remains; reviewers reuse the package's own target folder and venvs and never make others.
- **WSL:** one clone per package (`~/p5/p-5-xx`, removed at its merge); one shared cargo target `~/target/citar-p5`, kept between packages for its cache; podman's images and containers removed at each package's end except the base images (`python:3.12-slim-trixie`, the manylinux and maturin containers). **5-13 removes all of it**: `~/target/citar-p5`, `~/p5`, and podman's storage (`podman system reset`), recording `du -sh` of each and `df` of WSL's root before and after. The Phase 4 WSL targets (`~/target/citar-p-4-*`), the old `C:/dev/venv/p-2-*` to `p-4-*` venvs and the stray `target/` in the main checkout are the orchestrator's or owner's to remove; packages leave them alone.

### P5.18.4 CI parity and CI-only gates

- **Locally:** Windows x64 natively (the Windows wheel, bundle, helper, `install.ps1`), Linux x64 in WSL (the manylinux_2_28 x86_64 wheel and helper through the containers under podman, the image under podman, `install.sh`, the deploy and the staging), Python 3.11, 3.12 and 3.14 on Windows, 3.12 in WSL, `--locked` everywhere.
- **CI-only, named in each package:** macOS arm64 and x64, Linux arm64, Inno Setup's compile and the silent install, Scoop, winget, Homebrew, Docker's buildx and both native images, the attestation and publishing steps (tag-only, never run in Phase 5).
- **What only a release run exercises, and how each is covered earlier:**
  - *The version bump.* A dry run bumps every runner's checkout (P5.2), so the whole suite first runs bumped there. 5-01 rehearses it locally first (the whole S1 suite, with `cargo xtask develop`'s helpers and the update end-to-end tests, and S7's nextest in a bumped clone), and 5-13 repeats it at the exit.
  - *The shared artifact namespace.* Only a release run holds test.yml's and package.yml's artifacts together; rule 13 (the `pkg-`/`rel-` prefixes, named downloads, unique names) makes a collision a check failure on every pull request.
  - *The calling jobs.* `secrets:` and permissions on the two `uses:` jobs are rule 12's.
  - *The tag-only jobs* are the only release-only code: rules 2, 11 and 14 to 16 and the dry run's summary prove them, and none runs before Phase 6.
  Everything else in a release run is package.yml's and test.yml's, which run on every pull request.

### P5.18.5 The standard gates

Every package's gates include, besides its own (P4.7.5's, renumbered where Phase 5 adds):
- **S1** the Python suite (`python -m unittest discover -s tests`) in the package's venv, with `cargo xtask develop` when Rust changed; its count recorded. **S1b** the suite on Python 3.12 under `TZ=UTC0` (`C:/dev/venv/py312-check` with `CITAR_EXT_DIR` at the package's extension). From 5-02a, **S1c** the suite on Windows' Python 3.14 (`C:/dev/venv/p-5-xx-py314`) when the package changes Python.
- **S2** ruff (and `sync_requirements.py --check` until 5-10a retires it). **S3** `audit_routes.py --strict`. **S4** `check_links.py --strict`. **S5** when `docs/` changed: `mkdocs build --strict` and `build_wiki.py` (never `--push`) with `wiki/` committed. **S6** the front-end checks.
- **S7** when Rust (xtask included) changed: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, nextest, the doctests, `cargo xtask check`, the refcheck run, `cargo golden check`, the `jobs` set in `release`.
- **S8** when a workflow or a script one runs changed: every workflow parses; `scripts/ci/check_workflows.py` (from 5-02a); `cargo xtask check`; `node --test citar/web/tests/helper.test.mjs`.
- **S9** the package's Linux half in WSL, as its gates name it (Rust in WSL when citar-helper or citar-jobs changed).
- **S10** the owner's data and the machine are as they were, before and after: P4.7.5's list (the two `servers.json` hashes, `citar.db` and `secret_key` mtimes, `helper.json`, machine keys, `config/worker.json`, no helper, job-host or server process, no `citar-helper` service, Run value or task, no `CITAR_*` variable, the main checkout showing only `.claude/launch.json`), plus: the user's PATH value (HKCU `Environment`) hashed unchanged; the firewall rules naming `citar` or `python` listed unchanged (P5.18.1 rule 11); no `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CITAR\app` or `\bin`; no `{9C1D2A54-...}_is1` uninstall key; no Start menu `CITAR` folder; in WSL no `~/.local/bin/citar*` or `~/.local/share/citar-app` for `claude`, no `citar` user, `/opt/citar*`, `/var/lib/citar*`, `/etc/citar`, citar unit or staging slice, nginx (once a package has installed it) stopped and disabled, no running container; on GitHub the environments, tags and releases unchanged.
- **S11** `python scripts/release.py check` (from 5-01).
- **S12** where signing is touched: no private key in the tree, the ignored files or the package's history (Phase 4's key scan), `update-keys.pub` 0 bytes.
- **CI-only** gates as named per package; nobody dispatches anything outside P5.18.2.

CI timing lessons stand: speed budgets times three under `CI`, durations by `perf_counter`, `TZ=UTC0`, `fsutil.newest_first`, waits on the condition with a deadline (never sleep-then-assert), Windows runners elevated as `runneradmin`, macOS limits soft.

### P5.18.6 Process

As Phase 4: a worktree per package (`.claude/worktrees/p-5-xx`, branch `p-5-xx`, from the previous package's merge); `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` before every cargo or maturin command; files written with the Write tool (Git Bash mangles backslashes in heredocs); small commits ending with the Co-Authored-By line; never `ops/`, `saves/` or `.claude/launch.json`. Each package ends with its gates, a review and a fix round, its as-built note with its start and end times, and handoff notes in `ops/p5-notes.md` (gitignored), apt installs in WSL included.

---

## P5.19 Decisions taken in this design

1. **One release build, `package.yml`,** run on every pull request and called by release.yml, which builds nothing itself.
2. **Cargo's version is the source;** `release.py` checks and bumps the rest; a dry run bumps its runner's checkout, so it builds exactly what a tag would.
3. **The label** is `v<version>` on a tag, `v<version>-dryrun+<sha>` on a dry run, `ci+<sha>` otherwise, on every release build.
4. **Wheels are built natively** on five runners with maturin 1.15.0: abi3-py311, manylinux_2_28, macOS 11; each smoke-tested on its runner on 3.11 and 3.14, and all five must report one build and play one seeded lab job to one result hash.
5. **The sdist is built into a wheel as a user would** and smoke-tested.
6. **The release's tested dependency set is an asset** (`server-constraints`), used by the image and the VPS.
7. **The helper ships twice:** unversioned self-updating binaries (the download links, the manifest, `--worker`) and versioned `packaged` archives (Homebrew, Scoop, winget).
8. **A dry run proves the signing path with a throwaway key** and two verifiers; the real key signs only a tag, under the environment's reviewer, and must match a committed public key; the signing job builds and installs nothing (a prebuilt xtask, the runner's Python), and its environment is the tag expression, so no dry run can name it.
9. **Every publishing job is tag-push-only** (`event_name == 'push'` and a `refs/tags/v` ref), proven by `check_workflows.py`; every action in every workflow is pinned by SHA; the called workflows get no secrets; package.yml's artifacts are `pkg-` and downloaded by name.
10. **Attestations only for a tag,** the files' made before anything is published and the image's after its push; the dry run shows their subjects.
11. **The image pushed is the image tested** (saved, loaded, pushed by architecture, joined); both architectures built natively on `python:3.12-slim-trixie`; `:latest` and `:<major.minor>` move only after the GitHub release is published.
12. **The GitHub release is a draft until whole,** then published, so `latest` never lacks a helper.
13. **The install scripts install wheels only** and say so on an unsupported platform (a free-threaded Python included); `--worker` installs the self-updating helper with its checksum; the helper's Linux builds share the game's glibc 2.28 floor.
14. **The Homebrew tap ships only `citar-helper`;** the game's formula is deleted.
15. **Third-party notices** are generated by xtask and travel with every binary.
16. **The server runs from a release layout** (`/opt/citar/releases/<v>`, `current`), units in the state directory, one unit definition carrying the lab's old CPU and IO weights, no `citar-lab`, no `requirements.txt`.
17. **`citar-deploy` scripts install, cutover, rollback and uncutover,** each with `--plan`; the laptop fetches and verifies the wheel; the snapshot is taken with the service stopped just before the switch, and a failed release that migrated is rolled back with that snapshot; the box's other sites are checked before and after, and only CITAR's own failure rolls back automatically.
18. **The staging run** reproduces the VPS's layout and limits in WSL (the CPU scaled to the VPS's, the other sites' memory and CPU held, swappiness 10, the job host sized for one CPU and 961 MB, every limit probed), including the cutover from 0.1.5, and Phase 6 repeats it before the real deploy.
19. **The bundle's job host and the MCP command** are fixed for installed copies; `doctor` proves the job host everywhere.
20. **The Linux suite runs 3.14 too.**

## P5.20 Risks

1. **CI-only platforms fail late.** macOS, arm64, Inno, Scoop, winget and Homebrew are first seen at a merge push. Mitigation: package.yml on PR #5 at every merge push, watched; each package's local stand-ins; the three-round bound with a report.
2. **The runners change** (Inno Setup absent from an image, winget missing, a macOS image retired). Mitigation: each tool found or installed in its job, its version printed; a missing optional check (winget install) reported, never silently skipped.
3. **A release run partly publishes.** Mitigation: the signature and both verifiers, then the files' attestations, all before anything irreversible; PyPI's `skip-existing` for a re-run; the release a draft until whole; the moving image tags last; RELEASING.md says what to re-run at each point.
4. **The VPS's memory and CPU.** The server and its lab now share one cgroup. Mitigation: measured in staging under limits proven to bind, with the other sites' memory and CPU held and the job host sized as on the box; `MemoryHigh` sized to the server's peak plus the host's figure; `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` from the response-time gate; `OOMScoreAdjust` keeps the other sites safe.
5. **Dependency drift between the test and the deploy.** Mitigation: the constraints asset.
6. **The environment rule.** Mitigation: by construction, not by observation: every environment but `pypi` is the tag expression, empty on a dispatch (rule 14); every dispatch still reads the environments before and after, and a change stops the phase.
7. **Actions may refuse an empty environment name.** Then the first dry run (5-09) fails at startup, naming and creating nothing. Mitigation: P5.11's fallback, a dispatch-only `release-dry-run.yml`, made in 5-09's fix rounds.
8. **Staging's CPU scaling is an estimate** (about 60% of a laptop core for the VPS's vCPU). Mitigation: the figure is an argument and recorded; Phase 6 compares a lab game's speed on the box with staging's and records the ratio before any later rehearsal.
9. **A tag-only job runs for the first time on the real tag.** Mitigation: rules 2 and 11 to 16, the dry run's summary of what each would do, the order that puts every irreversible step after signing and attestation, and RELEASING.md's re-run table.
10. **The WSL disk** grows with images and targets. Mitigation: one target, prunes, the exit's `podman system reset` and removals, measured.
11. **winget** may never merge. Mitigation: nothing depends on it; the other routes cover Windows.

## P5.21 Questions for the owner, and what was settled without asking

**Two questions** (neither blocks a package: the design takes the default and makes the other answer one argument):
1. **The release number:** 0.1.6, as the branch and every design say, or 0.2.0 (plan §8's aside: "It might deserve the number 0.2.0")? **Answered (owner, 2026-10-19): 0.1.6.**
2. **The update-signing key before the 0.1.6 tag?** A helper trusts only the keys it was built with, so if 0.1.6 ships with `update-keys.pub` empty, no helper installed from 0.1.6 can ever update itself: each must be replaced by hand at 0.1.7. Default: the release proceeds either way (P5.4.2's first rule), and RELEASING.md's step 1 asks the owner to make `helper-signing` and commit the public key before tagging. **Answered (owner, 2026-10-19): yes.** The owner makes the key, `helper-signing` and its secret now; the public key line is committed to `update-keys.pub` in Phase 6 (RELEASING.md step 1), since Phase 5's gates (S12, 5-03 gate 3) hold that file empty.

**Settled without asking** (the plan, the owner's standing rules or common sense decide): the old Homebrew `citar` formula is deleted rather than deprecated (no other users; the CHANGELOG says how to move); Scoop and winget carry the helper as packaged builds; winget's manifests are still generated, validated and submitted in Phase 6 though Microsoft has merged none (minutes of work, no dependency on it); attestations only for a tag (a dry run would publish to a public log); the image moves to Debian 13 (Debian 12 is past regular security support); the Linux suite gains 3.14 (the classifiers claim it); release.yml does not call rust.yml or determinism.yml (Phase 6's bump pull request runs every workflow before the tag); the GitHub release is a draft until whole; the VPS's memory limits and CPU weights come from the staging measurement, not a guess; every action is pinned by SHA (Dependabot's `github-actions` updates are already configured, so no new automation is added); the Linux helpers are built in the manylinux_2_28 containers rather than given a second, higher floor; the image stays on Python 3.12, the VPS's (Dependabot's pull request #6 is superseded).

## P5.22 Work breakdown at a glance

Fifteen packages, strictly in this order, one agent at a time. Each ends with its gates, a review and a fix round, and its as-built note.

| # | Package | Plan §7 Phase 5 part |
|---|---|---|
| 5-01 | Versions, the build label and `release.py` | builds (release prerequisites) |
| 5-02a | The workflow guard, the pins and test.yml's call | builds |
| 5-02b | `package.yml`: the wheel matrix and the sdist | builds |
| 5-03 | The helper's release builds and the signing path | builds |
| 5-04 | Third-party notices | builds |
| 5-05 | The Windows bundle and installer (and the installed copies' fixes) | builds |
| 5-06 | The image from wheels, both architectures | builds |
| 5-07 | The install scripts: wheels only, the helper download | builds |
| 5-08 | Package managers and assembling a release | builds |
| 5-09 | release.yml end to end, the checklist, the first dry run | builds, exit |
| 5-10a | The server's release layout: units, backup, wrappers | VPS |
| 5-10b | `citar-deploy` and the deploy bundle | VPS |
| 5-11 | The staging dry run in WSL | VPS |
| 5-12 | The docs pass | docs |
| 5-13 | Phase 5 exit | exit |

The order: the version and label first, because every build uses them; the workflow guard and the pins before the first new workflow, so package.yml is born under its rules; the wheels before anything built from them (the bundle, the image, the install scripts, the deploy); the helper and notices before the package managers that ship them; `assemble` (5-08) before release.yml (5-09), which only publishes what package.yml assembled; the server's layout (5-10a) before the script that deploys into it (5-10b), and both after the release's asset names are fixed; the staging run when both exist; the docs pass when everything it describes exists; the exit last.

## P5.23 Review log (2026-10-18)

The design's review raised ten major and eleven minor findings. Each was checked against the tree, the plan and the private deploy log (read for facts only); all twenty-one hold, and each is fixed below. Where the fix differs from the one the review proposed, the reason is given.

| # | Finding (severity) | Checked against | Fix, and where | Package |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A dry run creates `helper-signing` only if a skipped job never creates its environment: unverified platform behaviour, and detected only afterwards (major) | release.yml `:275`; P5.11 | Made true by construction: every environment but `pypi` is the tag expression, empty on a dispatch (P5.4.2, P5.11); rule 14 requires the form, with a planted literal failing; fallback if Actions refuses an empty name: a dispatch-only `release-dry-run.yml` (P5.11, risk 7). release.yml is reduced to its gate until 5-09, so its old signing job (a literal environment) is gone before rule 14 exists | 5-02a (rule, stub), 5-09 (jobs) |
| 2 | The signing key reaches every xtask build script and proc macro (major) | `.cargo/config.toml` (`xtask = "run ..."`); release.yml `:297-306` | The signing job builds and installs nothing: it runs the xtask binary package.yml's `helper-manifest` job built (`pkg-xtask-linux-x64`, with its hash) from `release_sign.py` on the runner's own Python, in the one step that holds the secret, checkouts with `persist-credentials: false` (P5.4.2). Not the review's other option of building xtask in an earlier step of the same job: a process an earlier step leaves running can read a later step's environment, and rule 8 forbids builds in release.yml. The `cryptography` verifier moved to a separate job without the secret (`check-signature`) instead of being installed in the signing job. Rule 15 holds all of it | 5-03 (artifact), 5-09 (job), 5-02a (rule) |
| 3 | Publishing and building jobs use moving action references; nothing forbids `secrets: inherit` on the calling jobs (major) | release.yml, every workflow (18 actions, about 120 references); `.github/dependabot.yml` | Rule 11 pins every remote `uses:` in **every** workflow by SHA (not only release.yml and package.yml: test.yml runs inside a release run and docs.yml holds `WIKI_TOKEN`); Dependabot's `github-actions` group already exists, so no new configuration is added; rule 12 for the calling jobs (P5.11, P5.17) | 5-02a |
| 4 | `attest` runs first on the real tag, after PyPI and `:latest` are public, and lacks `packages: write` (major) | P5.11's old table | `attest-files` before any publishing job; `attest-image` after the push with `packages: write` and its login; `:V-<arch>` and `:V` first, `:<major.minor>` and `:latest` in `image-tags` after the release; rule 16; RELEASING.md's re-run table (P5.11, P5.12, P5.15) | 5-09 |
| 5 | A release run shares one artifact namespace between test.yml and package.yml, and the full suite first runs bumped in 5-09's dry run; "nothing only release.yml catches" is false (major) | test.yml `:88-158` (`ext-<os>`, `helper-<os>`); release.yml `:283-286`, `:365` | `pkg-`/`rel-` prefixes and named downloads (P5.3.1, rule 13); 5-01 gate 5 runs the whole S1 suite and S7's nextest in the bumped clone; P5.18.4 rewritten to name what only a release run exercises and what covers each | 5-01, 5-02a, 5-02b |
| 6 | Automatic rollback after a migration crash-loops the old release; the backup is minutes stale; the bad-release gate never migrates; 0.1.5's backup script survives the deploy (major) | `citar/server/boot.py:55-110`; `deploy/citar-backup.service` | `install` records the revision, stops the service, snapshots, switches; a failed health check with a moved revision restores the snapshot before the previous release starts; `rollback` refuses an unknown revision without `--restore-backup`; `citar-backup` and `citar-secrets` installed with the units (P5.13.3); staging plants a migrating upgrade and a migrating failure (P5.14) | 5-10b, 5-11 |
| 7 | Staging measures another machine: the job host sized for WSL, pip and admin scopes outside the slice, no other sites, swappiness and CPU speed, limits never proven (major) | `citar/machine_settings.py:159-186`; WSL's cgroup files and `vm.swappiness` (read); the private log's measurement | All five taken: machine settings set to `host_cpu_defaults(mode="server", cpus=1, ram_mb=961)` with the host's ~240 MB asserted; `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE`/`--slice` for every `systemd-run`; a ballast (figure from the private log, kept in `ops/p5-notes.md`) and a CPU-loaded stand-in; swappiness 10, restored; `CPUQuota` 60% as a recorded argument (no way to measure the VPS's core from here; Phase 6 corrects it); probes that a memory, swap and CPU limit binds (P5.14) | 5-10a, 5-10b, 5-11 |
| 8 | The lab no longer yields CPU or IO to the other sites, and `MemoryHigh` throttles the server first (major) | `git show v0.1.5:deploy/citar-lab.service`; `deploy/citar.service` | `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` on `citar.service` (20 to 50, settled by a stand-in response-time gate in staging); the sizing rule is now server peak plus host figure under `MemoryHigh`; the delegated child cgroup is the fallback, not the default, because `ProtectControlGroups=yes` makes `/sys/fs/cgroup` read-only to the unit and its combination with `Delegate=` on systemd 255 is unchecked (P5.13.2, P5.13.4, P5.14) | 5-10a, 5-11 |
| 9 | Linux helpers need glibc 2.35 while `install.sh` accepts 2.28 (major) | release.yml `:201-202`; helper.yml `:123-124`; `Cargo.toml:78-87` (rustls on ring) | Both Linux helpers built in the manylinux_2_28 containers, with an `objdump -T` check of the highest `GLIBC_` version, in package.yml and helper.yml; one floor for game and helper (P5.4.1, P5.8) | 5-03 |
| 10 | `citar.exe serve` in server mode binds `0.0.0.0` on the laptop and raises the firewall prompt (major) | `citar/settings.py:265` | P5.18.1 rule 11: every Windows server sets `CITAR_HOST=127.0.0.1` through `gate_run.py server`, which refuses otherwise; S10 lists the firewall rules before and after | 5-03 (harness), 5-05 |
| 11 | `assemble`'s one-label check includes the sdist build, which is `unknown` by design (minor) | P5.3.3, P5.10 | The label is checked across wheels, helpers, bundle and images; the sdist build for version, build id, jobs build and result hash, with its label asserted `unknown` (P5.10) | 5-08 |

---

## Appendix F: Phase 5 work breakdown

Each package is sized for one implementation session (a Phase 4 package or smaller), starts from the previous package's merge, and ends with its gates passing, then a review and a fix round. The standard gates S1-S12 (P5.18.5) are named where they apply; every other gate is local and can be rerun by a reviewer, and CI-only gates are named as such, with the agent that proves them (P5.18.2). Every package keeps the safety rules of P5.18.1, builds its inputs from its own head (P5.18.3), and runs release helpers and Windows servers only through `scripts/ci/gate_run.py` once 5-03 has made it.

### 5-01: Versions, the build label and `release.py`

- **Depends on:** nothing (`v0.1.6` at `51ce329`)
- **Estimated size:** ~450 Python (`scripts/release.py`) + ~350 tests + small edits
- **Scope:** P5.2: `scripts/release.py` (`check`, `bump`, `prepare`, `label`, `meta`, `notes`, `assets`) over the checked set (Cargo.toml, the lock's eleven members, the fuzz lock's three, `citar/__init__.py`, `installer/citar.iss`'s `AppVersion` with a new `AppVersionNumeric`, KNOWN_ISSUES' header, the CHANGELOG); `citar.iss`'s comment and `docs/INSTALL.md:33` made version-free; `tests/test_release_script.py`; test.yml's lint job runs `release.py check`; CONTRIBUTING's "Releasing" names `release.py bump` (RELEASING.md is 5-09's); any test that pins the version, found by gate 5, fixed.
- **Gates:**
  1. `release.py check` passes on the tree; each place planted out of step, one at a time, fails naming its file and line.
  2. In a scratch clone (`C:/dev/target/citar-p-5-01/clone`): `bump 0.1.6 --date 2026-11-01` moves every place and nothing else (`git diff --stat` recorded); `cargo metadata --locked` accepts the lock, and rust.yml's `cargo check --locked` of the fuzz crate accepts its lock; `cargo xtask check` passes; `check --version 0.1.6` passes; the CHANGELOG has `## [0.1.6] - 2026-11-01`, a new empty Unreleased and both compare links; a second bump to 0.1.6 and a bump to 0.1.4 are refused.
  3. `prepare`: `--mode tag --version 0.1.5` passes at the head and `--version 0.1.6` fails; `--mode dry-run --version 0.1.6` bumps the scratch clone and passes; `--mode ci` only checks.
  4. `notes 0.1.5` equals what release.yml's awk prints from the same file, byte for byte; `label` and `meta` for each mode (a bad version, `0.1.6;rm`, refused); `assets 0.1.6` and `assets 0.1.6-rc.1` equal P5.10's list (PEP 440 names `0.1.6rc1`).
  5. **The whole suite runs bumped, as every dry run will run it:** in the scratch clone after the bump, with `cargo xtask develop` into the package's target (the dev-guard helpers built, so the update end-to-end tests run rather than skip) and the venv `C:/dev/venv/p-5-01-bumped`: the whole S1 suite, its count and skips equal to S1's at the head; S7's nextest over the workspace (the `jobs` golden set: the build id moved, no bless) and `cargo golden check`.
  6. S1, S1b, S2-S4, S8, S10, S11.

### 5-02a: The workflow guard, the pins and test.yml's call

- **Depends on:** 5-01
- **Estimated size:** ~450 Python (`scripts/ci/check_workflows.py`) + ~350 tests and fixtures + ~150 workflow YAML (about 120 pinned references, test.yml, release.yml's gate)
- **Scope:** P5.17's `check_workflows.py`, rules 1 to 15 (rule 16 is 5-09's), and `tests/test_workflows.py` with a planted fixture per rule; every remote `uses:` in the seven workflows pinned to its commit SHA with a version comment (each tag resolved read-only with `gh api`, annotated tags dereferenced to their commit); **release.yml reduced to its gate** (one job that fails with the Phase 5 notice: no build, no publishing job, no environment, until 5-09), with `helper.test.mjs` reading helper.yml's `artifact` matrix in place of release.yml's `build-helper`; test.yml's `workflow_call` inputs, `release.py prepare` in every job, the concurrency group, the Linux 3.14 leg, and lint running `check_workflows.py`; docs.yml's pins only.
- **Gates:**
  1. `check_workflows.py` passes on the tree; each planted fixture fails naming its job and rule: a publishing step without the guard; the guard without the event check; a guard under `always()`; docs.yml's wiki step without its main guard; an environment named in test.yml; a literal `environment: helper-signing` in release.yml; a called workflow's group without `github.workflow`; `${{ inputs.version }}` in a `run:`; a maturin step without the pin; a build before `prepare`; an upload of `*.key`; `build_wiki.py --push` in a workflow; an action referenced by tag; `secrets: inherit` on a calling job; an unnamed download and a `pattern: helper-*` download in a package.yml fixture; an upload named `pkg-x` in test.yml; `cargo build` in a step that reads a secret; `pip install` in a release.yml job holding `id-token: write`.
  2. Every pin resolves: for each reference, `gh api` confirms the SHA is a commit of that repository and the commented tag points at it (read-only; the list recorded).
  3. Every workflow parses; `cargo xtask check` passes; `node --test citar/web/tests/helper.test.mjs` passes reading helper.yml, and fails with a sixth name planted.
  4. **S1c:** the suite on Windows' Python 3.14 passes before the CI leg is added. Tripwire: a 3.14 failure that needs more than small fixes is recorded in KNOWN_ISSUES with its tests named and skipped on 3.14 only, rather than growing the package.
  5. **CI-only, at the merge push (PR #5's runs, watched by the merge agent):** test.yml's 3.14 leg and every other workflow green on the pinned actions.
  6. S1, S1b, S2-S4, S8, S10, S11.

### 5-02b: `package.yml`: the wheel matrix and the sdist

- **Depends on:** 5-02a
- **Estimated size:** ~300 workflow YAML + ~250 Python (`scripts/ci/smoke_wheel.py`) + ~200 tests + check_dist edits + a small xtask change
- **Scope:** P5.3: `.github/workflows/package.yml` (pinned and `pkg-` named from the start) with `meta`, `wheels` (P5.3.2's table, `CITAR_BUILD_ID`, check_dist per tag), the smoke on 3.11 and 3.14 per runner (P5.3.4: the job host's `ready` and a seeded lab job's result hash), the constraints file, `sdist` and `sdist-build`; test.yml's `package`, `installer` and `docker` jobs moved onto the release wheels, with `twine check`; `cargo xtask check` reads package.yml (the helper rule) and rust.yml's filters list it (the triggers rule); WSL: podman, `python3-venv` and `python3.12-venv` installed as root (recorded).
- **Gates:**
  1. **Windows:** the release wheel built from this head with maturin 1.15.0 (`--release --locked --out` under the target, `CITAR_BUILD_ID` set) passes `check_dist.py` as `cp311-abi3-win_amd64` with `_engine.pyd`; `smoke_wheel.py` passes in fresh venvs on 3.11 and 3.14 (`C:/dev/venv/p-5-02b-py311`, `-py314`): label equal, job host verifiable, the lab job's result hash recorded.
  2. **WSL:** the manylinux_2_28 x86_64 wheel built in the maturin container (`ghcr.io/pyo3/maturin` at `MATURIN_VERSION`, `--compatibility manylinux_2_28`) under podman passes `check_dist.py` with its tag; the smoke passes on 3.12; the lab job's result hash equals Windows'. The constraints file is written from a `[server]` venv.
  3. The sdist passes `check_dist.py sdist`, and a wheel built from it in WSL passes the smoke with `--label unknown` and Windows' result hash.
  4. `check_workflows.py` passes with package.yml in the tree; `cargo xtask check` passes, and fails (`[triggers]`) with package.yml taken out of rust.yml's filters.
  5. **CI-only, at the merge push:** the five wheels on their runners, the smokes on 3.11 and 3.14, `sdist-build`, the moved installer and docker jobs.
  6. S1, S1b, S2-S4, S6, S7, S8, S9 (gates 2-3), S10, S11.

### 5-03: The helper's release builds and the signing path

- **Depends on:** 5-02b
- **Estimated size:** ~200 Rust (`cargo xtask verify-manifest` and its tests) + ~250 workflow YAML + ~300 Python (`verify_manifest.py`, `gate_run.py`, tests) + the names test
- **Scope:** P5.4: package.yml's `helpers` matrix (the self-updating build under its unversioned name and the packaged build in its versioned archive; the Linux builds in the manylinux_2_28 containers with the `objdump -T` glibc check; each run with `--version`, `--self-test` and `--home`; the packaged one's `update check`; the Fedora TLS check; the macOS deployment target; the label) and `helper-manifest` job (manifest, throwaway key in `$RUNNER_TEMP`, signature, both verifiers, the one-byte negative, the `update-keys.pub` check, and the artifacts `pkg-helper-manifest`, `pkg-helper-manifest-dry-run` and `pkg-xtask-linux-x64`); helper.yml's Linux `artifact` builds moved into the same containers; `xtask verify-manifest`; `scripts/ci/verify_manifest.py`; **`scripts/ci/gate_run.py`** with its `helper` and `server` modes (P5.18.1 rules 2 and 11); `helper.test.mjs` reads package.yml's `helpers` matrix and helper.yml's `artifact`; check_workflows rule 9 covers the key folder; the archives carry `LICENSE` and `NOTICE.md` (5-04 adds the third file).
- **Gates:**
  1. **Windows:** both builds of `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` in `release` with the label, run only through `gate_run.py helper` with `--home` under the target: `--version` names V, the label and the target; `--self-test` passes; the packaged build's `update check` says packaged and `notify`, and `updates = "auto"` in its `helper.json` is refused; `cargo nextest run -p citar-helper --features packaged` passes. `gate_run.py` refuses a helper run without `--home` or with one outside the target, and a server without `CITAR_HOST=127.0.0.1` or on port 8799 (its tests).
  2. **WSL:** both linux-x64 builds in the manylinux_2_28 container under podman; `objdump -T` shows no `GLIBC_` version above 2.28, and **a gate that can fail:** the same check refuses a build made on WSL's own Ubuntu 24.04 (glibc 2.39); the archive's listing is exactly its files (three until 5-04); `--self-test` on WSL's glibc.
  3. **Signing, in WSL:** with the linux-x64 build and stand-in copies for the other four names, `helper-manifest` writes the manifest; a key from `keygen` in `/tmp`; `sign-manifest`; `verify-manifest` passes with its public key, fails with another key and on a manifest with one byte changed; `verify_manifest.py` agrees in all three; the release build (no test features) with the empty `update-keys.pub` reports updates unavailable.
  4. **Names:** `helper.test.mjs` passes reading package.yml; a sixth name planted in the matrix, or a matrix line out of step with helper.yml, fails.
  5. `check_workflows.py`: package.yml names no environment or secret; an upload path under `$RUNNER_TEMP/throwaway` is refused.
  6. **CI-only, at the merge push:** the ten builds on five runners, the glibc check on both Linux runners, the `helper-manifest` job, helper.yml's container builds.
  7. S1, S2-S4, S6, S7, S8, S9 (gates 2-3), S10 (helper.json and the machine key unchanged), S11, S12.

- **Estimated size:** ~400 Rust (`cargo xtask licenses`) + the generated file + small pyproject, workflow and NOTICE edits
- **Scope:** P5.5: `cargo xtask licenses [--check]` (the closure of `citar-py` and `citar-helper` over the release targets from `cargo metadata --locked`, the licence texts from the registry sources, the allow-list), `packaging/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` committed; rust.yml's lint runs `--check` after `cargo fetch --locked`; `pyproject.toml`'s `license-files` and `check_dist.py`'s expectations; the packaged archives and the release carry it; NOTICE.md's section.

  5. S4, S7, S8, S9 (gate 1 in WSL), S10, S11.

- **Depends on:** 5-04
- **Estimated size:** ~250 Python (the frozen job host, `-P`, `jobs-host`, doctor's jobs line, `/api/meta`, `installer/python_licenses.py`) + ~40 JS + ~120 spec + ~200 workflow YAML + ~300 tests
- **Scope:** P5.6: `installer/citar.spec` from the installed wheel, PyInstaller run with `-P` from outside the checkout; `installer/python_licenses.py`; `citar/jobs/host.py`'s commands (`python -P -m citar.jobs host`, and the frozen `citar.exe jobs-host`) and the hidden `jobs-host` command; `citar doctor`'s jobs line; `/api/meta`'s `version`, `label`, `mcp_command` and no paths in server mode, and `lobby.js`; `installer/citar.iss` (`AppVersionNumeric`, `BundleDir`); package.yml's `windows` job (the venv from the wheel, PyInstaller, the bundle checks, `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt`, ISCC found or installed, the silent per-user install and uninstall on the runner, the zip); `tests/test_installer_script.py` (the .iss: per user, the three defines, `OutputBaseFilename`, the PATH task unchecked, the uninstall prompt's default No).
- **Gates:**
  1. **The bundle, locally:** the Windows wheel built from this head in a fresh 3.12 venv (`C:/dev/venv/p-5-05-bundle`, pip only into it) with PyInstaller, run as `python -P -m PyInstaller <absolute spec>` from a folder under the target; the bundle under the package's target; `citar.exe --version` (V), `where`, `doctor --quiet` exit 0 with every state variable under the target, its jobs line `verifiable`; `_engine.pyd` in the bundle; the spec's assertion holds (no module from the checkout's `citar/`); `PYTHON-LICENSES.txt` lists the `citar[all]` closure, CPython's licence and the bootloader's, and not PyInstaller or pillow.
  2. **The bundle serves and plays its lab:** through `gate_run.py server`, `citar.exe serve` in server mode with `CITAR_HOST=127.0.0.1`, a free port (never 8799) and an isolated data directory; `/api/meta` names the bundle's `citar.exe mcp` and no path; a lab experiment of one duel game is played by the bundle's job host (a results line appears); stopped, no process left.
  3. **A gate that can fail:** the old host command put back in a bundle makes the jobs line and gate 2's lab fail.
  4. Tests: the host command per kind (checkout, wheel, frozen), with `-P`; a module planted as `citar` in the job host's working directory is not imported (and is, with `-P` removed); `/api/meta` per mode; the lobby's command (`node --test`); the .iss test.
  5. **CI-only, at the merge push:** package.yml's `windows` job: the compile, the silent install, run and uninstall, the zip.

### 5-07: The install scripts: wheels only, the helper download

- **Depends on:** 5-06
- **Estimated size:** ~250 bash + ~200 PowerShell + ~180 workflow YAML + ~250 tests
- **Scope:** P5.8: `install.sh` and `install.ps1` (`--only-binary=citar`, the platform check with its free-threaded case and its test hook, `--prefix`/`-Prefix`, `--bin-dir`/`-BinDir`, `--no-profile`, `-NoPath`, `--from`/`-From` with a file or folder, `--worker`/`-Worker` downloading the self-updating helper with its checksum, `CITAR_RELEASE_URL` with the loopback-only `http` rule, uninstall within the given prefix); package.yml's `install` matrix (ubuntu x64 and arm64, macOS arm64 and x64, Windows) against the built wheels and helpers on a loopback server; `gate_run.py install-ps1`.
- **Gates:**
  1. **WSL, as `claude`, everything in a temporary `HOME`:** `install.sh --from <manylinux wheel built from this head> --prefix … --bin-dir … --no-profile --no-setup` installs a working `citar`; a folder holding only an sdist is refused with the no-wheel message; the platform check's message for musl, an unknown architecture, glibc 2.27 and a free-threaded Python (through its test hook); `--worker` with `CITAR_RELEASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:<port>` serving the WSL helper build (from the container, this head) and its `.sha256` installs a helper that runs (`--version --home <temp>`), a wrong checksum is refused before anything is written, and an `http` URL to a non-loopback host is refused; `--uninstall` leaves the prefix and bin folder empty.
  2. **Windows, only through `gate_run.py install-ps1`:** `install.ps1 -Prefix … -BinDir … -NoPath -From <win wheel built from this head> -NoSetup` installs a working `citar`; `-Worker` against a loopback server installs the Windows helper, run through `gate_run.py helper`; the ARM64 and free-threaded messages through the hook; the user's PATH value is byte-identical before and after; `-Uninstall` with the same prefix removes only it; the wrapper refuses a run without `-Prefix` and `-BinDir` under the target or without `-NoPath`.
  3. **CI-only, at the merge push:** the `install` matrix on five platforms (PATH changes allowed on runners).
  4. S1 (the tests), S2, S4, S8, S9 (gate 1), S10, S11.

### 5-08: Package managers and assembling a release

- **Depends on:** 5-07
- **Estimated size:** ~500 Python (`release_checksums.py` with `--verify`'s dry-run form, `assemble_release.py`) + the templates + ~250 workflow YAML + ~300 tests
- **Scope:** P5.9 and P5.10: `packaging/` templates (Scoop `citar` and `citar-helper`, winget `game/` and `helper/`, Homebrew `citar-helper.rb` with its service block), `packaging/homebrew/citar.rb` deleted; `scripts/release_checksums.py` rewritten (`--from-dir`, `--base-url`, `--out`, `--write`, `--verify` with `--pypi-json`); `scripts/ci/assemble_release.py` and package.yml's `assemble` job (the list, one build across every artifact with the sdist build's `unknown` label, one result hash, `twine check`, `SHA256SUMS`, the notes, the subject list, the manifests, the three artifacts); the `managers` job (Scoop with `-RunAsAdmin`, the winget schema validation and `winget` where present, Homebrew's audit on the real URLs and install on the loopback copy, on macOS arm64, macOS x64 and Linux x64); `packaging/README.md` rewritten.
- **Gates:**
  1. `assemble_release.py` on a fake artifact tree: the complete set passes and writes `SHA256SUMS` that `sha256sum -c` accepts; a missing asset, an extra one, a helper reporting another build, a wheel reporting another jobs build, a wheel with another result hash, and an sdist build with a label other than `unknown` each fail naming the file; the sdist build's `unknown` is not compared with L.
  2. The generator over the package's local artifacts (stand-ins for the CI-only files) writes every manifest: each URL and hash right; the Scoop JSON has its keys and parses; each winget file starts with its schema header, has the required fields (`Documentations` spelled so) and validates against its schema with `jsonschema`; the formula has a URL and SHA-256 under each of the four platform blocks, and `ruby -c` accepts it in WSL (ruby installed as root, recorded).
  3. `release_checksums.py --verify V --base-url http://127.0.0.1:<port> --pypi-json FILE` over the fake release passes; one changed byte in an asset and a PyPI hash that differs each fail naming the file.
  4. **CI-only, at the merge push:** `assemble` and `managers`: `scoop install` of both packages from loopback, the schema validation, `winget validate` and `install --manifest` where present (else the notice), `brew audit --strict` and `brew style` on the real-URL formula, `brew install` and `brew test` on the loopback copy, on three platforms.
  5. S1 (the tests), S2, S4, S8, S9 (gate 2's ruby), S10, S11.

### 5-09: release.yml end to end, the checklist, the first release dry run

- **Depends on:** 5-08
- **Estimated size:** ~300 workflow YAML + ~250 Python (rule 16, `scripts/ci/release_sign.py`, `scripts/release/managers.sh` with its tests) + `packaging/RELEASING.md` (~300 lines)
- **Scope:** P5.11, P5.12, P5.15: release.yml written (the gate removed; `meta`; `tests` and `package` with no `secrets:` and `contents: read`; the tag-only `sign-helper-manifest` with the tag-expression environment, building nothing, running the prebuilt xtask through `release_sign.py` on the runner's Python, `persist-credentials: false`; `check-signature`; `attest-files`; `publish-pypi`; `publish-image` (`:V-<arch>`, `:V`); `attest-image`; `github-release` as a draft then published; `image-tags`; the dry-run-only `dry-run-summary`; concurrency; per-job permissions); check_workflows rule 16; `packaging/RELEASING.md` (P5.15, with the re-run table) and `scripts/release/managers.sh` (clones into a temporary folder, copies, shows the diff, pushes only with `--push`); CONTRIBUTING's "Releasing".
- **Gates:**
  1. `check_workflows.py` passes; planted, each failing naming its job and rule: a publishing job guarded by `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/')` alone (a dispatch on a tag would publish); `publish-pypi` not needing `attest-files`; `attest-files` after `publish-pypi`; `:latest` pushed in `publish-image`; `attest-image` without `packages: write`; a `docker build` step in release.yml; `github-release` without `draft` first; the signing job with a `cargo` step, a `setup-python` step, or a checkout keeping its credentials; a calling job with `secrets: inherit`.
  2. `release_sign.py` with throwaway keys in a temporary folder and the xtask binary built in the package's target: the four states of P5.4.2 give their four outcomes (a notice, three refusals naming the cause, a signature that verifies against the committed line); it never runs cargo (a test puts a failing `cargo` first on `PATH`).
  3. The release's upload list equals `release.py assets V` (and the `.sig` only on a signed tag); the attestation subjects cover every asset; `managers.sh 0.1.6 --plan` against local throwaway clones shows the diff and pushes nothing.
  4. S2, S4, S8, S10, S11, S12.
  5. **The first release dry run (CI-only), by this package's merge agent after its push** (P5.18.2): environments, tags and releases read; `gh workflow run release.yml --ref v0.1.6 -f version=0.1.6`; `gh run watch --exit-status`; `meta`, `tests`, `package` and `dry-run-summary` green, every tag-only job skipped; the readings unchanged; the `pkg-release-preview` artifact's list equals `release.py assets 0.1.6`; at most three fix rounds; the run id and the jobs' conclusions in the merge's notes. If the run fails at startup on the empty environment name, P5.11's fallback (`release-dry-run.yml`) is made within those rounds.

### 5-10a: The server's release layout: units, backup, wrappers

- **Depends on:** 5-09
- **Estimated size:** ~250 bash (`citar-backup.sh`, `citar-admin.sh`) + ~150 Python (the wizard's unit, the retirement) + units + ~250 tests
- **Scope:** P5.13.1 and P5.13.2: `deploy/citar.service` (the console script, `WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/citar`, `CPUWeight=50` and `IOWeight=50` to start, the `MemoryHigh` rule recorded beside the limits), `citar-backup.sh` (the whole state directory and the release that wrote it), `citar-admin.sh` (`systemd-run` as `citar` with the sandbox, in `$CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` when set), `citar-secrets.sh` in substance unchanged; the wizard's unit rendered from `deploy/citar.service`'s directives (a test holds them equal); `install.sh --server` on the layout, with `--plan`; `requirements.txt`, `scripts/sync_requirements.py` and test.yml's step retired (code references updated; the docs are 5-12's, listed in the as-built note).
- **Gates:**
  1. `shellcheck` on every script in `deploy/` (installed in WSL as root, recorded); `systemd-analyze verify` of the units in WSL.
  2. `citar-backup.sh` in WSL against a temporary state directory: a file planted in each subfolder is in the tarball, the live database files are not, the snapshot passes its integrity check and names the release; a corrupted database makes the backup `UNTRUSTWORTHY`.
  3. The wizard's rendered unit and `deploy/citar.service` hold the same `[Service]` directives; `install.sh --server --plan` names the layout; `citar-admin` prints its `systemd-run` command with the slice when `CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE` is set and without it otherwise.
  4. S1, S1b, S2 (ruff alone from here), S3, S4, S8 (test.yml), S9 (gates 1-2), S10, S11.

### 5-10b: `citar-deploy` and the deploy bundle

- **Depends on:** 5-10a
- **Estimated size:** ~500 bash (`citar-deploy.sh`) + ~200 Python (`scripts/deploy/bundle.py`) + ~300 tests + the expected plans
- **Scope:** P5.13.3 and P5.13.5: `deploy/citar-deploy.sh` (`install`, `cutover`, `rollback`, `uncutover`, `status`, `prune`, `--plan`, `--slice`/`CITAR_DEPLOY_SLICE`; the staged verification with temporary state; the revision recorded, the service stopped and the snapshot taken just before the switch; the units and scripts installed; the automatic rollback that restores the snapshot when the revision moved; `rollback`'s refusal across an unknown revision; a test-only root prefix, `CITAR_DEPLOY_ROOT`, under which the logic runs without root against stubbed `systemctl` and `systemd-run`); `scripts/deploy/bundle.py` (`gh release download`, hashes, `gh attestation verify`, `git archive` of `deploy/` and `env.example`; `--from-dir`).
- **Gates:**
  1. `shellcheck` on `citar-deploy.sh` and `scripts/deploy/`.
  2. `--plan` of `install`, `cutover`, `rollback` (with and without `--restore-backup`), `uncutover` and `prune` against stand-in bundles prints exactly the committed expected texts (`tests/data/deploy/*.txt`, LF-pinned), with and without a slice; a bundle with a changed byte, a wheel of another platform, too little disk and a failing other site in the preflight are each refused before any change, naming why.
  3. **The revision logic, in WSL as `claude`, under `CITAR_DEPLOY_ROOT` with stubs and a real SQLite database:** a failed health check after the revision moved restores the snapshot before the previous release starts, and with the revision unchanged does not; `rollback` across a revision the target does not know is refused without `--restore-backup` and names the deploy's backup; the staged verification's environment holds no live state path.
  4. `bundle.py --from-dir` makes a bundle whose `SHA256SUMS` checks; a tampered wheel is refused; the published path is exercised with a stubbed `gh` (the arguments of `release download` and `attestation verify`).
  5. S1, S2, S4, S8, S9 (gates 1 and 3), S10, S11.

### 5-11: The staging dry run in WSL

- **Depends on:** 5-10b
- **Estimated size:** ~550 bash (`scripts/deploy/staging.sh`) + ~300 Python (`scripts/deploy/staging_check.py`, the probes, the load generator) + RUNBOOK's staging section
- **Scope:** P5.14 with wheels built from this head in WSL: the slice with its probes, `vm.swappiness`, the ballast (`--ballast-mb` from the private log, kept in `ops/p5-notes.md`), the loaded stand-in and the plain one, the 0.1.5 layout, `cutover`, the machine settings at one CPU and 961 MB, the game and the lab under the sandbox, the measurements with the stand-in's response times, the migrating upgrade (`0.1.6-rc.1`), `rollback` across it, the planted migrating failure (`0.1.6-rc.2`), the backup and restore drill, `uncutover`, `cleanup`; the two `-rc` wheels built in the maturin container from bumped scratch clones; the settled `CPUWeight=`/`IOWeight=` and memory figures written into `deploy/citar.service` with the measurement beside them.
- **Gates:**
  1. **The limits bind:** `staging.sh setup` exits 0 with the probes passing (the slice's files read back; the OOM probe killed and counted; the CPU probe within 10% of its quota); **a gate that can fail:** with the slice's `CPUQuota` removed, the CPU probe fails.
  2. `staging.sh run` exits 0, every step's check recorded: the stand-in sites 200 before and after each step; the cutover's health within 60 s; the job host's `ready` reports about 240 MB; the game's 40 rounds and the lab's three games played (the job host under `citar.service`, `RLIMIT_DATA` fallback logged); `oom_kill` 0 in the slice; the stand-in's p95 with a lab game running within twice its idle p95 plus 50 ms; the upgrade, the refused and the accepted rollback, and the second install each healthy with the right version on `/api/meta`; the restore drill reads its accounts; `uncutover` brings 0.1.5 back and it answers.
  3. **Gates that can fail:** the planted `-rc.2` makes `install` restore the snapshot and roll back by itself, exit 1, with `-rc.1` serving at its own revision; a planted release whose server exits before migrating rolls back without a restore.
  4. The measurements (idle, game, lab, pip install, the switch's downtime, seconds a round, the stand-in's response times) in the as-built note with the recommended `MemoryMax`, `MemoryHigh`, host memory figure and weights. If the response-time bound fails even at `CPUWeight=20`, the package stops and reports, and the delegated child cgroup (P5.13.2) becomes a package of its own before 5-12.
  5. `cleanup` leaves S10's WSL items clean and `vm.swappiness` restored.
  6. S2, S4, S5 (RUNBOOK), S10, S11.

### 5-13: Phase 5 exit

- **Depends on:** 5-12
- **Estimated size:** evidence, scripts' runs, small fixes; "As built in 5-13"
- **Scope:** P5.1.4 measured row by row; the dry run on the pushed `v0.1.6` (P5.18.2); the staging run with the dry run's CI-built linux-x64 wheel and constraints (`gh run download`, then `bundle.py --from-dir`) under the VPS's limits; the bump rehearsal; RELEASING.md walked through in its dry-run forms (`release.py bump` in a scratch clone, `release_checksums.py --from-dir` and `--verify --base-url --pypi-json` over the dry run's `pkg-release-assets`, `managers.sh --plan`, `bundle.py --from-dir`, `citar-deploy --plan`, the image checked with `podman run`); WSL's shared target, clones and podman storage removed and measured (P5.18.3); the Phase 6 handoff in `ops/p5-notes.md`.
- **Gates:**
  1. **The release dry run is green on every platform** (CI-only, dispatched by this package on the pushed head; any fix goes through its merge agent, which dispatches again, three rounds at most): `meta`, test.yml's every leg, and package.yml's every job (the five wheels and their smokes on 3.11 and 3.14, the sdist and its build, the bundle and installer with the silent install, the ten helpers with the glibc check and the manifest with its throwaway signature verified, both images built natively and run, the install scripts on five platforms, `assemble` with one build and one result hash, the package managers), every tag-only job skipped, no environment named, the environments, tags and releases unchanged. The run id, each job's conclusion and duration, and the `pkg-release-preview` contents recorded.
  2. **The staging deploy from the CI-built wheel:** `staging.sh` with a bundle made from the dry run's artifacts for the cutover, and `0.1.7-rc.1` and `0.1.7-rc.2` wheels built locally from this head as 5-11 builds its own, for the upgrade, the rollbacks and the planted failure; every check of 5-11's gates 1 to 3 again, the measurements again, compared with 5-11's.
  3. **The bump rehearsal:** in a scratch clone, `release.py bump 0.1.6`; `cargo xtask develop`; the whole S1 suite and S7's nextest, `golden check` and `xtask check` green there; the clone deleted.
  4. **The checklist's dry runs:** each step of RELEASING.md that has one run in that form, its output recorded; the manifests from the dry run's assets equal the `pkg-release-preview` artifact's; `--verify` passes over the dry run's assets; helpers run only through `gate_run.py helper`.
  5. The key scan (S12); WSL's `~/target/citar-p5`, `~/p5` and podman storage removed with `du` and `df` before and after; S10 before and after the whole package; S1, S2, S4, S8, S11 at the head.
